Tilting sheaves for real groups and Koszul duality
Andrei Ionov, Zhiwei Yun

Abstract
. For a certain class of real analytic varieties with Lie group actions we develop a theory of (free-monodromic) tilting sheaves, and apply it to flag varieties stratified by real group orbits. For quasi-split real groups, we construct a fully faithful embedding of the category of tilting sheaves to a real analog of the category of Soergel bimodules, establishing real group analogs of Soergel’s Structure Theorem and Endomorphism Theorem. We apply these results to give a purely geometric proof of the main result of [ 9 ] which proves Soergel’s conjecture [ 24 ] for quasi-split groups.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Koszul duality and tilting sheaves for complex groups

The formalism of Koszul duality in representation theory was introduced in
[
23
]
and further developed in
[
5
]
and subsequent works.

For a complex reductive group
G
, it is proved in
[
5
]
that its category
{\mathcal{O}}
is equivalent to modules over a quadratic Koszul algebra. Moreover, this Koszul algebra is self-dual, or more canonically Koszul dual to its counterpart for the Langlands dual
\check{G}
. The Koszul duality in this case can be stated as an equivalence between the graded version of the derived category
D^{b}({\mathcal{O}})
for
G
and the graded version of
D^{b}(\check{{\mathcal{O}}})
for
\check{G}
. It sends irreducible objects in
\check{{\mathcal{O}}}
to indecomposable projective objects in
{\mathcal{O}}
. It is well-known that the category
{\mathcal{O}}
is equivalent to the category of Harish-Chandra bimodules for
G
, as well as to the category of perverse sheaves on the flag variety of
G
constructible with respect to the Schubert stratification.

Another important duality, namely the Ringel duality, was introduced in
[
21
]
. In the context of the highest weight categories this is an equivalence of derived categories that sends standard objects to costandard objects and projective objects to tilting objects. The composition of Koszul duality and Ringel duality was first studied in
[
4
]
. It sends irreducible objects in
\check{{\mathcal{O}}}
to indecomposable tilting objects in
{\mathcal{O}}
. Compared to the original Koszul duality, the version composed with Ringel duality has the advantage that it preserves the monoidal structure on modified versions of both sides (using tensor product of Harish-Chandra bimodules).

In the geometric setting the theory of tilting perverse sheaves was developed in
[
3
]
. In particular, it is proved that the long intertwining functor provides a Ringel self-duality of the category of the perverse sheaves on the flag variety of
G
. The monoidal version of the Koszul duality (really the composition of Koszul duality with Ringel duality) was proved in
[
10
]
for arbitrary Kac-Moody groups. It sends irreducible perverse sheaves on
\check{B}\backslash\check{G}/\check{B}
to indecomposable
free-monodromic
tilting sheaves on
U\backslash G/U
(where
\check{B}\subset\check{G}
is a Borel subgroup;
B\subset G
is a Borel subgroup with unipotent radical
U
).

In the above versions of Koszul duality, the authors prove the equivalences of categories by showing that both categories are equivalent to graded modules over isomorphic algebras. To construct these algebras, the key ingredients are to construct
Soergel functors
to the appropriate category of Soergel bimodules, and to prove that the Soergel functors are fully faithful on certain classes of objects (irreducible, projective or tilting objects). For example, the Soergel functor maps category
{\mathcal{O}}
to coherent sheaves on the
complex block variety

{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}\times_{{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}W}{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}
, where
{\mathfrak{t}}
is the abstract Cartan algebra of
{\mathfrak{g}}
.

1.2.
Koszul duality for real groups

In
[
24
]
W. Soergel formulated the Koszul duality conjecture for real groups as a categorification of Vogan’s character duality
[
27
]
. Let
G_{\mathbb{R}}
be a real form of a semisimple group
G
and let
{\mathcal{M}}
be a block of its representations with regular integral infinitesimal character. Vogan’s duality associates to
{\mathcal{M}}
a block
\check{{\mathcal{M}}}
for a real form
\check{G}_{\mathbb{R}}
of the Langlands dual group
\check{G}
, which can be geometrically realized as a full subcategory
\check{\mathcal{D}}
in the
\check{K}
-equivariant derived category (with
\mathbb{C}
-coefficients) of the flag variety
\check{X}
of
\check{G}
(where
\check{K}\subset\check{G}
is the fixed points of the Cartan involution defined using
\check{G}_{\mathbb{R}}
).

A breakthrough in this direction appears in
[
9
]
where the authors prove Soergel’s conjecture for quasi-split groups.

Theorem 1.3
.

(
[
9
, Theorem 5.1]
) Let
G_{\mathbb{R}}
be a quasi-split semisimple real group.
Then there is an equivalence between graded versions of triangulated categories
D^{b}({\mathcal{M}})
and
\check{\mathcal{D}}
. It maps indecomposable projective objects in
{\mathcal{M}}
to irreducible perverse sheaves in
\check{\mathcal{D}}
.

Once again the analog of the Soergel functors played an important role in the proof of the above theorem. For the category
{\mathcal{M}}
, the authors of
[
9
]
constructed a Soergel functor from
{\mathcal{M}}
to coherent sheaves on the
real block variety

{\mathfrak{a}}^{*}\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}W^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}\times_{{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}W}{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}
where
{\mathfrak{a}}
is the complexification of the Lie algebra of the maximal split torus of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
, and
W^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{M}}}
is a certain subgroup of the real Weyl group. The construction of the Soergel functor in
[
9
]
is algebraic: it is given by the translation functor to the most singular infinitesimal character.

Theorem
1.3
can be stated geometrically: the category
D^{b}({\mathcal{M}})
can be identified with the
K
-equivariant derived category of sheaves on the enhanced flag variety
\widetilde{X}=G/U
. The goal of this paper is to give a geometric proof of this geometric reformulation. Two new ingredients leading to the proof are the theory of tilting sheaves on spaces stratified by real group orbits, and an analog of Soergel’s Structure Theorem for quasi-split real groups. We give an overview of these ingredients below.

1.4.
Main results: Tilting sheaves for real group orbits

We work with sheaves in
{\mathbf{k}}
-vector spaces, where
{\mathbf{k}}
is an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero
1
1

1

We believe that with a more careful study, the results should hold for mod
p
coefficients when
p
is not too small.
.

The Matsuki correspondence is a bijection between
K
-orbits and
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbits on the flag variety
X
of
G
. In
[
20
]
the authors upgraded this set-theoretic bijection to an equivalence of categories between
K
-equivariant and
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant derived categories of
X
. When
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is a complex group, this equivalence can be identified with the long intertwining endo-functor of the category
\mathcal{O}
. This suggests that the Matsuki equivalence in
[
20
]
might be a Ringel duality.

To justify this expectation, we first need to define tilting sheaves on
X
(or rather free-monodromic tilting sheaves on
\widetilde{X}=G/U

2
2

2

In the main body of the paper we work with the compact manifold
(G/U)/{\mathbf{T}}^{>0}
homotopy equivalent to
G/U
.
) with respect to the
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbits. In this paper we develop such a theory of (free-monodromic) tilting sheaves in the general setting of a real analytic variety
\widetilde{X}
stratified by Lie group orbits satisfying certain conditions. The development here follows that of
[
3
]
and
[
10
]
. One point worth mentioning is that the definition of tilting sheaves requires working with a new
t
-structure on
\widetilde{X}
coming from a specific perversity function (see Section
3.4
). The perversity function takes into account not only the dimension of the stratum but also the size of its equivariant fundamental group.

Applying the general theory to the case of
G(\mathbb{R})
orbits on
\widetilde{X}=G/U
, we show:

Theorem 1.5
.

(Theorem
5.7
)
The Matsuki correspondence in
[
20
]
is a Ringel duality in the sense that it sends indecomposable projective pro-objects in
\operatorname{Perv}_{K}(\widetilde{X})
to indecomposable free-monodromic tilting sheaves in
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}:=\widehat{D}^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}\textup{-mon}}
.

Here,
\widehat{D}^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}\textup{-mon}}
is a completed version of the derived category of
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant complexes on
\widetilde{X}
that are monodromic along the right
{\mathbf{T}}
-action (where
{\mathbf{T}}=B/U
is the abstract Cartan) with unipotent monodromy. More details of the completion procedure in the real analytic setting is developed in Section
2
.

It is worth noting that the common approach to the representation theory of
G({\mathbb{R}})
passes through the Harish-Chandra
({\mathfrak{g}},K)
-modules, which are then studied by algebraic methods. Similarly, in geometry more attention has been put on
K
-equivariant sheaves on
X
rather than
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant sheaves, perhaps to stay within the context of algebraic geometry. On the other hand, the category
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
is directly related to the category of
G(\mathbb{R})
-representations by globalization functors of
[
17
]
(see also
[
25
]
). We wonder whether the
t
-structure we introduced on
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
and tilting sheaves therein have any significance for
G(\mathbb{R})
-representations.

1.6.
Main results: Soergel’s Structure Theorem for real groups

Now we assume that
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split. Let
\mathcal{R}
be the completion of the
{\mathbf{k}}
-group ring of
\pi_{1}(T)
at the augmentation ideal. In this case we define a real Soergel functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}:{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}\to\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R}
as a generic vanishing cycles functor to the closed
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit
3
3

3

In the complex case the Soergel functor was identified with the generic vanishing cycles functor to the closed orbit in
[
7
, Section 11]
, which motivates our definition.
.

We prove a generalization of Soergel’s Struktursatz and Endomorphismensatz of
[
23
]
to quasi-split groups. To state it, we need some notations. Let
T_{\mathbb{R}}
be a maximally split real Cartan subgroup of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
and
W(\mathbb{R})=W(G(\mathbb{R}),T(\mathbb{R}))
be the real Weyl group. Let
\mathcal{S}
be the completion of the
{\mathbf{k}}
-group ring of
\pi_{1}(T(\mathbb{C})/T(\mathbb{R})^{\circ})
. Let
\mathcal{B}_{0}=(\prod_{\chi}\mathcal{S})^{W(\mathbb{R})}
where the product is over characters
\chi:\pi_{0}(T(\mathbb{R}))\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
and
W(\mathbb{R})
permutes the factors under Vogan’s
cross action
. Finally let
{\mathcal{B}}
be the commutative
{\mathbf{k}}
-algebra
\mathcal{B}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}
, where
{\mathbf{W}}
is the abstract Weyl group acting on
{\mathbf{T}}
, hence on
{\mathcal{R}}
. The algebra
\mathcal{B}
is the real analog of
\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}
that appear in the theory of Soergel bimodules, and it can be identified with formal functions on a disjoint of union of real block varieties of
[
9
]
.

Theorem 1.7
.

(Corollary
9.22
and Theorem
11.14
) Assume the quasi-split group
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is nice (see Definition
9.2
, in particular,
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is nice if
G
is adjoint). Let
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
be the full subcategory of
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
consisting of free-monodromic tilting sheaves.

(1)

The real Soergel functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
on free-monodromic tilting sheaves can be lifted to a functor

\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}\colon\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})\to\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}

and is compatible with the Hecke action on
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
and the Soergel bimodule action on
\textup{mod-}\mathcal{B}
(via the classical Soergel functor
\mathbb{V}
for the monodromic Hecke category).

(2)

(Struktursatz) The functor
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}
is fully-faithful.

(3)

(Endomorphismensatz) The functor
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}
is corepresented by a ‘‘big tilting’’ object
\oplus_{a}\mathcal{T}^{a}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
(where the direct sum is over blocks of
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
), and
\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits(\oplus_{a}\mathcal{T}^{a})\cong\mathcal{B}
.

Here is our proof strategy for Theorem
1.7
. Tautologically, we can lift
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\textup{Tilt}}
to be valued in
\textup{mod-}\mathcal{A}
, where
\mathcal{A}
is the ring opposite to the endomorphism ring of the functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\textup{Tilt}}
. By the localization technique developed in Section
7
, which is analogous to the equivariant localization, we show that
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
are isomorphic at the generic points. Moreover, by localizing along codimension one loci of
\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{A}
, we deduce that
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sharp}:\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})\to\textup{mod-}\mathcal{A}
is fully faithful. This relies on a case-by-case check for quasi-split groups of semisimple split rank one. Finally, we prove
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
are canonically isomorphic.

When
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split but not nice, we prove an analogue of the Struktursatz in Theorem
10.8
by reducing the situation a nice group isogenous to
G_{\mathbb{R}}
.

When
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is not quasi-split, the theory of tilting sheaves is still valid, while the Soergel functor should be replaced by an appropriate microlocalization functor. We hope to return to this problem in future work with the goal of proving Koszul duality for real groups in general.

1.8.
Application to Koszul duality for quasi-split groups

Theorem
1.7
then allows us to prove a variant of Theorem
1.3
geometrically. We give a brief statement below and refer to Section
11
for more details.

Theorem 1.9
.

(Theorem
11.8
) Let
G_{\mathbb{R}}
be semisimple and quasi-split. Let
{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
be a block of
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
and
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}\subset D^{b}_{\check{K}}(\check{X})
be the dual (principal) block in the sense of Vogan. There is a triangulated functor

\widetilde{\kappa}:\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}:=K^{b}(\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}))\to{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}

(where
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
stands for the full subcategory of semisimple complexes in
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
), satisfying the following properties:

(1)

\widetilde{\kappa}
is invariant under the internal shift
\{1\}
on
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
.

(2)

For
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}
, natural maps induce a graded
\mathcal{R}
-linear isomorphism

\displaystyle(\oplus_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\{m\})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\widetilde{\kappa}(\mathcal{F}_{1}),\widetilde{\kappa}(\mathcal{F}_{2})).

(3)

The functor
\widetilde{\kappa}
intertwines the actions of the Hecke categories
\check{\mathcal{H}}^{gr}_{\check{G}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
.

(4)

The functor
\widetilde{\kappa}
sends a simple perverse sheaf in
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
and to an indecomposable free-monodromic tilting sheaf in
{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
, and induces a bijection between the sets of isomorphisms classes of such objects.

(5)

The functor
\widetilde{\kappa}
sends the graded lift of the standard objects (resp. the costandard objects) to the free-monodromic standard objects (resp. free-monodromic costandard objects).

The last property is a new feature of the tilting version of Koszul duality compared to the version in Theorem
1.3
. It roughly says that the functor
\widetilde{\kappa}
preserves the relative size of support.

As a consequence of Koszul duality, we deduce that the ranks of stalks of the free-monodromic tilting sheaves
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
are given by the Lusztig-Vogan polynomials of the dual symmetric pair
(\check{G},\check{K})
, see Corollary
11.12
.

1.10.
Organization of the paper

In Section
2
and Section
3
we develop a general theory of free-monodromic tilting sheaves on real analytic varieties stratified by group orbits such that each orbit is equivariantly homotopy equivalent to a torus. It requires an extension of the construction of completed monodromic categories (see
[
10
, Appendix A]
and
[
8
]
) and a new
t
-structure where these tilting sheaves live.

Section
4
to Section
7
are devoted to the study of free-monodromic tilting sheaves on the flag varieties stratified by real group orbits. The discussions in these sections are valid for all real groups. After reviewing some background on real groups in Section
4
, we apply the theory of tilting sheaves developed in Section
3
to the flag variety stratified by real group orbits in Section
5
. We prove that the Matsuki equivalence of
[
20
]
is a Ringel duality (see Theorem
5.7
). In Section
6
we investigate the behavior of tilting sheaves under the Hecke action. In Section
7
we develop the technique of localization for completed monodromic categories, which will be used in the proof of the structure theorem later.

Starting from Section
8
we restrict to quasi-split groups. In Section
8
, we construct the real Soergel functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
for quasi-split groups. In Section
9
we prove our Soergel’s structure theorem for quasi-split groups satisfying a technical condition called
nice
, which includes adjoint quasi-split groups. In Section
10
we extend the structure theorem to all quasi-split groups.

Finally, in Section
11
we prove Theorem
1.9
by constructing a dg-model for the category
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
using the structure theorem we proved. We also derive the numerical result on stalks of free-monodromic tilting sheaves, and prove that the real Soergel functor is copresentated by a ‘‘big tilting’’ object.

1.11.
Acknowledgements

We learned the idea of considering
G_{\mathbb{R}}
-equivariant tilting sheaves on the flag variety from Roman Bezrukavnikov. We thank Kari Vilonen and David Vogan for teaching us about real groups and answering our questions. We thank Simon Riche for pointing out a mistake.

A.I. was funded by RFBR, project number 19-31-90078. Z.Y. is supported partially by the Simon Foundation and the Packard Foundation.

2.
Completed monodromic category

Completed monodromic categories were defined in
[
10
, Appendix A]
in order to make sense of free-monodromic local systems and tilting sheaves on enhanced flag varieties
G/U
. In
[
8
]
, this construction has been adapted to the topological context allowing an arbitrary coefficient field. The rough idea in both cases is to take certain pro-objects in
D^{b}(\widetilde{X},{\mathbf{k}})
that include local systems with unipotent monodromy with infinite Jordan block along the fibers of
\pi
. In these works, each stratum has the homotopy type of a torus of the same dimension.

For the purpose of this paper we need to extend the construction of completed monodromic categories to the case where different strata are (equivariantly) homotopy equivalent to tori of different dimensions. The ‘‘size’’ of the free-monodromy will then vary on different strata. We will show that the completion construction still gives a well-behaved category of sheaves.

Throughout the paper, let
{\mathbf{k}}
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
. All sheaves will be assumed to be the sheaves of
{\mathbf{k}}
-vector spaces. All (co)homology groups are taken with
{\mathbf{k}}
coefficients unless otherwise stated.

2.1.
The setup

Let
X
be a real analytic variety (for example the real points of a scheme of finite type over
\mathbb{R}
). Let
T^{c}
be a
compact
torus. Let
\pi:\widetilde{X}\to X
be a principal right
T^{c}
-bundle.

Let
H
be a Lie group with an analytic action on
\widetilde{X}
from the left commuting with
T^{c}
. Then there is an induced
H
-action on
X
such that
\pi
is
H
-equivariant.

Remark 2.2
.

In application, we consider
X=G/B
to be the flag manifold of a complex reductive group
G
. Let
Y=G/U
(where
U
is the unipotent radical of
B
), then
Y\to X
is a
T
-torsor, where
T=B/U
is the abstract Cartan of
G
. The canonical decomposition
\mathbb{C}^{\times}=\mathbb{R}^{>0}\times S^{1}
gives a canonical decomposition
T=T^{>0}\times T^{c}
where
T^{>0}=\mathbb{R}^{>0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
and
T^{c}=S^{1}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, a compact torus. We then let
\widetilde{X}=Y/T^{>0}
. Since
T^{>0}
is contractible, the pullback functor
D^{b}(\widetilde{X})\to D^{b}(Y)
is fully faithful, so if we are interested only in sheaves on
Y
monodromic under the right
T
-action, we may equivalently consider sheaves on
\widetilde{X}
monodromic under the right
T
-action.

2.3.
Completion

Consider the adjoint functors

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.73752pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.73752pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{D^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.02087pt\raise 7.19026pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.9764pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{!}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.73752pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.73752pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{D^{b}_{H}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.02087pt\raise-8.39778pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.24501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi^{!}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.73753pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Let
D^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}\subset D^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})
be the full subcategory generated by the image of
\pi^{!}
.

Let
\mathcal{R}
be the completion of the group algebra
{\mathbf{k}}[\pi_{1}(T^{c})]
at the augmentation ideal. Equip
\mathcal{R}
with the adic topology coming from the augmentation ideal. Then
\mathcal{R}
is a complete regular local ring with residue field
{\mathbf{k}}
and its cotangent space canonically isomorphic to
H_{1}(T^{c},{\mathbf{k}})=\pi_{1}(T^{c})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbf{k}}
. The monodromy action along the fibers of
\pi
gives an
\mathcal{R}
-linear structure on
D^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
, namely
\mathcal{R}
acts on the identity functor of
D^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
.

Following
[
10
, A.3]
, let
\widehat{D}^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
be the category of pro-objects in
D^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
indexed by positive integers. Let
\widetilde{D}^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}\subset\textup{pro}D^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
be the full subcategory consisting of pro-objects
(\mathcal{F}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
that satisfy two conditions:

(1)

(
\pi
-constancy) The pro-object
(\pi_{!}\mathcal{F}_{n})_{n}\in\textup{pro}D^{b}_{H}(X)
lies in the essential image of the natural functor
D^{b}_{H}(X)\to\textup{pro}D^{b}_{H}(X)
consisting of constant pro-objects.

(2)

(uniform boundedness)
(\mathcal{F}_{n})_{n}
is isomorphic to a pro-object
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{n})_{n}
in
\textup{pro}D^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
where each
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{n}
has perverse degrees in
[-N,N]
for some
N>0
independent of
n
.

It is proved in
[
10
, Theorem A.3.2]
that
\widehat{D}^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
is an
\mathcal{R}
-linear triangulated category. By the
\pi
-constancy of objects in
\widehat{D}^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
, we have an adjunction induced from the adjunction
(\pi_{!},\pi^{!})

\textstyle{\widehat{D}^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\pi}_{!}}
\textstyle{D^{b}_{H}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\pi}^{!}}

It will be convenient to introduce adjunctions
(\pi_{\dagger},\pi^{\dagger})
between the same categories:

(2.1)

\pi_{\dagger}:=\widehat{\pi}_{!}[\dim T^{c}],\quad\pi^{\dagger}:=\widehat{\pi}_{!}[-\dim T^{c}]\cong\widehat{\pi}^{*}.

Moreover, given an
H
-equivariant map
f:X\to Y
that lifts to an
H
-equivariant map
\widetilde{f}:\widetilde{X}\to\widetilde{Y}
of
T^{c}
-torsors over
X
and
Y
, under the assumption that
H
has finitely many orbits on
X
and
Y
, the functors
\widetilde{f}^{*},\widetilde{f}_{*},\widetilde{f}_{!},\widetilde{f}^{!}
and their adjunctions induce functors
\widehat{f}^{*},\widehat{f}_{*},\widehat{f}_{!},\widehat{f}^{!}
between the completed categories
\widehat{D}_{H}(\widetilde{X})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
and
\widehat{D}_{H}(\widetilde{Y})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
.

2.4.
Situation over a point

Consider the special case where
X=\textup{pt}
,
\widetilde{X}=T^{c}
, and
H=T^{c}_{1}
is a closed subgroup of
T^{c}
acting on
\widetilde{X}
by left translation. We shall give an algebraic description of the completed category
\widehat{D}^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
.

Let
(T^{c}_{1})^{\circ}
be the neutral component of
T^{c}_{1}
, and let
\overline{T}^{c}=T^{c}/(T^{c}_{1})^{\circ}
, the quotient torus. Then
\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{1})=T^{c}_{1}/(T^{c}_{1})^{\circ}
is a finite subgroup of
\overline{T}^{c}
.

For each character
\chi:\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{1})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
, let
{\mathbf{k}}_{\chi}
be the one-dimensional
{\mathbf{k}}
-vector space with an
T^{c}_{1}
-equivariant structure via
\chi
. Let
D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(\textup{pt})_{\chi}
be the full subcategory of
D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(\textup{pt})
consisting of objects
\mathcal{F}
such that the action of
T^{c}_{1}
on
H^{i}\mathcal{F}
(an
{\mathbf{k}}
-vector space) is via
\chi
(pulled back to
T^{c}_{1}
). Then we have a decomposition

D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(\textup{pt})=\bigoplus_{\chi:\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{1})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}}D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(\textup{pt})_{\chi}.

Tensoring with the rank
1
local system corresponding to
\chi
gives an equivalence
D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(\textup{pt})_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(\textup{pt})_{\chi}
.

Similarly, let
D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon},\chi}
be the full triangulated subcategory generated by the image of
D_{T^{c}_{1}}(\textup{pt})_{\chi}
under the pullback
\pi^{!}:D_{T^{c}_{1}}(\textup{pt})\to D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
. Again we have a decomposition

D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}\cong\bigoplus_{\chi:\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{1})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}}D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon},\chi}.

Let
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\chi}
be the rank one
T^{c}_{1}
-equivariant local system on
T^{c}
with monodromy action given by
\chi
(whose underlying local system is trivial).

Lemma 2.5
.

(1)

The forgetful functor
D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}\to D^{b}_{(T^{c})_{1}^{\circ}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
induces an equivalence

D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon},\chi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}D^{b}_{(T^{c})_{1}^{\circ}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}.

(2)

Let
\sigma:T^{c}\to\overline{T}^{c}
be the projection. Then
\sigma^{*}
induces an equivalence of categories

\sigma^{*}:D^{b}_{(T^{c}_{1})^{\circ}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}\cong D^{b}(\overline{T}^{c})_{\overline{T}^{c}\textup{-mon}}.

(3)

Let
\overline{\mathcal{R}}
be the completion of
{\mathbf{k}}[\pi_{1}(\overline{T}^{c})]
at the augmentation ideal. We have an equivalence

D^{b}(\overline{\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod}_{\textup{nil}})\cong D^{b}(\overline{T}^{c})_{\overline{T}^{c}\textup{-mon}}.

Here
\overline{\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod}_{\textup{nil}}
is the category of
\overline{\mathcal{R}}
-modules of finite dimension over
{\mathbf{k}}
. It sends
M\in\overline{\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod}_{\textup{nil}}
to the local system
\mathcal{L}_{M}
on
\overline{T}^{c}
whose stalk at
1\in\overline{T}^{c}
is
M
and the monodromy representation of
\pi_{1}(\overline{T}^{c})
is given by the
\overline{\mathcal{R}}
-module structure on
M
.

(4)

Combining (1)(2)(3) we get an equivalence

(2.2)

D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}\cong\bigoplus_{\chi:\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{1})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}}D^{b}(\overline{\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod}_{\textup{nil}}).

For a collection of finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathcal{R}}
-modules
(M_{\chi})_{\chi}
indexed by characters
\chi:\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{1})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
, the equivalence (
2.2
) sends
\oplus M_{\chi}
on the right side to
\oplus(\sigma^{*}\mathcal{L}_{M_{\chi}}\otimes{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\chi})\in D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
.

Proof.

For (3) see
[
10
, Corollary A.4.7(1)]
or
[
8
, Lemma 4.1]
. The rest of the lemma is clear.
∎

We also have a description of
D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(\textup{pt})
following
[
13
]
as modules over the homology algebra of the torus
(T^{c}_{1})^{\circ}
. Let

\Lambda_{\bullet}=H_{*}((T^{c}_{1})^{\circ},{\mathbf{k}})

as a graded algebra in degrees
0,-1,\cdots,-\dim T^{c}_{1}
.

Lemma 2.6
.

(1)

The forgetful functor
D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(\textup{pt})\to D^{b}_{(T^{c})_{1}^{\circ}}(\textup{pt})
restricts to an equivalence for each
\chi
:

D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(\textup{pt})_{\chi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}D^{b}_{(T^{c}_{1})^{\circ}}(\textup{pt}).

(2)

Taking the stalk induces an equivalence

\textup{R}\Gamma(\textup{pt},-):D^{b}_{(T^{c}_{1})^{\circ}}(\textup{pt})\cong D^{f}_{+}(\Lambda_{\bullet}\textup{-mod}).

Here
D^{f}_{+}(\Lambda_{\bullet}\textup{-mod})
denotes the full subcategory of the bounded below derived category of dg
\Lambda_{\bullet}
-modules with cohomology finitely generated over
\Lambda_{\bullet}
.

(3)

Combining (1)(2), there is an equivalence

(2.3)

D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(\textup{pt})\cong\bigoplus_{\chi:\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{1})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}}D^{b}(\Lambda_{\bullet}\textup{-mod}).

Proof.

(2) follows from
[
13
, Theorem 11.2]
. The rest of the statements are clear.
∎

Lemma 2.7
.

(1)

The equivalence (
2.2
) extends to a canonical equivalence

(2.4)

\widehat{D}^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}\cong\bigoplus_{\chi:\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{1})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}}D^{f}(\overline{\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod})

where
D^{f}(\overline{\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod})
is the bounded derived category of finitely generated
\overline{\mathcal{R}}
-modules.

(2)

Under the equivalences (
2.4
) and (
2.3
), the adjunction
(\pi_{\dagger},\pi^{\dagger})
(see (
2.1
)) can be identified with the composition of adjunctions

\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\chi}D^{f}(\overline{\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathbf{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}}}{{\otimes}}_{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}(-)}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\chi}D^{f}({\mathbf{k}}\textup{-mod})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\bullet}\otimes_{{\mathbf{k}}}(-)}
forg
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\chi}D^{f}_{+}(\Lambda_{\bullet}\textup{-mod})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
forg

Here both right adjoints are the forgetful functors for the ring homomorphisms
\overline{\mathcal{R}}\to{\mathbf{k}}
(augmentation) and
{\mathbf{k}}\to\Lambda_{\bullet}
.

Proof.

(1) Let
\sigma:T^{c}\to\overline{T}^{c}
be the projection. Combining the equivalences in Lemma
2.5
(1)(2) we have an equivalence

\Phi:\bigoplus_{\chi}D^{b}(\overline{T}^{c})_{\overline{T}^{c}\textup{-mon}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}

given by sending
(\mathcal{F}_{\chi})_{\chi}
to
\oplus\sigma^{*}\mathcal{F}_{\chi}\otimes{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\chi}
. Passing to pro-objects we get an equivalence
\textup{pro}(\Phi)
. We claim that
\textup{pro}(\Phi)
restricts to an equivalence of full subcategories

\widehat{\Phi}:\bigoplus_{\chi}\widehat{D}^{b}(\overline{T}^{c})_{\overline{T}^{c}\textup{-mon}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\widehat{D}^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}.

Note here the completions on the two sides are with respect to different torus actions. Let
\mathcal{F}_{\chi}=(\mathcal{F}_{\chi,n})_{n\geq 0}\in\textup{pro}D^{b}(\overline{T}^{c})_{\overline{T}^{c}\textup{-mon}}
. We need to show that each
\mathcal{F}_{\chi}
satisfies the two conditions defining
\widehat{D}^{b}(\overline{T}^{c})_{\overline{T}^{c}\textup{-mon}}
if and only if the pro-object
\oplus_{\chi}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\chi}\otimes\sigma^{*}\mathcal{F}_{\chi,n}
satisfies the two conditions defining
\widehat{D}^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
. This easily reduces to check the same statement for
\chi=1
: i.e.,
(\mathcal{F}_{n})_{n}
satisfies
\overline{\pi}
-constancy (where
\overline{\pi}:\overline{T}^{c}\to\textup{pt}
) and if and only if
(\sigma^{*}\mathcal{F}_{n})_{n}
satisfies
\pi
-constancy and uniform boundedness. Since
\sigma^{*}
is
t
-exact up to a shift, the equivalence of uniform boundedness is clear. Now

(2.5)

\pi_{!}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{F}_{n}=\overline{\pi}_{!}\sigma_{!}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{F}_{n}\cong(\overline{\pi}_{!}\mathcal{F}_{n})\otimes H^{*}_{c}((T^{c}_{1})^{\circ},{\mathbf{k}}).

Therefore,
(\pi_{!}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{F}_{n})_{n}
is isomorphic to a constant object in
\textup{pro}D^{b}_{T^{c}_{1}}(\textup{pt})
if and only if
(\overline{\pi}_{!}\mathcal{F}_{n})_{n}
is isomorphic to a constant object in
\textup{pro}D^{b}(\textup{pt})
.

By
[
10
, Corollary A.4.7(2)]
or
[
8
, Corollary 4.6]
, we have
D^{b}(\overline{T}^{c})_{\overline{T}^{c}\textup{-mon}}\cong D^{f}(\overline{\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod})
. Combining with
\widehat{\Phi}
it gives the equivalence (
2.4
).

(2) By tensoring with
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\chi}
the case for general
\chi
reduced to the case of trivial character
\chi
. In this case we need to describe the functor

\pi_{\dagger}=\widehat{\pi}_{!}[\dim T^{c}]:\widehat{D}^{b}_{(T^{c}_{1})^{\circ}}(T^{c})\to\widehat{D}^{b}_{(T^{c}_{1})^{\circ}}(\textup{pt}).

By Lemma
2.5
, for
M\in D^{f}(\overline{\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod})
, the corresponding object in
\widehat{D}^{b}_{(T^{c}_{1})^{\circ}}(T^{c})
is
\sigma^{*}\mathcal{L}_{M}
. Then (
2.5
) implies

\displaystyle\pi_{\dagger}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{L}_{M}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}_{!}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{L}_{M}[\dim T^{c}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\widehat{\overline{\pi}}_{!}\mathcal{L}_{M})[\dim\overline{T}^{c}]\otimes H^{*}_{c}((T^{c}_{1})^{\circ},{\mathbf{k}})[\dim T_{1}^{c}]

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}_{\dagger}\mathcal{L}_{M}\otimes\Lambda_{\bullet}.

Here
\overline{\pi}_{\dagger}\mathcal{L}_{M}\in D^{b}(\textup{pt})\cong D^{f}({\mathbf{k}}\textup{-mod})
. It is well-known that
\overline{\pi}_{\dagger}\mathcal{L}_{M}\cong{\mathbf{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}}}{{\otimes}}_{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}M
. Therefore

\pi_{\dagger}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{L}_{M}\cong({\mathbf{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}}}{{\otimes}}_{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}M)\otimes\Lambda_{\bullet}.

∎

3.
Real tilting exercises

In this section we develop the theory of (free-monodromic) tilting sheaves on spaces stratified by group orbits in the style of
[
3
]
. A key assumption that ensures the existence of tilting sheaves is a certain cohomological bound for the links between strata.

3.1.
The setting

We are back to the setup of Section
2.1
. Namely, let
X
be a real analytic variety. Let
T^{c}
be a
compact
torus. Let
\pi:\widetilde{X}\to X
be a principal right
T^{c}
-bundle. Let
H
be a Lie group with an analytic action on
\widetilde{X}
from the left commuting with
T^{c}
. We assume additionally that the action of
H
on
X
has finitely many orbits
\{X_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in I}
, which then gives a Whitney stratification of
X
. We put
\widetilde{X}_{\lambda}=\pi^{-1}(X_{\lambda})
. Let
i_{\lambda}\colon X_{\lambda}\to X
and
\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}\colon\widetilde{X}_{\lambda}\to\widetilde{X}
be the inclusions. We say
\lambda\leq\mu
if
X_{\lambda}\subset\overline{X}_{\mu}
. Put
d_{\lambda}:=\dim X_{\lambda}
.

Let
X_{\lambda}\subset X
be an
H
-orbit, and
\widetilde{X}_{\lambda}=\pi^{-1}(X_{\lambda})
. We choose a point
x_{\lambda}\in X_{\lambda}
. Let
H_{x_{\lambda}}
be its stabilizer in
H
. Then
H
acts on the fiber
\pi^{-1}(x_{\lambda})
commuting with the right
T^{c}
-action. This defines a homomorphism
\varphi_{x_{\lambda}}:H_{x_{\lambda}}\to T^{c}
such that the action of
h\in H
on
\pi^{-1}(x_{\lambda})
is by right translation by
\varphi_{x_{\lambda}}(h)^{-1}
. Let
T^{c}_{x_{\lambda}}\subset T^{c}
be the image of
\varphi_{x_{\lambda}}
. Changing the choice of
x_{\lambda}
changes
\varphi_{x_{\lambda}}
by
H
-conjugation. Since
T^{c}
is abelian,
T^{c}_{x_{\lambda}}
stays the same. Therefore
T^{c}_{x_{\lambda}}
is independent of the choice of
x_{\lambda}
, and we denote it by
T^{c}_{\lambda}
.

For
\lambda\in I
, let
\textup{LS}_{\lambda}
denote the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible
H
-equivariant local systems on
X_{\lambda}
with
{\mathbf{k}}
-coefficients. Then we have a canonical bijection

(3.1)

\textup{LS}_{\lambda}\cong\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{\lambda}),{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}).

Denote

\widetilde{I}=\{(\lambda,\chi)|\lambda\in I,\chi\colon\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{\lambda})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}\mbox{ is a character}\}.

For
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
, let
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda}
be the corresponding rank one local system on
X_{\lambda}
.

3.2.
Assumptions

We assume:

(3.2)

The subgroup
T^{c}_{\lambda}\subset T^{c}
is closed, and
\ker(H_{x_{\lambda}}\to T^{c}_{\lambda})
is contractible.

In particular, the identity component
(T^{c}_{\lambda})^{\circ}
of
T^{c}_{\lambda}
is a compact torus. For
\lambda\in I
, we put

\overline{T}^{c}_{\lambda}=T^{c}_{\lambda}/(T^{c}_{\lambda})^{\circ},\quad d_{\lambda}=\dim X_{\lambda},\quad n_{\lambda}=\dim T^{c}-\dim T^{c}_{\lambda}=\dim\overline{T}^{c}_{\lambda}.

We impose the following parity condition:

(3.3)

The parity of the numbers
d_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda}
is the same for all
\lambda\in I
.

For
\lambda<\mu\in I
and
x_{\lambda}\in X_{\lambda}
, let
L^{\mu}_{x_{\lambda}}
be the link of
X_{\lambda}
in
X_{\mu}
at
x_{\lambda}
. More precisely, let
D_{x_{\lambda}}
be a sufficiently small transversal slice to
X_{\lambda}
at
x_{\lambda}
, and take
L_{x_{\lambda}}^{\mu}=D_{x_{\lambda}}\cap X_{\mu}
. Then
L^{\mu}_{x_{\lambda}}
is a smooth manifold of dimension
d_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}
, well-defined up to diffeomorphism.

We assume that for any
(\mu,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
and any
\lambda<\mu
, we have

(3.4)

H^{i}(L_{x_{\lambda}}^{\mu},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\chi})=0\mbox{ for }i>\frac{1}{2}(d_{\mu}+n_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}-n_{\lambda}).

Here we use
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\chi}
to denote the restriction of
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\chi}
to
L^{\mu}_{x_{\lambda}}
.

Since
L_{x_{\lambda}}^{\mu}
is a smooth manifold of dimension
d_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}
, by Poincaré duality (
3.4
) is equivalent to the following bound for all
\chi:\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{\mu})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}

(3.5)

H^{i}_{c}(L_{x_{\lambda}}^{\mu},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\chi})=0\mbox{ for }i<\frac{1}{2}(d_{\mu}-n_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda}).

Remark 3.3
.

If
n_{\lambda}=n_{\mu}
, a typical situation where the bounds (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) hold is when
L^{\mu}_{x_{\lambda}}
is diffeomorphic to a Stein manifold (e.g. smooth affine complex algebraic variety) of complex dimension
\frac{1}{2}(d_{\mu}-d_{\lambda})
. If
n_{\mu}>n_{\lambda}
, then there is a possible overlap of length
n_{\mu}-n_{\lambda}
for the nonvanishing degrees of
H^{*}_{c}(L_{x_{\lambda}}^{\mu},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\chi})
and
H^{*}(L_{x_{\lambda}}^{\mu},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\chi})
, which can happen if
L^{\mu}_{x_{\lambda}}
fibers over a Stein manifold of complex dimension
\frac{1}{2}(d_{\mu}-n_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda})
with fibers compact manifolds of real dimension
n_{\mu}-n_{\lambda}
(e.g. compact torus fibration). On the other hand, if
n_{\mu}<n_{\lambda}
, then there is a gap of length at least
n_{\lambda}-n_{\mu}
between the lowest nonvanishing degree of
H^{*}_{c}(L_{x_{\lambda}}^{\mu},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\chi})
and the highest nonvanishing degree of
H^{*}(L_{x_{\lambda}}^{\mu},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\chi})
, which can happen if
L^{\mu}_{x_{\lambda}}
admits a fiber bundle whose total space is diffeomorphic to a Stein manifold of complex dimension
\frac{1}{2}(d_{\mu}-n_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda})
and whose fibers are compact manifolds of real dimension
n_{\lambda}-n_{\mu}
. In our applications, the cohomological bounds hold essentially for these reasons.

3.4.
A new
t
-structure

We define a perversity function
p:I\to\mathbb{Z}
by

(3.6)

p_{\lambda}=\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(d_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda})\rfloor.

As in
[
2
, Section 2.1]
, it defines a
t
-structure on
D^{b}_{H}(X)
, whose heart we denote by
{}^{p}P_{H}(X)
.

For
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
, let
\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}
and
\nabla_{\lambda,\chi}\in D_{H}^{b}(X)
be the
!
- and
*
-extensions of the local system
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}[p_{\lambda}]
on
X_{\lambda}
.

Lemma 3.5
.

For
(\mu,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
and
\lambda\leq\mu
,
i^{!}_{\lambda}\Delta_{\mu,\chi}
lies in degrees
\geq-p_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda}-n_{\mu}
, and
i^{*}_{\lambda}\nabla_{\mu,\chi}
lies in degrees
\leq-p_{\lambda}
. In particular
\nabla_{\mu,\chi}
lies in the heart of the
t
-structure
{}^{p}P_{H}(X)
.

Proof.

We first show the statement about
\nabla_{\mu,\chi}
. The stalk of
i^{*}_{\lambda}\nabla_{\mu,\chi}
at
x_{\lambda}\in X_{\lambda}
is
H^{*}(L^{\mu}_{x_{\lambda}},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\chi})[p_{\mu}]
. By the cohomological bound (
3.4
), it is concentrated in degrees
\leq-p_{\mu}+p_{\mu}-p_{\lambda}=-p_{\lambda}
. Since
\nabla_{\mu,\chi}
has vanishing costalks, it lies in the heart of the
t
-structure
{}^{p}P_{H}(X)
.

For
i^{!}_{\lambda}\Delta_{\mu,\chi}
, we note that
\Delta_{\mu,\chi}
is Verdier dual to
\nabla_{\mu,\chi^{\prime}}[d_{\mu}-2p_{\mu}]
for some
\chi^{\prime}
. Therefore
i^{!}_{\lambda}\Delta_{\mu,\chi}
is Verdier dual to
i^{*}_{\lambda}\nabla_{\mu,\chi^{\prime}}[d_{\mu}-2p_{\mu}]
. Since
i^{*}_{\lambda}\nabla_{\mu,\chi^{\prime}}[d_{\mu}-2p_{\mu}]
lies in degrees
\leq-p_{\lambda}-d_{\mu}+2p_{\mu}
,
i^{!}_{\lambda}\Delta_{\mu,\chi}
lies in degrees
\geq-d_{\lambda}+p_{\lambda}+d_{\mu}-2p_{\mu}=-p_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda}-n_{\mu}
.

∎

3.6.
Free-monodromic local systems on an orbit

We apply results from Section
2.4
to the situation of a single orbit
H\backslash X_{\lambda}
. We have adjoint functors

\textstyle{\widehat{D}^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X}_{\lambda})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\lambda\dagger}}
\textstyle{D^{b}_{H}(X_{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\dagger}_{\lambda}}

Let
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}
be the completion of the group algebra
{\mathbf{k}}[\pi_{1}(\overline{T}^{c}_{\lambda})]
at the augmentation ideal. Let
\Lambda_{\lambda,\bullet}=H_{*}((T_{\lambda}^{c})^{\circ},{\mathbf{k}})
be the homology of the torus
(T_{\lambda}^{c})^{\circ}
, viewed as a graded algebra in degrees
0,-1,\cdots,-\dim T_{\lambda}^{c}
.

Corollary 3.7
.

For
\lambda\in I
, we have canonical equivalences

(3.7)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{\lambda}:\widehat{D}^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X}_{\lambda})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}\cong\bigoplus_{\chi:\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{\lambda})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}}D^{f}(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}\textup{-mod}),

(3.8)

\displaystyle\Phi_{\lambda}:D^{b}_{H}(X_{\lambda})\cong\bigoplus_{\chi:\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{\lambda})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}}D^{f}_{+}(\Lambda_{\lambda,\bullet}\textup{-mod}).

Under these equivalences, the adjunction
(\pi_{\lambda\dagger},\pi_{\lambda}^{\dagger})
takes the form

\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\chi}D^{f}(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}\textup{-mod})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathbf{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}}}{{\otimes}}_{\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}}(-)}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\chi}D^{f}({\mathbf{k}}\textup{-mod})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\lambda,\bullet}\otimes_{{\mathbf{k}}}(-)}
forg
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\chi}D^{f}_{+}(\Lambda_{\lambda,\bullet}\textup{-mod})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
forg

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{x}_{\lambda}\in\widetilde{X}_{\lambda}
with image
x_{\lambda}\in X_{\lambda}
. Then
\pi^{-1}(x_{\lambda})\cong T^{c}
given by the base point
\widetilde{x}_{\lambda}
. Restricting to
\pi^{-1}(x_{\lambda})
gives an equivalence
i^{*}:D^{b}_{H}(X_{\lambda})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}D^{b}_{H_{x_{\lambda}}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
, which extends to the completions
\widehat{i}^{*}:\widehat{D}^{b}_{H}(X_{\alpha})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\widehat{D}^{b}_{H_{x_{\lambda}}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
. Since
\ker(\varphi_{x_{\lambda}})
is contractible, we have
D^{b}_{H_{x_{\lambda}}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}\cong D^{b}_{T^{c}_{\lambda}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
which also extends to completions. Similarly,
D^{b}_{H}(X_{\lambda})\cong D^{b}_{T^{c}_{\lambda}}(T^{c})
. It remains to apply Lemma
2.7
. It is easy to check that the equivalence thus defined is independent of the choice of
\widetilde{x}_{\lambda}
.
∎

In the situation of Corollary
3.7
, for each
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
, we have a
free-monodromic
local system
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}\in\widehat{D}^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X}_{\lambda})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
that corresponds to the free rank one
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}
-module
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}
placed in the
\chi
-summand on the right side of (
3.7
).

3.8.
Free-monodromic sheaves

For the triangulated category of free-monodromic sheaves we put
{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}:=\widehat{D}^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
for short, whenever it does not cause ambiguity. Define a
t
-structure on
{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}
using the same perversity function
p
as in (
3.6
), whose heart we denote by
{\mathcal{P}}_{H,X}
.

For
\lambda\in I
let
{\mathcal{M}}_{\lambda}:=\widehat{D}_{H}(\widetilde{X}_{\lambda})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
. We have adjunctions

\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}_{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{i}_{\lambda!}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{!}}

\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}_{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{i}_{\lambda*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{*}}

For
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
, we have the free-monodromic local system
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\lambda}
as defined in Section
3.6
. We define standard and costandard objects
{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}
and
{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}
of
{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}
as, respectively, the
!
- and
*
-extensions under
\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}
of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda,\chi}[p_{\lambda}]
.

Lemma 3.9
.

For any
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
, we have

\pi_{\dagger}\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\cong\Lambda_{\lambda,\bullet}\otimes\Delta_{\lambda,\chi},\quad\pi_{\dagger}\widetilde{\nabla}_{\lambda,\chi}\cong\Lambda_{\lambda,\bullet}\otimes\nabla_{\lambda,\chi}.

Proof.

By Corollary
3.7
we have

\pi_{\lambda\dagger}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}\cong\Lambda_{\lambda,\bullet}\otimes{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}.

Shifting by
p_{\lambda}
and applying
i_{\lambda!}
to the above we get

\pi_{\dagger}\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}=i_{\lambda!}\pi_{\lambda\dagger}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}[p_{\lambda}]\cong i_{\lambda!}(\Lambda_{\lambda,\bullet}\otimes{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}[p_{\lambda}])=\Lambda_{\lambda,\bullet}\otimes\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}.

The argument for the costandard sheaf is the same, using that
\pi_{\dagger}\widetilde{i}_{\lambda*}\cong i_{\lambda*}\pi_{\lambda\dagger}
because
\widehat{\pi}_{!}=\widehat{\pi}_{*}
(since
\pi
is proper).
∎

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
(\mu,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
and
\lambda<\mu
.

(1)

The restriction
\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\lambda}{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu,\chi}
lies in degrees
\leq-p_{\lambda}
.

(2)

Under the equivalence
\Phi_{\lambda}
, the corestriction
\widetilde{i}^{!}_{\lambda}{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\chi}
corresponds to a collection
M_{\chi^{\prime}}\in D^{f}(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}\textup{-mod})
(where
\chi^{\prime}:\pi_{0}(T_{\lambda}^{c})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
) where each
M_{\chi^{\prime}}
can be represented by a complex of free
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}
in degrees
\geq-p_{\lambda}
.

(3)

The standard and costandard objects
{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\chi}
and
{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu,\chi}
lie in the heart of the
t
-structure
{\mathcal{P}}_{H,X}
.

Proof.

(1) By Lemma
3.9
,
\pi_{\lambda\dagger}\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\lambda}{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu,\chi}\cong i^{*}_{\lambda}\pi_{\dagger}{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu,\chi}\cong\Lambda_{\mu,\bullet}\otimes i^{*}_{\lambda}\nabla_{\mu,\chi}
. By Lemma
3.5
,
i^{*}_{\lambda}\nabla_{\mu,\chi}
lies in degrees
\leq-p_{\lambda}
, hence
\pi_{\lambda\dagger}\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\lambda}{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu}
lies in degrees
\leq-p_{\lambda}
(note
\Lambda_{\mu,\bullet}
is in non-positive degrees). From the description of
\pi_{\lambda\dagger}
given in Corollary
3.7
we see that
\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\lambda}{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu}
is in degrees
\leq-p_{\lambda}
.

(2) Note the statement is stronger than saying that
M_{\chi^{\prime}}
lies in cohomological degrees
\geq-p_{\lambda}
, but saying that it admits a free resolution (as
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}
-modules) in degrees
\geq-p_{\lambda}
.

By Lemma
3.9
, we have
\pi_{\lambda\dagger}\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{!}\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu,\chi}\cong i_{\lambda}^{!}\pi_{\dagger}\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu,\chi}\cong\Lambda_{\mu,\bullet}\otimes i_{\lambda}^{!}\Delta_{\mu,\chi}
. By Lemma
3.5
,
i_{\lambda}^{!}\Delta_{\mu,\chi}
lies in degrees
\geq-p_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda}-n_{\mu}
. Therefore
\pi_{\lambda\dagger}\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{!}\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu,\chi}\cong\Lambda_{\mu,\bullet}\otimes i_{\lambda}^{!}\Delta_{\mu,\chi}
lies in degrees
\geq-p_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda}-n_{\mu}-\dim T_{\mu}^{c}=-p_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda}-\dim T^{c}=-p_{\lambda}-\dim T_{\lambda}^{c}
. By Corollary
3.7
,
\pi_{\lambda\dagger}\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{!}\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu,\chi}
corresponds to
\oplus_{\chi^{\prime}}\Lambda_{\lambda,\bullet}\otimes({\mathbf{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}}}{{\otimes}}_{\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}}M_{\chi^{\prime}})
, hence
{\mathbf{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}}}{{\otimes}}_{\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}}M_{\chi^{\prime}}
lies in degrees
-p_{\lambda}-\dim T_{\lambda}^{c}+\dim T_{\lambda}^{c}=-p_{\lambda}
(the lowest degree of
\Lambda_{\lambda,\bullet}
is
-\dim T_{\lambda}^{c}
). This implies that
M_{\chi^{\prime}}
admits a free resolution (as
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}
-modules) in degrees
\geq-p_{\lambda}
.

(3) The statement follows from (1)(2) and the observation that
\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\lambda}{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\chi}=0
and
\widetilde{i}^{!}_{\lambda}{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu,\chi}=0
.

∎

3.11.
Tilting sheaves

We are in the situation of Section
3.1
-Section
3.2
.

Definition 3.12
.

An object
{\mathcal{T}}
of
{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}
is called a
free-monodromic tilting sheaf
, if for each
\lambda\in I
, both complexes
\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}
and
\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{!}{\mathcal{T}}
are free-monodromic local systems in degree
-p_{\lambda}
.

From the definition, we see that an object
{\mathcal{T}}
of
{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}
is a free-monodromic tilting sheaf if and only if
{\mathcal{T}}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{H,X}
and
{\mathcal{T}}
has a
{\widetilde{\Delta}}
-flag and
{\widetilde{\nabla}}
-flag, i.e. it is both a successive extension of
{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}
’s and a successive extension of
{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}
’s in
{\mathcal{P}}_{H,X}
.

We will denote by
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X})\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}
the full additive subcategory free-monodromic tilting sheaves.

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X})
.

(1)

The complex
\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}^{\prime})
is concentrated in degree
0
, and
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}^{\prime})
has an increasing filtration indexed by the poset
I
with associated graded
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X_{\lambda}}}(\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{T},\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{!}\mathcal{T}^{\prime})
, which is finite free over
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}
.

(2)

In particular, if
\widetilde{X}_{\lambda}
is open in the intersections of the supports of both
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, then there is a natural surjection
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}^{\prime})\twoheadrightarrow\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X_{\lambda}}}(\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{T},\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{T}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

(1) Write
\mathcal{T}
as a
\widetilde{\Delta}
-flag and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
as a
\widetilde{\nabla}
-flag. The spectral sequence calculating
\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}^{\prime})
using these filtrations gives a filtration on
\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}}(\mathcal{T},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
with associated graded
\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X_{\lambda}}}(\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{T},\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{!}\mathcal{T}^{\prime})
, indexed by the poset
\lambda\in I_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\subset I
, where
I_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
consists of
\lambda\in I
such that
\widetilde{X}_{\lambda}
is in the support of both
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
. By Corollary
3.7
,
\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X_{\lambda}}}(\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{T},\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{!}\mathcal{T}^{\prime})\cong\oplus_{\chi:\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{\lambda})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}}\operatorname{RHom}_{\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}}(M_{\chi},M^{\prime}_{\chi})
where
(M_{\chi})
and
(M^{\prime}_{\chi})
are free
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}
-modules corresponding to
\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{T}
and
\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{!}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
. In particular,
\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X_{\lambda}}}(\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{T},\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{!}\mathcal{T}^{\prime})
is concentrated in degree
0
and is finite free over
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}
.

(2) Under the assumption
\lambda
is a maximal element in
I_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
. The filtration in (1) can be arranged to have the last quotient
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X_{\lambda}}}(\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{T},\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{!}\mathcal{T}^{\prime})
.
∎

Proposition 3.14
.

(1)

For each
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
there exists an indecomposable free-monodromic tilting sheaf
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\chi}
whose restriction to
\widetilde{X}_{\lambda}
is
{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda,\chi}[p_{\lambda}]
and whose support is the closure of
\widetilde{X}_{\lambda}
. Moreover, such
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\chi}
is unique up to isomorphism.

(2)

The map
(\lambda,\chi)\mapsto\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
is a bijection between
\widetilde{I}
and the set of isomorphism classes of indecomposable objects in
\mathrm{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X})
.

(3)

Every object
{\mathcal{T}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X})
is isomorphic to a finite direct sum of
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
, and the multiplicity of each
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
is an invariant of
\mathcal{T}
.

Proof.

(1) We first show the existence of
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
. Proceeding by the descending induction on strata we may assume that
Z=X_{\mu}
is a minimal stratum of
X
and on the preimage
\widetilde{U}=\pi^{-1}(U)
of its complement
U=X-Z
there is an indecomposable free-monodromic tilting sheaf
{\mathcal{T}}_{U}
satisfying the required conditions. Let
\widetilde{j}\colon\widetilde{U}\to\widetilde{X}
and
\widetilde{i}\colon\widetilde{Z}=\pi^{-1}(Z)\to\widetilde{X}
be the inclusions.

Let
\mathcal{C}=\widetilde{i}^{*}\widetilde{j}_{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{U}
and
(M_{\chi^{\prime}})_{\chi^{\prime}}=\Phi_{\mu}(\mathcal{C})\in D^{f}(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}\textup{-mod})
. Since
{\mathcal{T}}_{U}
has a
{\widetilde{\nabla}}
-flag, by Lemma
3.10
,
H^{i}M_{\chi^{\prime}}=0
for
i>-p_{\mu}
. Since
{\mathcal{T}}_{U}
has a
\widetilde{\Delta}
-flag and
\mathcal{C}[-1]\cong\widetilde{i}^{!}\widetilde{j}_{!}{\mathcal{T}}_{U}
, then by Lemma
3.10
, each
M_{\chi^{\prime}}[-1]
can be represented by a bounded complex of free
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}
-modules in degrees
\geq-p_{\mu}
, therefore
M_{\chi^{\prime}}
can be represented by a perfect complex of
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}
-modules in degrees
\geq-p_{\mu}-1
. Combining these, we see that
M_{\chi^{\prime}}
is quasi-isomorphic to a two-step complex of free
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}
-modules in degrees
-p_{\mu}-1
and
-p_{\mu}
. Correspondingly,
\mathcal{C}
is quasi-isomorphic to a two-step complex
[{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\varphi}{\mathcal{B}}]
of free-monodromic local systems on
Z
in degrees
-p_{\mu}-1
and
-p_{\mu}
respectively. We have a map
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{A}[p_{\mu}+1]
.

As in
[
3
]
we now put
{\mathcal{T}}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{H,X}
to be the extension

(3.10)

0\to\widetilde{i}_{*}{\mathcal{A}}[p_{\mu}]\to{\mathcal{T}}\to\widetilde{j}_{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{U}\to 0

defined by the map
\widetilde{j}_{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{U}\to\widetilde{i}_{*}\mathcal{C}\to\widetilde{i}_{*}{\mathcal{A}}[p_{\mu}+1]
. From this we see that

(3.11)

\widetilde{i}^{!}{\mathcal{T}}\cong{\mathcal{A}}[p_{\mu}].

Applying
\widetilde{i}^{*}
to the exact sequence (
3.10
) we see that
\mathcal{A}[p_{\mu}]\to\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}\to\mathcal{C}
is a distinguished triangle hence

(3.12)

\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}\cong{\mathcal{B}}[p_{\mu}].

Therefore we also have an exact sequence

0\to\widetilde{j}_{!}{\mathcal{T}}_{U}\to{\mathcal{T}}\to\widetilde{i}_{*}{\mathcal{B}}\to 0

in
{\mathcal{P}}_{H,X}
. From (
3.11
) and (
3.12
) and the fact that
{\mathcal{T}}_{U}
is free-monodromic tilting on
U
we conclude that
{\mathcal{T}}
is a free-monodromic tilting sheaf on
X
.

To ensure
\mathcal{T}
is indecomposable, we may represent
\mathcal{C}
by a two-step complex
[\mathcal{A}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathcal{B}]
of free-monodromic local systems such that
\operatorname{rk}_{\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}}\mathcal{A}+\operatorname{rk}_{\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}}\mathcal{B}
is minimal. This is equivalent to requiring that
\overline{\varphi}:\mathcal{A}/{\mathfrak{m}}\to\mathcal{B}/{\mathfrak{m}}
be zero, where
{\mathfrak{m}}\subset\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}
is the maximal ideal. Now if
\mathcal{T}
was decomposable as
\mathcal{T}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{T}_{2}
where
\mathcal{T}_{1}|_{\widetilde{U}}\neq 0
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}\neq 0
, then since
\mathcal{T}_{U}
is indecomposable, we must have
\mathcal{T}_{2}|_{\widetilde{U}}=0
, hence
\mathcal{T}_{2}
must be supported on
\widetilde{Z}
. This would imply that the complex
[\mathcal{T}_{2}\xrightarrow{{\rm id}}\mathcal{T}_{2}]
is a direct summand of
[\mathcal{A}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathcal{B}]
, which contradicts the minimality of
\operatorname{rk}_{\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}}\mathcal{A}+\operatorname{rk}_{\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}}\mathcal{B}
.

Proceeding as above by induction on strata, we have constructed an indecomposable
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X})
satisfying the desired properties.

We show that:

(3.13)

If
\alpha:\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}\to\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
is an isomorphism when restricted to
\widetilde{X}_{\lambda}
, then it is an isomorphism.

We show this by induction on strata. Using notation as above, we need to show that if
\alpha:\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{T}
is such that
\alpha_{U}=\alpha|_{\widetilde{U}}:\mathcal{T}_{U}\to\mathcal{T}_{U}
is an isomorphism, then so is
\alpha
. Indeed,
\alpha
induces an endomorphism of complexes
(a,b):[\mathcal{A}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathcal{B}]\to[\mathcal{A}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathcal{B}]
, where
a\in\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits_{\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}}(\mathcal{A})
and
b\in\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits_{\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}}(\mathcal{B})
. Since
\alpha_{U}
is an isomorphism, it induce an automorphism on
\mathcal{C}
. Therefore
(a,b)
is a quasi-isomorphism. After reduction mod
{\mathfrak{m}}
,
\varphi
becomes zero, and
(a,b)
mod
{\mathfrak{m}}
induces a quasi-isomorphism of
[\mathcal{A}/{\mathfrak{m}}\xrightarrow{0}\mathcal{B}/{\mathfrak{m}}]
.Therefore
a
and
b
are both isomorphisms mod
{\mathfrak{m}}
, and hence
a
and
b
are isomorphisms by Nakayama’s lemma. This shows in particular that
\alpha
restricts to an automorphism of
\widetilde{i}^{*}\mathcal{T}\cong\mathcal{B}[p_{\mu}]
. Since
\alpha_{U}
is an isomorphism as well,
\alpha
is then an isomorphism.

The uniqueness of
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
up to isomorphism will be shown together with part (2).

To prove (2) and (3), we first prove:

(3.14)

Let
\mathcal{T}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X})
. Let
\widetilde{X}_{\lambda}
be open in the support of
\mathcal{T}
, and suppose
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}[p_{\lambda}]
is a direct summand of
\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\lambda}\mathcal{T}
. Then
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
is isomorphic to a direct summand of
\mathcal{T}
.

By assumption, we have maps
\alpha:\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}[p_{\lambda}]\to\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\lambda}\mathcal{T}
and
\beta:\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\lambda}\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}[p_{\lambda}]
such that
\beta\circ\alpha={\rm id}
. By Lemma
3.13
,
\alpha
and
\beta
extend to
\widetilde{\alpha}:\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}\to\mathcal{T}
and
\widetilde{\beta}:\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
. Since
(\widetilde{\beta}\circ\widetilde{\alpha})|_{\widetilde{X}_{\lambda}}
is an isomorphism, by (
3.13
),
\widetilde{\beta}\circ\widetilde{\alpha}
is also an isomorphism. Therefore,
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
is isomorphic to a direct summand of
\mathcal{T}
.

(2) Clearly
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}\cong\mathcal{T}_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}}
if
(\lambda,\chi)\neq(\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
. Now let
\mathcal{T}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X})
be indecomposable and let
\widetilde{X}_{\lambda},\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}
be as in (
3.14
). By (
3.14
), we must have
\mathcal{T}\cong\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
. This shows that
\{\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}\}_{(\lambda,\chi)\in I}
exhaust all indecomposable objects in
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X})
.

(3) Extend the partial order on
I
to a total order, and we prove this statement by induction on the strata. For
\mathcal{T}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X})
, let
\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}
be the maximal element such that
\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{T}\neq 0
. When
\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}
is the minimal element in
I
,
\mathcal{T}
is supported on a closed stratum and the statement is clear. If
\lambda=\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}
is not minimal, let
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}[p_{\lambda}]
is a direct summand of
\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\lambda}\mathcal{T}
. By (
3.14
),
\mathcal{T}\cong\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}\oplus\mathcal{T}_{1}
for some
\mathcal{T}_{1}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X})
. Then
\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\lambda}\mathcal{T}_{1}
has smaller rank than
\mathcal{T}_{1}
. Repeating this process, some
\mathcal{T}_{n}
will have zero restriction to
\widetilde{X}_{\lambda}
. Therefore
\lambda_{\mathcal{T}_{n}}<\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}
and we apply inductive hypothesis to
\mathcal{T}_{n}
.

∎

We next prove the functoriality of free-monodromic tilting sheaves under proper pushforward. The result is not used in this paper.

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
\widetilde{X}\to X
and
\widetilde{Y}\to Y
be
H
-equivariant
T^{c}
-torsors satisfying the conditions of Section
3.2

4
4

4

In fact we don’t need to assume the cohomological bounds for links in
X
and
Y
.
. Let
\widetilde{f}\colon\widetilde{X}\to\widetilde{Y}
be an
H\times T^{c}
-equivariant proper map. Then for any free-monodromic tilting sheaf
{\mathcal{T}}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}
,
\widetilde{f}_{*}{\mathcal{T}}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{H,Y}
is also a free-monodromic tilting sheaf.

Proof.

Since
\widetilde{f}
is proper,
\widetilde{f}_{*}
commutes with restriction and corestriction to
H\times T^{c}
-orbits, it suffices to assume that both
X
and
Y
has a single stratum, so that
X=H/H_{x},Y=H/H_{y}
and
y=f(x)
,
H_{x}\subset H_{y}
. Let
T^{c}_{x}\subset T^{c}_{y}\subset T^{c}
be the images of
H_{x}
and
H_{y}
.

Let
n_{X}=\dim T^{c}/T^{c}_{x}
,
n_{Y}=\dim T^{c}/T^{c}_{y}
,
d_{X}=\dim X
,
d_{Y}=\dim Y
,
p_{X}=\lfloor\frac{d_{X}+n_{X}}{2}\rfloor
and
p_{Y}=\lfloor\frac{d_{Y}+n_{Y}}{2}\rfloor
.

We claim that

(3.15)

p_{X}-n_{X}=p_{Y}-n_{Y}.

Indeed, let
U_{x}=\ker(H_{x}\to T^{c}_{x})
and
U_{y}=\ker(H_{x}\to T^{c}_{x})
, which are contractible Lie groups by assumption. Since
f:X\to Y
is proper,
H_{y}/H_{x}
is compact. On the other hand,
H_{x}/H_{y}
is a fibration over
T^{c}_{y}/T^{c}_{x}
with contractible fiber
U_{y}/U_{x}
. This implies
U_{x}=U_{y}
, and
H_{y}/H_{x}\cong T^{c}_{y}/T^{c}_{x}
. Hence
d_{X}-d_{Y}=\dim H_{y}-\dim H_{x}=\dim T^{c}_{y}-\dim T^{c}_{x}=n_{X}-n_{Y}
. Therefore
d_{X}-n_{X}=d_{Y}-n_{Y}
, which implies (
3.15
).

Restricting to the fibers over
x
and
y
respectively we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{i}_{x}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\widehat{D}^{b}_{T_{x}^{c}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{i}_{y}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\widehat{D}^{b}_{T_{y}^{c}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}}

where
\varphi_{*}
is the induction functor for the inclusion
T^{c}_{x}\subset T^{c}_{y}
. In this situation, we may assume
\widetilde{i}_{x}^{*}\mathcal{T}\in D^{b}_{T_{x}^{c}}(T^{c})_{T^{c}\textup{-mon}}
is the shifted free-monodromic local system
\mathcal{L}_{\chi}[p_{X}]
for some
\chi:\pi_{0}(T^{c}_{x})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
. The fiber of the quotient map
\overline{\varphi}:T_{x}^{c}\backslash T^{c}\to T_{y}^{c}\backslash T^{c}
is isomorphic to
T^{c}_{x}\backslash T^{c}_{y}
, a compact Lie group whose neutral component is a torus of dimension
n_{X}-n_{Y}
. Therefore,
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\chi}
is a direct sum of free-monodromic local systems in degree
n_{X}-n_{Y}
. This implies that
\widetilde{f}_{*}\mathcal{T}
is a direct sum of free-monodromic local systems in degree
-p_{X}+n_{X}-n_{Y}
. By (
3.15
),
-p_{X}+n_{X}-n_{Y}=-p_{Y}
, therefore
\widetilde{f}_{*}\mathcal{T}
is a free-monodromic tilting sheaf on
\widetilde{Y}
.
∎

4.
Preliminaries on real groups

In this section we collect some facts about real algebraic groups and their orbits on the flag variety.

4.1.
Abstract Cartan and abstract Weyl group

Let
G
be a connected reductive complex Lie group. Let
X
be the flag variety of
G
.
Let
{\mathbf{W}}
be the
abstract Weyl group
of
G
. As a set, it is defined as the set of
G
-orbits on
X\times X
. For
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
let
X^{2}_{w}\subset X\times X
be the corresponding
G
-orbit. Simple reflections in
{\mathbf{W}}
are those
s\in{\mathbf{W}}
such that
\dim X^{2}_{w}=\dim X+1
. When
(B,B^{\prime})\in X^{2}_{w}
, we write
pos(B,B^{\prime})=w
.

Consider the space
Y
of pairs
T\subset B
where
T
is a maximal torus of
G
and
B
is a Borel subgroup containing it. Let
\mathscr{T}
be the space of maximal tori in
G
. If we choose a maximal torus
T\subset G
, we may identify
Y
with
G/T
and
\mathscr{T}
with
G/N_{G}(T)
. We get the following diagram where the maps are forgetting
T
or
B
:

\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{\mathscr{T}.}

Both maps
\beta,\gamma
are
G
-equivariant. For each
(T\subset B)\in Y
and
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
, there is a unique
(T\subset B^{w})\in Y
such that
pos(B,B^{w})=w
. This defines a group structure on
{\mathbf{W}}
so that
\gamma
becomes a
G
-equivariant
{\mathbf{W}}
-torsor.

For different choices of Borel subgroups
B
and
B^{\prime}
of
G
, their reductive quotients are canonically identified, which we call the
abstract Cartan

{\mathbf{T}}
of
G
. There is a canonical right action of
{\mathbf{W}}
characterized as follows: for any
(T\subset B)\in Y
, and
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
, the following diagram is commutative

(4.1)

\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{can}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{T}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)w}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{w}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{can}
\textstyle{\mathbf{T}}

where the maps ‘‘can’’ are the canonical quotients.

For each
(T\subset B)\in Y
we have the based root system
\Phi(G,B,T)
where positive roots are those appearing in
B
. For different choices of
(T\subset B)\in Y
these based root systems are canonically identified with one another. We denote the resulting canonical based root system by
\Phi
. It can be viewed as a based root system for the abstract Cartan
{\mathbf{T}}
with Weyl group
{\mathbf{W}}
. Let
\underline{\Phi}
be the underlying root system of
\Phi
(i.e., without the basis).

4.2.
Real form

Let
\sigma\colon G\to G
be an anti-holomorphic involution on
G
compatible with the group structure. We put
G_{\mathbb{R}}=G^{\sigma}
for the corresponding real form, viewed as an algebraic group over
\mathbb{R}
. We use
G(\mathbb{R})
to denote the real points of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
, as a Lie group. Put
{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathrm{Lie}(G)
and
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{R}}=\mathrm{Lie}(G_{\mathbb{R}})={\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
.

Lemma 4.3
.

(1)

Each Borel subgroup
B\subset G
contains a
\sigma
-stable maximal torus
T
.

(2)

Any two
\sigma
-stable maximal tori in
B
are conjugate under
G(\mathbb{R})\cap B
.

Proof.

(1) The subgroup
H:=B\cap\sigma(B)
of
G
is stable under
\sigma
, hence is the complexification of a real group
H_{\mathbb{R}}\subset G_{\mathbb{R}}
. Note that
H
is solvable and contains a maximal torus of
G
. By
[
11
, Proposition 7.10]
,
H
contains a maximal torus
T
defined over
\mathbb{R}
, then
T\subset H\subset B
is
\sigma
-stable and is a maximal torus of
G
.

(2) If
T,T^{\prime}
are two
\sigma
-stable maximal tori in
B
, then they are both in
H
, hence they are conjugate by some
u\in H^{u}
(the unipotent radical of
H
). This implies
u^{-1}\sigma(u)\in N_{H}(T)\cap H^{u}=\{1\}
hence
u\in H^{u}(\mathbb{R})\subset G(\mathbb{R})\cap B
.

∎

4.4.
Real orbits on the flag variety

Let
I
be the set of
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbits on
X
by left translation. For
\lambda\in I
, we denote the corresponding orbit by
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
, so that

X=\bigcup_{\lambda\in I}O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}.

Let
\mathscr{T}^{\sigma}\subset\mathscr{T}
be the set of
\sigma
-stable maximal tori in
G
. Let
Y_{\sigma}\subset Y=\gamma^{-1}(\mathscr{T}^{\sigma})
whose points are pairs
(T\subset B)\in Y
where
T
is
\sigma
-stable. Both
\mathscr{T}^{\sigma}
and
Y_{\sigma}
carry left actions of
G(\mathbb{R})
by conjugation. We have the following diagram where the maps are forgetting
T
or
B
:

\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{Y_{\sigma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{\sigma}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{T}^{\sigma}}

Both maps
\beta_{\sigma},\gamma_{\sigma}
are
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant. The map
\gamma_{\sigma}
is a
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant
{\mathbf{W}}
-torsor.

Lemma 4.5
.

(1)

The map
\beta_{\sigma}:Y_{\sigma}\to X
is surjective and it induces a bijection on
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbits
\underline{\beta}_{\sigma}:G(\mathbb{R})\backslash Y_{\sigma}\leftrightarrow G(\mathbb{R})\backslash X
.

(2)

The map
\gamma_{\sigma}:Y_{\sigma}\to\mathscr{T}^{\sigma}
is a
{\mathbf{W}}
-torsor. It induces a surjective map
\underline{\gamma_{\sigma}}:G(\mathbb{R})\backslash Y_{\sigma}\leftrightarrow G(\mathbb{R})\backslash\mathscr{T}^{\sigma}
whose fibers are
{\mathbf{W}}
-orbits.

(3)

The right
{\mathbf{W}}
action on
G(\mathbb{R})\backslash Y_{\sigma}
defines a right
{\mathbf{W}}
-action on
I=G(\mathbb{R})\backslash X
via the bijection
\underline{\beta_{\sigma}}
, and the composition
\underline{\gamma_{\sigma}}\circ\underline{\beta_{\sigma}}^{-1}:I\to G(\mathbb{R})\backslash\mathscr{T}^{\sigma}
is the quotient map by
{\mathbf{W}}
. We denote the right
{\mathbf{W}}
-action on
I
by
\lambda\mapsto\lambda\cdot w
(
\lambda\in I
,
w\in W
).

Proof.

(1) follows from Lemma
4.3
. (2) and (3) are clear.
∎

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\lambda\in I,B\in O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
and
T\subset B
be a
\sigma
-stable maximal torus. Consider the isomorphism of tori

\iota_{B}:T\subset B\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbf{T}}.

We have:

(1)

The real structure
\sigma|_{T}
induces via
\iota_{B}
a real structure
\sigma_{T\subset B}
(anti-holomorphic involution) on
{\mathbf{T}}
. Then
\sigma_{T\subset B}
depends only on the orbit
\lambda
. We denote it by
\sigma_{\lambda}
.

(2)

The real points
{\mathbf{T}}^{\sigma_{\lambda}}
under
\sigma_{\lambda}
is the image of the canonical projection
G(\mathbb{R})\cap B\subset B\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbf{T}}
(which is then independent of
B\in O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
).

Proof.

(1) By Lemma
4.5
(1), any two such
(T\subset B)
(with
B\in O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
) are
G(\mathbb{R})
-conjugate. For
g\in G(\mathbb{R})
, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Ad}(g)}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{B}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{T}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ad}(g)T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{Ad}(g)B}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{T}}

From this we conclude that
\sigma_{T\subset B}
is the same as
\sigma_{\operatorname{Ad}(g)T\subset\operatorname{Ad}(g)B}
.

(2) We use the notation
H
from the proof of part (1) of Lemma
4.3
. We have
G(\mathbb{R})\cap B=H(\mathbb{R})
. Moreover,
T(\mathbb{R})
is a maximal torus in the solvable real group
H(\mathbb{R})
. Since the kernel of the projection
H(\mathbb{R})\to{\mathbf{T}}
is unipotent, its image is the reductive quotient of
H(\mathbb{R})
. Therefore
T(\mathbb{R})
maps isomorphically to the image of
H(\mathbb{R})\to{\mathbf{T}}
. By definition,
T(\mathbb{R})
also maps isomorphically via
\iota_{B}
to
{\mathbf{T}}^{\sigma_{\lambda}}
. The statement follows.
∎

Definition 4.7
.

Let
T
be a
\sigma
-stable maximal torus of
G
with real points
T(\mathbb{R})
. We say that an orbit
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
is
attached to

T
if
\underline{\gamma_{\sigma}}\circ\underline{\beta_{\sigma}}
maps
\lambda
to the
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit of
T
. In other words,
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
is attached to
T
if there exists a
T
-fixed point in
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
.

4.8.
Roots

Fix a
\sigma
-stable maximal torus
T\subset G
. Let
\Phi(G,T)
be the set of roots of
G
with respect to
T
.

Note that
\sigma
acts on the set of roots
\Phi(G,T)
: if
\alpha\in\Phi(G,T)
viewed as a homomorphism
T\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
over
\mathbb{C}
, then
\sigma\alpha:T\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
is defined as
t\mapsto\overline{\alpha(\sigma t)}
.

Let
\langle{-,-}\rangle:\mathfrak{g}\times\mathfrak{g}\to\mathbb{C}
be the Killing form. Note that
\langle{\sigma(x),\sigma(y)}\rangle=\overline{\langle{x,y}\rangle}
for any
x,y\in\mathfrak{g}
. In particular,
\langle{x,\sigma(x)}\rangle\in\mathbb{R}
.

Definition 4.9
.

A root
\alpha\in\Phi(G,T)
is called

(1)

complex
if
\sigma\alpha\neq\pm\alpha
;

(2)

real
if
\sigma\alpha=\alpha
;

(3)

compact imaginary
if
\sigma\alpha=-\alpha
and for nonzero
x\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{\alpha}
,
\langle{x,\sigma(x)}\rangle<0
.

(4)

noncompact imaginary
if
\sigma\alpha=-\alpha
and for nonzero
x\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{\alpha}
,
\langle{x,\sigma(x)}\rangle>0
.

Remark 4.10
.

This definition is compatible with the definition for a root system invariant under the corresponding Cartan involution (see, for example,
[
27
, Section 2]
.

4.11.
Based root system attached to a real orbit

Recall we have the abstract based root system
\Phi
on
{\mathbf{T}}
with Weyl group
{\mathbf{W}}
. To each point
(T\subset B)\in Y_{\sigma}
we have a canonical isomorphism of based root systems
\Phi(G,B,T)\cong\Phi
, under the isomorphism
T\subset B\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbf{T}}
. Since
T
is
\sigma
-stable,
\sigma
acts on the root system
\Phi(G,T)
without necessarily preserving the positive roots. In particular, we get an involution
\sigma
on the underlying root system
\underline{\Phi}
of
\Phi
. The assignment
Y_{\sigma}\ni(T\subset B)\mapsto\textup{Inv}(\underline{\Phi})
(the set of involutions on
\underline{\Phi}
) is
G(\mathbb{R})
-invariant, hence it induces a map
G(\mathbb{R})\backslash Y_{\sigma}\to\textup{Inv}(\underline{\Phi})
. Using the bijection
\underline{\beta}_{\sigma}
in Lemma
4.5
, we get a map
G(\mathbb{R})\backslash X=I\to\textup{Inv}(\underline{\Phi})
. For
\lambda\in I
, we denote by
\Phi_{\lambda}
the based root system
\Phi
equipped with the involution constructed above on
\underline{\Phi}
.

For
\alpha\in\Phi_{\lambda}
, we can talk about whether it is real, complex or imaginary according to Definition
4.9
.

For a simple root
\alpha\in\Phi
, let
X_{\alpha}
be the partial flag variety parametrizing parabolic subgroups conjugate to
P_{\alpha}
(generated by a Borel
B
and root subgroup of
-\alpha
). Let
\pi_{\alpha}\colon X\to X_{\alpha}
be the projection which is a
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
-fibration.

There is a partial order on
I
:
\mu\leq\lambda
if and only if
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}\subset\overline{O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}}
.
The following statement is analogous to the results of
[
26
, Lemma 5.1]
and
[
22
, Sections 2.2, 2.3]
.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
\lambda\in I
. Let
\alpha\in\Phi_{\lambda}
be a simple root and
s_{\alpha}\in{\mathbf{W}}
