be the corresponding simple reflection.

(1)

If
\alpha
is a complex root, then
\lambda\cdot s_{\alpha}\neq\lambda
, and
\pi_{\alpha}^{-1}\pi_{\alpha}(O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}})=O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}\cup O_{\lambda\cdot s_{\alpha}}^{\mathbb{R}}
.

•

If
\sigma\alpha>0
, then
\lambda<\lambda\cdot s_{\alpha}
and
\pi_{\alpha}|O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
is an isomorphism onto its image.

•

If
\sigma\alpha<0
, then
\lambda\cdot s_{\alpha}<\lambda
and
\pi_{\alpha}|O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
is an
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-fibration over its image.

(2)

If
\alpha
is a real root, then
\lambda\cdot s_{\alpha}=\lambda
. Moreover, one of the following happens:

•

Type I: there are two orbits
\mu^{+},\mu^{-}>\lambda
such that
\mu^{-}=\mu^{+}\cdot s_{\alpha}
, and
\pi_{\alpha}^{-1}\pi_{\alpha}(O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}})=O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}\cup O_{\mu^{+}}^{\mathbb{R}}\cup O_{\mu^{-}}^{\mathbb{R}}
. Moreover,
\pi_{\alpha}|O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
is an
S^{1}
-fibration, and
\pi_{\alpha}|O_{\mu^{+}}^{\mathbb{R}}
and
\pi_{\alpha}|O_{\mu^{-}}^{\mathbb{R}}
are
D^{2}
-fibrations (
D^{2}
is an open real
2
-dimensional disk).

•

Type II: there is
\mu>\lambda
such that
\mu\cdot s_{\alpha}=\mu
and
\pi_{\alpha}^{-1}\pi_{\alpha}(O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}})=O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}\cup O_{\mu}^{\mathbb{R}}
, and
\pi_{\alpha}|O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
is an
S^{1}
-fibration over its image.

(3)

If
\alpha
is compact imaginary, then
\lambda\cdot s_{\alpha}=\lambda
and
\pi_{\alpha}^{-1}\pi_{\alpha}(O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}})=O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
.

(4)

If
\alpha
is noncompact imaginary, then there is a unique
\mu<\lambda
with
\mu\cdot s_{\alpha}=\mu
such that one of the following happens:

•

Type I:
\lambda\cdot s_{\alpha}\neq\lambda
,
\mu<\lambda\cdot s_{\alpha}
, and
\pi_{\alpha}^{-1}\pi_{\alpha}(O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}})=O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}\cup O_{\lambda\cdot s_{\alpha}}^{\mathbb{R}}\cup O_{\mu}^{\mathbb{R}}
. Moreover,
\pi_{\alpha}|O_{\mu}^{\mathbb{R}}
is an
S^{1}
-fibration, and
\pi_{\alpha}|O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
and
\pi_{\alpha}|O_{\lambda\cdot s_{\alpha}}^{\mathbb{R}}
are
D^{2}
-fibrations (
D^{2}
is an open real
2
-dimensional disk).

•

Type II:
\lambda\cdot s_{\alpha}=\lambda
,
\pi_{\alpha}^{-1}\pi_{\alpha}(O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}})=O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}\cup O_{\mu}^{\mathbb{R}}
, and
\pi_{\alpha}|O_{\mu}^{\mathbb{R}}
is an
S^{1}
-fibration over its image.

Proof.

See
loc.cit.

∎

4.13.
Real Weyl groups

Let
T
be a
\sigma
-stable maximal torus. Let
T_{\mathbb{R}}=T^{\sigma}
be the corresponding real form of
T
. We denote the Weyl group of
T
by
W
, which carries an action of
\sigma
. Let
W(\mathbb{R})=W^{\sigma}\subset W
be the fixed point subgroup of
\sigma
. Indeed,
W
carries the structure of a finite étale group scheme over
\mathbb{R}
, and
W(\mathbb{R})
thus defined is its group of
\mathbb{R}
-points. On the other hand we have the Weyl group
W(G(\mathbb{R}),T(\mathbb{R}))=N_{G(\mathbb{R})}(T(\mathbb{R}))/T(\mathbb{R})
. Clearly we have
W(G(\mathbb{R}),T(\mathbb{R}))\subset W(\mathbb{R})
.

Lemma 4.14
.

(1)

We have
W(\mathbb{R})=\mathrm{Stab}_{W}(T(\mathbb{R}))
.

(2)

Suppose
T
is a
\sigma
-stable maximal torus that is maximally split, then the inclusion
W(G(\mathbb{R}),T(\mathbb{R}))\subset W(\mathbb{R})
is an equality.

Proof.

(1) Let
{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{R}}
(resp.
{\mathfrak{t}}
) be the Lie algebra of
T(\mathbb{R})
(resp.
T
). Then both
W(\mathbb{R})=W^{\sigma}
and
\mathrm{Stab}_{W}(T(\mathbb{R}))
consist of
w\in W
that preserve the decomposition
{\mathfrak{t}}={\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{R}}\oplus i{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

(2) follows from
[
27
, Propositions 3.12 and 4.16]
as there are no noncompact imaginary roots for maximally split torus.
∎

If
T\in\mathscr{T}^{\sigma}
and
B
is a Borel subgroup containing
T
, we get a canonical identification
T\cong{\mathbf{T}}
and
W\cong{\mathbf{W}}
compatible with the actions.

Lemma 4.15
.

In the above situation, suppose
B\in O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
. Then under the isomorphism
W\cong{\mathbf{W}}
(induced by
B
),
W(G(\mathbb{R}),T(\mathbb{R}))
is identified with the stabilizer
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda}
of
\lambda
under the right action of
{\mathbf{W}}
on
I
.

Proof.

Let
\iota:W\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{W}}
be the canonical isomorphism. If
\dot{w}\in N_{G(\mathbb{R})}(T(\mathbb{R}))
has image
w\in W
, then
B
and
\operatorname{Ad}(\dot{w})B
are both in
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
and both contain
T
. By definition
\iota(w)=pos(B,\operatorname{Ad}(\dot{w})B)
. By the definition of the
{\mathbf{W}}
-action, we see that
\lambda\cdot\iota(w)=\lambda
. This proves
\iota(W(G(\mathbb{R}),T(\mathbb{R})))\subset{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda}
.

Conversely, suppose
v\in{\mathbf{W}}
is such that
\lambda\cdot v=\lambda
, then
B^{v}
(the unique Borel containing
T
such that
pos(B,B^{v})=v
) lies in
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
. Therefore there exists
g\in G(\mathbb{R})
such that
B^{v}=\operatorname{Ad}(g)B
. Now
T
and
\operatorname{Ad}(g)T
are both
\sigma
-stable maximal tori in
B^{v}
, by Lemma
4.3
, there exists
h\in G(\mathbb{R})\cap B^{v}
such that
\operatorname{Ad}(hg)T=T
, i.e.,
hg\in G(\mathbb{R})\cap N_{G}(T)=N_{G(\mathbb{R})}(T(\mathbb{R}))
. Since
\operatorname{Ad}(hg)B=\operatorname{Ad}(h)(B^{v})=B^{v}
, we see that the image
w
of
hg
in
W
satisfies
\iota(w)=v
. Therefore
v\in\iota(W(G(\mathbb{R}),T(\mathbb{R})))
. This finishes the proof.
∎

4.16.
The cross action of
{\mathbf{W}}
on
\widetilde{I}

Recall from Lemma
4.6
(1) the real form
\sigma_{\lambda}
on
{\mathbf{T}}
for
\lambda\in I
. Let
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
be the image of the real points
{\mathbf{T}}^{\sigma_{\lambda}}
under the projection
{\mathbf{T}}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
. By Lemma
4.6
(2),
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}
is the image of
G(\mathbb{R})\cap B\to{\mathbf{T}}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
for any
B\in O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
, therefore this notation is consistent with that of Section
3.1
.

If
T
is a
\sigma
-stable maximal torus and
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
is attached to
T
, then by Lemma
4.6
, via a choice of
B\in(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})^{T}
,
{\mathbf{T}}_{\lambda}^{c}
can be identified with the compact part of
T(\mathbb{R})
. In particular, we have an isomorphism

(4.2)

\iota_{B}:\pi_{0}(T(\mathbb{R}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}).

Recall the right action of
{\mathbf{W}}
on
{\mathbf{T}}
, which induces a right action on
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
. From the commutative diagram (
4.1
) we see that

{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}\cdot w={\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda\cdot w},\quad\forall\lambda\in I,w\in{\mathbf{W}}

as subgroups of
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
. In particular, the action of
{\mathbf{W}}
on
{\mathbf{T}}
restricts to an action of
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda}
on
{\mathbf{T}}_{\lambda}^{c}
.

Recall the notations
\textup{LS}_{\lambda}
, the bijection (
3.1
) and
\widetilde{I}=\{(\lambda,\chi)|\chi:\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}\}
from Section
3.1
.
In
[
27
, Definition 4.1]
, Vogan defines a
cross action
of
{\mathbf{W}}
on
\widetilde{I}
that lifts the action of
{\mathbf{W}}
on
I
from Lemma
4.5
. We will turn the cross action
w\times(-)
into a right action and denote it by

(\lambda,\chi)\cdot w:=w\times(\lambda,\chi),\quad\forall w\in{\mathbf{W}},(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}.

By
[
26
, Definition 6.3]
, for a simple reflection
s\in{\mathbf{W}}
, its action on
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
is as follows:

(1)

If
\alpha_{s}
is a complex root for
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
, then there is a canonical isomorphism
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})\cong\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda\cdot s})
(both are identified with the
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant fundamental group of the image of
O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
in the partial flag variety
X_{s}
. Under this isomorphism, we have
(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s=(\lambda\cdot s,\chi)
.

(2)

If
\alpha_{s}
is type I noncompact imaginary, then there is a canonical isomorphism
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})\cong\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda\cdot s})
for the same reason as above. Under this isomorphism, we have
(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s=(\lambda\cdot s,\chi)
.

(3)

If
\alpha_{s}
is type II real, and the local system
{\mathbf{k}}_{\chi}
on
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
extends to
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})
. Let
\mu>\lambda
be as in Lemma
4.12
. Then
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\mu}\cap{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}
has index
2
, which induces a sign character

(4.3)

\operatorname{sgn}_{s}:\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}/{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}\cap{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\mu}\cong\{\pm 1\}\subset{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}.

Then
(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s=(\lambda,\chi\otimes\operatorname{sgn}_{s})
.

(4)

In all other cases,
(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s=(\lambda,\chi)
.

5.
Matsuki correspondence as Ringel duality

In this section, we apply the theory of tilting sheaves developed in Section
3
to real group orbits on the enhanced flag variety.

5.1.
Setup

Let
G
be a connected semisimple complex Lie group together with the anti-holomorphic involution
\sigma
. We put
G_{\mathbb{R}}=G^{\sigma}\subset G
to be the corresponding real form. Let
K_{\mathbb{R}}\subset G_{\mathbb{R}}
be a maximal compact subgroup of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
and
K\subset G
be the complexification of
K_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Choose a maximal torus
T_{0}
and a Borel subgroup
B_{0}\subset G
, let
U_{0}\subset B_{0}
be the unipotent radical. Via
B_{0}
,
T_{0}
is identified with the abstract Cartan
{\mathbf{T}}
. Let
{\mathbf{T}}={\mathbf{T}}^{>0}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
be the decomposition of the
\mathbb{C}
-points of the abstract Cartan
{\mathbf{T}}
into the neutral component
{\mathbf{T}}^{>0}
and the maximal compact subgroup
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
.

Consider the flag variety
X=G/B_{0}
of
G
. Consider the
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-torsor
\pi\colon\widetilde{X}=(G/U)/{\mathbf{T}}^{>0}\to X
. When
G
is adjoint, we can define
\widetilde{X}
as the space of
(B,\{x_{\alpha}\})
where
B
is a Borel subgroup and for each simple root
\alpha\in\Phi
,
x_{\alpha}
is basis of the
\alpha
-weight space of
U/[U,U]
under the
{\mathbf{T}}
-action. For general
G
, we need to choose a base point
B_{0}\in X
in order to define
\widetilde{X}
. We will consider the left action of
H=G({\mathbb{R}})
on
\widetilde{X}
.

5.2.
Matsuki correspondence

Let us recall some of the results and constructions of
[
20
]
.

The Matsuki correspondence is a canonical order-reversing bijection between the
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbits and
K
-orbits on
X
. This bijection is realized by a
K(\mathbb{R})
-invariant flow
\Phi_{t}\colon X\to X\,(t\in{\mathbb{R}})
, such that

(1)

The fixed point set of
\Phi_{t}
is a finite union of
K(\mathbb{R})
-orbits
\{C_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in I}
indexed a finite set
I
.

(2)

For any
\lambda\in I
set
O_{\lambda}^{{\mathbb{R}}}
(resp.
O^{K}_{\lambda}
) to be the
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit (resp.
K
-orbit) of
X
containing
C_{\lambda}
. Then we have
O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}=\{x\in X|\lim_{t\to+\infty}\Phi_{t}(x)\in C_{\lambda}\}
and
O_{\lambda}^{K}=\{x\in X|\lim_{t\to-\infty}\Phi_{t}(x)\in C_{\lambda}\}
. The bijection
O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}\leftrightarrow O^{K}_{\lambda}
gives an order reversing bijection between the
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbits and
K
-orbits on
X
which is called the
Matsuki correspondence for orbits
.

(3)

The orbits
\{O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}\}
and
\{O^{K}_{\lambda}\}
intersect pairwise transversally. The natural projections
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}\to C_{\lambda}
and
O^{K}_{\lambda}\to C_{\lambda}
given by the limits of the flow
\Phi_{t}
are fibrations with contractible fibers.

For
\lambda\in I
, let
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
be the preimage of
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
in
\widetilde{X}
. For
\lambda\in I
, recall the subgroup
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
defined in Section
4.16
, which by Lemma
4.6
(2) coincides with the subgroup
T^{c}_{\lambda}
defined in Section
3.1
for
T^{c}={\mathbf{T}}^{c}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
x\in C_{\lambda}
and
K(\mathbb{R})_{x}
be the stabilizer of
x
under
C_{\lambda}
. Then the projection
K(\mathbb{R})_{x}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
is injective and its image is
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}
. Moreover, the
G(\mathbb{R})
-action on
X
satisfies the condition (
3.2
).

Proof.

Since
K(\mathbb{R})_{x}
is compact and solvable (as an algebraic group over
\mathbb{R}
), its neutral component is a compact torus. The projection
K(\mathbb{R})_{x}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
is injective with closed image.

By Lemma
4.6
(2),
{\mathbf{T}}_{\lambda}^{c}
is the image of the projection
\gamma_{x}:G(\mathbb{R})_{x}\to{\mathbf{T}}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
. The square of the projection
{\mathbf{T}}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
is real algebraic, hence
\gamma^{2}_{x}:G(\mathbb{R})_{x}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
is real algebraic, and its image is therefore a real algebraic subgroup of
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
, hence closed with finitely many components. This implies that the image
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}
is a closed subgroup of
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
with finitely many components. The kernel
\ker(\gamma_{x})
is an extension of a closed subgroup of
{\mathbf{T}}^{>0}
and the unipotent real algebraic group
\ker(\widetilde{\gamma}_{x})
, hence
\ker(\gamma_{x})
is contractible, and
G(\mathbb{R})_{x}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}
is a homotopy equivalence.

Since
x
lies in the critical
K(\mathbb{R})
-orbit
C_{\lambda}
, which is homotopy equivalent to
O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
, the inclusion
K(\mathbb{R})_{x}\hookrightarrow G(\mathbb{R})_{x}
is a homotopy equivalence. Therefore
K(\mathbb{R})_{x}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}
is also a homotopy equivalence. Now
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}/K(\mathbb{R})_{x}
is both a compact manifold and contractible, hence it must be a point. We conclude that
K(\mathbb{R})_{x}
maps isomorphically to
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
.
∎

5.4.
Tilting sheaves on real orbits

We will now observe that

Proposition 5.5
.

The
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-torsor
\pi\colon\widetilde{X}\to X
with the action of
H=G(\mathbb{R})
satisfies the conditions of Section
3.2
.

Proof.

The condition (
3.2
) is already checked in Lemma
5.3
.

We check the parity condition (
3.3
). From the transversality of
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
and
O^{K}_{\lambda}
we get

d_{\lambda}+2\dim_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\lambda}=2\dim_{\mathbb{C}}X+\dim C_{\lambda}.

By Lemma
5.3
,

(5.1)

\dim C_{\lambda}=\dim K(\mathbb{R})-\dim{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}=\dim K(\mathbb{R})-\dim{\mathbf{T}}^{c}+n_{\lambda}.

These imply

(5.2)

d_{\lambda}-n_{\lambda}=2\operatorname{codim}_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\lambda}+\dim K(\mathbb{R})-\dim{\mathbf{T}}^{c}.

Since
\dim K(\mathbb{R})-\dim{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
is independent of
\lambda
, (
3.3
) holds.

We check the cohomological bound of links (
3.4
).
Suppose
\lambda<\mu
and consider now the intersection
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}\cap O^{K}_{\lambda}
. The limit maps
\lim_{t\to\pm\infty}\Phi_{t}(p)
provide a diagram

\textstyle{Y:=O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}\cap O^{K}_{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{h_{\mu}}
\textstyle{C_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{C_{\mu}}

with the maps being the fibrations. For
x_{\lambda}\in C_{\lambda}
, the fiber of
O^{K}_{\lambda}\to C_{\lambda}
over
x_{\lambda}
is a transversal slice to
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
, therefore the fiber
h_{\lambda}^{-1}(x_{\lambda})
is diffeomorphic to the link
L^{\mu}_{x_{\lambda}}
, which we shall denote by
{}^{\mathbb{R}}L^{\mu}_{x_{\lambda}}
to emphasize it is the link for
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbits. Similarly, for
x_{\mu}\in C_{\mu}
,
h_{\mu}^{-1}(x_{\mu})
is diffeomorphic to the link
{}^{K}L^{\lambda}_{x_{\mu}}
for the
K
-orbit
O^{K}_{\mu}
in
O^{K}_{\lambda}
. Let

\mathcal{F}_{\chi}=Rh_{\lambda*}h_{\mu}^{*}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\chi},\quad\mathcal{F}^{i}_{\chi}:=R^{i}h_{\lambda*}h_{\mu}^{*}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\chi}.

Since
h_{\lambda}
is
K(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant,
\mathcal{F}^{i}_{\chi}
is a
K(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant local system on
C_{\lambda}
. We need to show that

\mathcal{F}^{i}_{\chi}=0,\quad i>\Delta:=\frac{1}{2}(d_{\mu}+n_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}-n_{\lambda}).

As a
K(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant local system on
C_{\lambda}
,
\mathcal{F}^{i}_{\chi}
is determined by its stalk at
x_{\lambda}
and the monodromy action of
\pi_{0}(K(\mathbb{R})_{x_{\lambda}})=\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})
(by Lemma
5.3
) on
\mathcal{F}^{i}_{\chi}|_{x_{\lambda}}
. Then
\mathcal{F}^{i}_{\chi}=0
if and only if
H^{\dim C_{\lambda}}(C_{\lambda},\mathcal{F}^{i}_{\chi}\otimes{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\theta})=0
for any character
\theta:\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
.

Now we introduce
K(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant complexes and local systems on
C_{\mu}
for any character
\theta:\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}

\mathcal{G}_{\theta}=Rh_{\mu*}h_{\lambda}^{*}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\theta},\quad\mathcal{G}^{i}_{\theta}=R^{i}h_{\mu*}h_{\lambda}^{*}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\theta}.

Note that

(5.3)

H^{*}(C_{\lambda},\mathcal{F}_{\chi}\otimes{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\theta})\cong H^{*}(Y,{}_{\theta}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\chi})\cong H^{*}(C_{\mu},\mathcal{G}_{\theta}\otimes{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\chi}).

Here
{}_{\theta}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\chi}
is the local system
h_{\lambda}^{*}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\theta}\otimes h_{\mu}^{*}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\chi}
on
Y
.

Let
N
be the largest number such that
\mathcal{F}_{\chi}^{N}\neq 0
. We need to show
N\leq\Delta
. By the first isomorphism in (
5.3
) and the Leray spectral sequence,
H^{\dim C_{\lambda}+N}(Y,{}_{\theta}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\chi}))\cong H^{\dim C_{\lambda}}(C_{\lambda},\mathcal{F}^{N}_{\chi}\otimes{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\theta})
. Therefore, it suffices to show that

(5.4)

H^{\dim C_{\lambda}+N}(Y,{}_{\theta}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\chi})),\quad\mbox{ for }i>\dim C_{\lambda}+\Delta,\forall(\theta,\chi).

Reversing the argument using the second equality in (
5.3
), we see that (
5.4
) holds if and only if

(5.5)

\mathcal{G}^{i}_{\theta}=0,\quad i>\dim C_{\lambda}+\Delta-\dim C_{\mu},\forall\theta.

Since
\mathcal{G}^{i}_{\theta}
is a local system whose stalks calculate link cohomology for
K
-orbits, (
5.5
) holds if and only if

(5.6)

H^{i}({}^{K}L^{\lambda}_{x_{\mu}},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\theta})=0,\quad i>\dim C_{\lambda}+\Delta-\dim C_{\mu},\forall\theta.

By (
5.1
) and (
5.2
), we have

\dim C_{\lambda}-\frac{1}{2}(d_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda})=\frac{1}{2}(\dim K(\mathbb{R})-\dim{\mathbf{T}}^{c})-\operatorname{codim}_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\lambda}.

Therefore,

(5.7)

\displaystyle\dim C_{\lambda}+\Delta-\dim C_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\dim C_{\lambda}-\frac{1}{2}(d_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda})\right)-\left(\dim C_{\mu}-\frac{1}{2}(d_{\mu}+n_{\mu})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{codim}_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\mu}-\operatorname{codim}_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\lambda}=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\lambda}-\dim_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\mu}.

Let
i^{K}_{\lambda}:O^{K}_{\lambda}\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion of the
K
-orbits. Then
H^{*}({}^{K}L^{\lambda}_{x_{\mu}},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\theta})
is the stalk of
i^{K}_{\lambda*}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\theta}
at
x_{\mu}
. By
[
14
, Proposition 4.1]
(a result due to Beilinson and Bernstein),
i^{K}_{\lambda}
is an affine map. Therefore,
i^{K}_{\lambda*}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\theta}[\dim_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\lambda}]
is perverse, and the stalk of
i^{K}_{\lambda*}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\theta}
vanishes in degrees
>\dim_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\lambda}-\dim_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\mu}=\dim C_{\lambda}+\Delta-\dim C_{\mu}
(by (
5.7
)). This proves (
5.6
) and confirms (
3.4
).
∎

In this present setting, we further abbreviate the notation by putting
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}:={\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},X}
. We denote the
t
-structure on
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}}
given by the perversity function
p
defined in (
3.6
) by
({\mathcal{M}}^{\leq 0}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}},{\mathcal{M}}^{\geq 0}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
, and denote its heart by
{\mathcal{P}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
.

The general result in Proposition
3.14
then implies:

Corollary 5.6
.

Let
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
be the additive subcategory of
{\mathcal{P}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
consisting of free-monodromic tilting sheaves. Then:

(1)

For each
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
, there is up to isomorphism a unique indecomposable free-monodromic tilting sheaves
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
supported on the closure of
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
with
\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\lambda}\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}\cong\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}[p_{\lambda}]
.

(2)

The map
(\lambda,\chi)\mapsto\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
is a bijection between
\widetilde{I}
and the set of isomorphism classes of indecomposable objects in
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
.

(3)

Every object
\mathcal{T}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
is isomorphic to a finite direct sum of
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
, and the multiplicity of each
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
is an invariant of
\mathcal{T}
.

The Matsuki equivalence functors of
[
20
, Theorem 6.6 (2)]
are compatible with passing to the completion. By the general argument of 2.3 in
[
3
]
the following result is now also a formal consequence of
[
20
, Theorems 5.7 and 6.6]
.

Theorem 5.7
.

The Matsuki functors in
[
20
]
induces an equivalence

\widehat{D}^{b}_{K}(G/U)_{{\mathbf{T}}\textup{-mon}}\cong\widehat{D}^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}={\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}

It is a Ringel duality in the sense that it sends standard objects to costandard objects, and sends the projective cover of
{\operatorname{IC}}(O^{K}_{\lambda},{\mathbf{k}}_{\lambda,\chi})
to the indecomposable free-monodromic tilting sheaf
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
, for all
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
.

5.8.
Quasi-split case with split rank
1

We describe real group orbits and free-monodromic tilting sheaves for semisimple quasi-split real groups whose maximally split torus has rank
1
. The following is the list of such groups:

R_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\operatorname{SL}_{2},\quad R_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}{\rm PGL}_{2},\quad\operatorname{SL}_{2,\mathbb{R}},\quad{\rm PGL}_{2,\mathbb{R}},\quad\textup{SU}(2,1)\mbox{ and }\textup{PU}(2,1).

In the first two cases,
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
is identified with the completed version of the monodromic Hecke category (see Section
6.1
), for which free-monodromic tilting sheaves are described in
[
10
, Appendix C]
. Below we consider the rest of the cases.

1) Let
G=\operatorname{SL}_{2}
and
G_{\mathbb{R}}=\operatorname{SL}_{2,{\mathbb{R}}}
. Respectively, we have
K={\rm SO}_{2}
and
K_{\mathbb{R}}={\rm SO}_{2,\mathbb{R}}
. The flag variety is
X={\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{C}})\simeq S^{2}
and
\widetilde{X}=({\mathbb{C}}^{2}-0)/\mathbb{R}^{>0}\simeq S^{3}
.

There are three
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbits on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
: upper and lower half planes
\mathbb{H}_{+},\mathbb{H}_{-}
and the real flag variety
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}})=S^{1}
. We will label them by
I=\{+,-,0\}
. The stabilizer of a point in
\mathbb{H}_{+}
or
\mathbb{H}_{-}
is conjugated to
{\rm SO}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
and
n_{+}=n_{-}=0
. The constant local systems are the only
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant free-monodromic local systems on
\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{H}_{+})
and
\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{H}_{-})
. The stabilizer of a point in
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}})
is conjugated to the group of real points of the Borel subgroup
B({\mathbb{R}}):=\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
0&a^{-1}\end{pmatrix}|a\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times},b\in{\mathbb{R}}\}.
Since
\pi_{0}(B({\mathbb{R}}))={\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\pi_{1}(B({\mathbb{R}}))=0
, we have
n_{0}=1
and there are two indecomposable
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant free-monodromic local systems
\mathcal{L}_{0,\textup{triv}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{0,\operatorname{sgn}}
on the closed orbit, corresponding to the trivial and sign characters of
\pi_{0}(B({\mathbb{R}}))={\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
.

We see that
p_{+}=p_{-}=p_{0}=1
and the abelian category
{\mathcal{P}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
is (up to a shift) the category of constructible free-monodromic sheaves in the orbit stratification.

The category
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
is the direct sum of two blocks:
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{\circ}\oplus{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{\operatorname{sgn}}
. The block
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{\operatorname{sgn}}
is generated by the standard object
\widetilde{\Delta}_{0,\operatorname{sgn}}
(extension by zero of
\mathcal{L}_{0,\operatorname{sgn}}
), and the other standard objects
\widetilde{\Delta}_{+},\widetilde{\Delta}_{-}
and
\widetilde{\Delta}_{0,\textup{triv}}
generate the other block
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{\circ}
. Correspondingly
\mathcal{P}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}={\mathcal{P}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{\circ}\oplus{\mathcal{P}}^{\operatorname{sgn}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
.

Below we focus on the block
{\mathcal{P}}^{\circ}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
. We represent an object in
\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
by the following diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 10.34723pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.34723pt\raise 2) Let
G={\rm PGL}_{2}
and
G_{\mathbb{R}}={\rm PGL}_{2,{\mathbb{R}}}
. Respectively, we have
K=\mathrm{O}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})/\{\pm I_{2}\}
and
K_{\mathbb{R}}=\mathrm{O}_{2,{\mathbb{R}}}/\{\pm I_{2}\}
. The flag variety is
X={\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{C}})\simeq S^{2}
and
\widetilde{X}=({\mathbb{C}}^{2}-0)/{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{3}({\mathbb{R}})
.

There are two
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbits on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
: the real flag variety
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}})
and its complement
\mathbb{H}_{\pm}=\mathbb{H}_{+}\coprod\mathbb{H}_{-}
. We label them by
I=\{0,h\}
. The stabilizer of a point in
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}})
is conjugated to the group of real points of the Borel subgroup
B({\mathbb{R}}):=\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
0&c\end{pmatrix}|a,c\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times},b\in{\mathbb{R}}\}/\{\begin{pmatrix}d&0\\
0&d\end{pmatrix}|d\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}\}.
Since
\pi_{0}(B({\mathbb{R}}))={\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\pi_{1}(B({\mathbb{R}}))=0
, we have
n_{0}=1
, there are two indecomposable
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant free-monodromic local systems
\mathcal{L}_{0,\textup{triv}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{0,\operatorname{sgn}}
on the closed orbit corresponding to the trivial and sign characters of
\pi_{0}(B({\mathbb{R}}))
. The stabilizer of a point in
\mathbb{H}
is conjugated to
{\rm SO}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})/\{\pm 1\}
and
n_{h}=0
.

We see that
p_{h}=p_{0}=1
and the abelian category
{\mathcal{P}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
is (up to a shift) the category of constructible free-monodromic sheaves in the orbit stratification. We represent an object of this category by the following diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.63545pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.63545pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V_{\textup{triv}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}{{}{{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}{{}}}}}}}}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern where
V_{h}
is the stalk on
\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{H}_{\pm})
,
V_{\textup{triv}}
and
V_{\operatorname{sgn}}
are eigenspaces of the stalk at a point of
\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))
corresponding, respectively, to the trivial and sign characters of
\pi_{0}(B({\mathbb{R}}))={\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
. The maps
m_{\textup{triv}}:V_{\textup{triv}}\to V_{\textup{triv}}
and
m_{\operatorname{sgn}}:V_{\operatorname{sgn}}\to V_{\operatorname{sgn}}
are the pro-unipotent monodromy automorphisms along the fibers of
\pi
, and
s_{\textup{triv}}\colon\operatorname{Coker}(m_{\textup{triv}}-1)\to V_{h}
,
s_{\operatorname{sgn}}\colon\operatorname{Coker}(m_{\operatorname{sgn}}-1)\to V_{h}
are the cospecialization maps. In these terms we have

0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right).

3) Let
G=\operatorname{SL}_{3}
and
G_{\mathbb{R}}=\textup{SU}(2,1)
or
G={\rm PGL}_{3}
and
G_{\mathbb{R}}=\textup{PU}(2,1)
. These cases have an identical geometry and, so we will discuss only the first one.

The six orbits are defined by the signature of the hermitian form on each vector space in the flag. Namely, we put
I=\{0|+0,0|+-,+|+0,-|+-,+|+-,+|++\}
, where to the left of
|
we have a signature of the hermitian form restricted to
V_{1}
and to the right restricted on
V_{2}
for a given flag
V_{1}\subset V_{2}
. The poset of the orbits is

(5.8)

\textstyle{0|+0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0|+-\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{+|+0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{-|+-}
\textstyle{+|+-}
\textstyle{+|++.}

We have

n_{0|+0}=n_{0|+-}=n_{+|+0}=1,n_{-|+-}=n_{+|+-}=n_{+|++}=0

and

d_{0|+0}=3,d_{0|+-}=d_{+|+0}=5,d_{-|+-}=d_{+|+-}=d_{+|++}=6.

The stabilizer of a point in each orbit is connected.

The local geometry of the orbits in a transversal slice to a point in the closed orbit can be modeled as follows. Consider the space
\mathbb{R}^{3}
together with two smooth surfaces
S_{0|+-}
and
S_{+|+0}
that are tangent to each other at their only intersection point
S_{0|+0}
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Then the complement of
S_{0|+-}\cup S_{+|+0}
has 3 connected components
S_{-|+-},S_{+|+-}
and
S_{+|++}
, such that the closure relation of these strata is given by (
5.8
).

From the local model, we see that the closures
\overline{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{0|+-}
and
\overline{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{+|+0}
are smooth and are tangent to each other along the closed orbit
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{0|+0}
. We conclude that
\nabla_{-|+-}
and
\nabla_{+|++}
are shifted (in degree
-3
) constant sheaves on the closures
\overline{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{-|+-}
and
\overline{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{+|++}
.
For
\nabla_{+|+-}
, if we restrict to the complement of the closed orbit
\overline{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{+|+-}\backslash O^{\mathbb{R}}_{0|+0}
, it is the constant sheaf in degree
-3
. Along the closed orbit
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{0|+0}
its stalks are one dimensional at degree
-3
and one dimensional at degree
-2
.

We have
{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{0|+0}={\widetilde{\nabla}}_{0|+0}={\mathcal{T}}_{0|+0}
. Since the stratifications of
S_{0|+-}
and
S_{+|+0}
are by a smooth point
S_{0|+0}
and its complement we conclude that the free-monodromic tilting objects
{\mathcal{T}}_{0|+-}
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{+|+0}
behave fiberwise over the closed orbit as the tilting sheaves in the complex group case (see
[
10
, Appendix C]
).

For
\lambda\in I
put
{\mathcal{C}}_{\lambda}:=\ker({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}\to\widetilde{i}_{0|+0,*}\widetilde{i}_{0|+0}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda})\in\mathcal{P}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
. It follows that we have distinguished triangles:

(5.9)

{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{-|+-}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{-|+-}\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{0|+-}\to,

(5.10)

{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{+|++}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{+|++}\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{+|+0}\to,

(5.11)

{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{+|+-}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{+|+-}\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{0|+-}\oplus{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{+|+0}\to.

These calculations are local near a point of the orbits
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{0|+-}
and
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{+|+0}
, and are identical to the case of
\operatorname{SL}_{2,\mathbb{R}}
.

6.
Hecke action

In this section we study the action of the Hecke category on
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
, and the behavior of tilting sheaves under the action.

6.1.
Hecke category

If we view the complex group
G
as a real group, i.e.,
R_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}G
, its complexification
(R_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}G)_{\mathbb{C}}
is
G\times G^{\prime}
, where
G^{\prime}
is
G
equipped with the opposite complex structure. Similarly, the flag variety of
(R_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}G)_{\mathbb{C}}
is
X\times X^{\prime}
(where
X^{\prime}
is
X
equipped with the opposite complex structure). We can identify
{\mathcal{M}}_{R_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}G}
with the (free-monodromic)
Hecke category

{\mathcal{H}}_{G}=\widehat{D}^{b}_{G}(\widetilde{X}\times\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}.

It is equivalent to the triangulated category
\widehat{D}^{b}_{U}(\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}
, which was studied in
[
10
]
and
[
8
]
.

Note that in this case we have
\widetilde{I}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}I=W
, where
W
is the Weyl group of
G
, which we consider equipped with the Bruhat order. For
w\in W
let
X^{2}_{w}\subset X^{2}
be the corresponding
G
-orbit, and
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{w}
be its preimage in
\widetilde{X}^{2}
. In particular, for
w=e
(identity in
W
),
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}
is the preimage of the diagonal
\Delta(X)\subset X^{2}
in
\widetilde{X}^{2}
.

For any
w\in W
, we have that
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{w}\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
is the graph of the
w
-action on
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
. In particular,
n_{w}=r=\dim{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
. Also
\dim_{\mathbb{R}}X^{2}_{w}=2(\dim_{\mathbb{C}}X+\ell(w))
. The perversity function is

p_{w}=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}X+\ell(w)+\lfloor r/2\rfloor
.
We will use a slightly different perversity function
p^{\prime}:W\to{\mathbb{Z}}

p^{\prime}_{w}=\ell(w)+r

to define a
t
-structure on
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
. Denote its heart by
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}^{\heartsuit}
.

We have the free-monodromic standard objects and costandard objects
\widetilde{\Delta}_{w},\widetilde{\nabla}_{w}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\heartsuit}_{G}
being the
!
- and
*
- extensions of the free-monodromic
G
-equivariant local system on
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{w}
placed in degree
-p^{\prime}_{w}
. In particular, we denote
{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{e}={\widetilde{\nabla}}_{e}
by
\widetilde{\delta}
. This is a free-monodromic local system on the closed stratum
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}
placed in degree
-r
. If we identify
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}
with
\widetilde{X}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
such that
(\widetilde{x},t)\in\widetilde{X}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
corresponds to
(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{x}t)
, then
\widetilde{\delta}
is the extension by zero of
{\mathbf{k}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}[r]
on
\widetilde{X}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
.

We define a monoidal structure on
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
by convolution. Let
\operatorname{pr}_{ij}:\widetilde{X}^{3}\to\widetilde{X}^{2}
be the projection to the
(i,j)
-factors. The convolution product on
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
is defined using

\mathcal{K}_{1}\star\mathcal{K}_{2}:=\operatorname{pr}_{13*}(\operatorname{pr}_{12}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{1}\otimes\operatorname{pr}_{23}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{2})

for
\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
. This equips
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
with a monoidal structure with monoidal unit
\widetilde{\delta}
. If we identify
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
with
\widehat{D}^{b}_{U}(\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}
, this monoidal structure is the same as the one defined in
[
10
, Section 4.3]
and
[
8
, Section 7]
.

6.2.
Hecke action

We define a right action of
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
on
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
as follows.

For a
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant,
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-monodromic sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
on
\widetilde{X}
and an
U
-equivariant
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-monodromic sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
on
\widetilde{X}
we have a
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant,
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-monodromic sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
on the fibered product
G\times^{{\mathbf{T}}^{>0}U}\widetilde{X}
. We push it forward with respect to the action map
G\times^{{\mathbf{T}}^{>0}U}\widetilde{X}\to\widetilde{X}
to define a
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant,
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-monodromic sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
on
\widetilde{X}
. As in Lemma 4.3.1 in
[
10
]
or Lemma 7.4 in
[
8
]
the operation is compatible with passing to the limit and we have an action of the monoidal category
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
on
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
:

\star\colon{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}\times{\mathcal{H}}_{G}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
and
\mathcal{K}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
. Consider the two projections

\operatorname{pr}_{1},\operatorname{pr}_{2}:\widetilde{X}\times\widetilde{X}\to\widetilde{X}.

Define

\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{K}=\operatorname{pr}_{2*}(\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{K}).

More precisely, we start with the above definition for the uncompleted monodromic categories and pass to the limit as in
[
10
, Lemma 4.3.1]
or
[
8
, Lemma 7.4]
. This defines a right action of the Hecke category
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
on
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
:

\star\colon{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}\times{\mathcal{H}}_{G}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}.

We now compute the action of some (co)standard objects in
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
on some (co)standard objects of
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
. The calculation is parallel to
[
18
, Lemma 3.5]
(which is for
K
-orbits on
X
).

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
and
s\in{\mathbf{W}}
be a simple reflection. Let
\alpha_{s}
be the corresponding simple root in the based root system
\Phi_{\lambda}
. We will use the notations from Lemma
4.12
and Section
4.16
.

Suppose
O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
is closed inside
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})
. Then we have the following cases:

(1)

If
\alpha_{s}
is a complex root and
\sigma\alpha>0
. Let
\mu=\lambda\cdot s>\lambda
be such that
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}=\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}})-O_{\lambda}^{{\mathbb{R}}}
. Then there is a canonical isomorphism
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})\cong\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\mu})
, through which we may view
\chi
as a character of
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\mu})
. We then have

{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\chi},\quad{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu,\chi},

{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi},\quad{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}.

(2)

If
\alpha_{s}
is a compact imaginary root. Then

{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}[-1],\quad{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}[1],

{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}[-1],\quad{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}[1].

(3)

If
\alpha_{s}
is a real root. Then exactly one of the following options holds:

•

The local system
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}
on
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
does not extend to
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})
.
5
5

5

This happens if and only if the composition
\{\pm 1\}=\pi_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\times})\xrightarrow{\alpha^{\vee}_{s}}\pi_{0}(T(\mathbb{R}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})\xrightarrow{\chi}{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
is nontrivial, where the
\sigma
-stable maximal torus
T
is such that
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
is attached to
T
.
Then

{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi},

{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}.

•

The local system
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}
on
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
extends (uniquely) to a
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant local system
\widetilde{{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}}
on
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})
, and
\alpha_{s}
is type II real.

Let
\mu>\lambda
be such that
O_{\mu}^{\mathbb{R}}=\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}})-O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\psi}
be the restrictions of
\widetilde{{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}}
to
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}
. There are distinguished triangles

(6.1)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\psi}\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{s}\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s}\to,

(6.2)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s}\to{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\to{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu,\psi}\to,

(6.3)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\psi}[-1]\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\psi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\oplus{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s}\to,

(6.4)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}\oplus{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s}\to{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu,\psi}\star{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{s}\to{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu,\psi}[1]\to.

•

The local system
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}
on
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
extends (uniquely) to a
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant local system
\widetilde{{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}}
on
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})
, and
\alpha_{s}
is type I real.

Let
\mu^{+},\mu^{-}>\lambda
be such that
O_{\mu^{+}}^{\mathbb{R}}\coprod O_{\mu^{-}}^{\mathbb{R}}=\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}})-O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu^{+},\psi^{+}}
and
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu^{-},\psi^{-}}
be the restrictions of
\widetilde{{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}}
to
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu^{+}}
and
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu^{-}}
. There are distinguished triangles

(6.5)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu^{+},\psi^{+}}\oplus{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu^{-}\psi^{-}}\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{s}\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\to,

(6.6)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu^{\mp},\psi^{\mp}}[-1]\to\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu^{\pm},\psi^{\pm}}\star\widetilde{\nabla}_{s}\to\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\to,

(6.7)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}\to{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\to{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu^{+},\psi^{+}}\oplus{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu^{-},\psi^{-}}\to,

(6.8)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nabla}_{\lambda,\chi}\to\widetilde{\nabla}_{\mu^{\pm},\psi^{\pm}}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s}\to\widetilde{\nabla}_{\mu^{\mp},\psi^{\mp}}[1]\to

Proof.

In the proof we will compute the non-monodromic versions
\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s}
. Here
\Delta_{w}\in D_{G}(X\times X)
is the
!
-extension of
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}[\ell(w)]
on the
G
-orbit
X^{2}_{w}
, and for
\mathcal{F}\in D_{G(\mathbb{R})}(X)
,
\mathcal{K}\in D_{G}(X\times X)
,
\mathcal{F}\overline{\star}\mathcal{K}:=\operatorname{pr}_{2*}(\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{K})
. Note the degree shift for
\Delta_{w}
differs from the one for
\widetilde{\Delta}_{w}
by
\rho=\dim T^{c}
. Using Lemma
3.9
we have

(6.9)

\pi_{\dagger}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s})\cong\pi_{\dagger}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi})\overline{\star}\Delta_{s}\cong\Lambda_{\lambda,\bullet}\otimes(\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s}),\quad\forall(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}.

In order to recover stalks of
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s}
from that of
\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s}
, we will use the following simple observation which follows from Corollary
3.7
. Suppose
(\mu,\psi)\in\widetilde{I}
,
\mathcal{F}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
:

(6.10)

If
i_{\mu}^{*}\pi_{\dagger}\mathcal{F}\cong\Lambda_{\mu,\bullet}\otimes{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\psi}\otimes V
as a free
\Lambda_{\mu,\bullet}
-module (where
V
is a complex of
{\mathbf{k}}
-vector spaces), then
\widetilde{i}_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}\cong\mathcal{L}_{\mu,\psi}\otimes V
.

Choose a point
x\in O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
corresponding to a Borel
B_{x}
, and let
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s}=\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(x)
. Let
P_{x}
be the parabolic containing
B_{x}
with negative root
-\alpha_{s}
. Note that
G(\mathbb{R})\cap P_{x}
acts on
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s}
. The stalk of
\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s}
at
y\in\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s}
is

(6.11)

i_{y}^{*}(\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s})\cong\begin{cases}H_{c}^{*}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}\cap\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s}-\{y\},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi})[p_{\lambda}+1]&y\in O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda};\\
H^{*}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}\cap\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi})[p_{\lambda}+1]&y\notin O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}.\end{cases}

Moreover, in the case
y\notin O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
(corresponding to a Borel
B_{y}
), the inclusion
B_{y}\hookrightarrow P_{x}
induces an isomorphism
\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R})\cap B_{y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R})\cap P_{x})
, and the isomorphism above is equivariant under the actions of
\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R})\cap B_{y})
on the left and
\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R})\cap P_{x})
on the right via this isomorphism.

Moreover, we have the triangle
\delta\to\Delta_{s}\to{\operatorname{IC}}_{s}\to
in
D_{G}(X\times X)
gives a distinguished triangle

(6.12)

\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\to\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s}\to\pi_{s}^{*}\pi_{s*}\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}[1]\to

(1) In this case,
O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}\cap\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s}=\{x\}
. By (
6.11
),
\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s}
has nonzero (
1
-dimensional) stalk only at
y\in\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s}-\{x\}
, and
\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R})\cap B_{y})
acts via
\chi
via the natural isomorphisms
\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R})\cap B_{y})\cong\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R})\cap P_{x})\cong\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R})\cap B_{x})=\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})
. We conclude that

(6.13)

\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\star\Delta_{s}\cong\Delta_{\mu,\chi}

By (
6.10
), this implies
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s}\cong\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu,\chi}
. The formula
{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu,\chi}
follows from the same argument from
\nabla_{\lambda,\chi}\star\nabla_{s}\cong\nabla_{\mu,\chi}
, which follows from (
6.13
) by Verdier duality. The third and the forth isomorphisms now follow from the fact that

(6.14)

\widetilde{\Delta}_{s}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s}\cong\widetilde{\delta}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{G}

(see
[
8
, Lemma 7.7 (1)]
).

(2) In this case
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s}\subset O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
. From (
6.11
) we see that
\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s}
has stalk
H^{*}_{c}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s}\backslash\{y\},{\mathbf{k}})[p_{\lambda}+1]\cong{\mathbf{k}}[p_{\lambda}-1]
at every point
y\in\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s}
. Moreover, the first map in (
6.12
) induces an isomorphism on degree
1-p_{\lambda}
stalks, and the monodromy on
\pi_{s}^{*}\pi_{s*}\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}
is given by the same character
\chi
, we conclude that the same is true for the monodromy of
\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s}
, i.e.,
\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s}\cong\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}[-1]
. By (
6.10
), this implies
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s}\cong\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu,\chi}[-1]
. The other formulae are proved similarly.

(3) Fix a
\sigma
-stable maximal torus
T\subset B_{x}
. Let
L
be the Levi subgroup of
G
generated by
T
and root spaces
\pm\alpha_{s}
. Then
L
is
\sigma
-stable with real form
L_{\mathbb{R}}=L^{\sigma}
. We have an
L
-equivariant isomorphism
\widetilde{X}_{L}\cong\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s})
. Therefore we may assume
G=L
. Note that the derived group of
L_{\mathbb{R}}
is either
\operatorname{SL}_{2,\mathbb{R}}
or
{\rm PGL}_{2,\mathbb{R}}
. The flag variety
X_{L}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
is defined over
\mathbb{R}
with real points the real projective line
\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R})=O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
.

In the first option,
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}|_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}
has nontrivial monodromy, hence
\pi_{s*}\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}=0
. By (
6.12
) we have an isomorphism
\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s}
. By (
6.9
), we conclude that
{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{s}\cong{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}
. The other isomorphisms follow from this one by Verdier duality and (
6.14
).

In the second option, we have
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}=\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s}-\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
. We first compute
\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{*}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s})
. Using (
6.11
), the stalk of
\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s}
at a point
y\in\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
is
H^{*}_{c}(\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R})-\{y\},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi})[p_{\lambda}+1]\cong{\mathbf{k}}[p_{\lambda}]
. Moreover, the action of
\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R})\cap B_{y})
on
H^{*}_{c}(\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R})-\{y\},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi})
is
\chi
twisted by the sign character
\operatorname{sgn}_{s}
(see (
4.3
)) through which
\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R})\cap B_{y})
acts on
H^{1}_{c}(\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R})-\{y\})\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R}))
. We conclude that

(6.15)

i^{*}_{\lambda}(\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s})\cong{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi\otimes\operatorname{sgn}_{s}}[p_{\lambda}]

By (
6.10
) we conclude that

(6.16)

\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{*}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s})\cong\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi\otimes\operatorname{sgn}_{s}}[p_{\lambda}]=\mathcal{L}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s}[p_{\lambda}].

Next we show that

(6.17)

\widetilde{i}_{\mu}^{*}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s})\cong\mathcal{L}_{\mu,\psi}[p_{\mu}].

For this, computing
i^{*}_{\mu}(\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s})
as a local system is not enough, since we need to keep track of the
\Lambda_{\mu,\bullet}
-action in order to recover
\widetilde{i}_{\mu}^{*}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s})
. Nevertheless we first use (
6.11
) to see the stalk of
\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s}
at a point
y\in\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s}-\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
is equal to
H^{*}(\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R}),{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi})[p_{\lambda}+1]
. Using the long exact sequence attached to the triangle
j_{!}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\mu,\psi}\to\widetilde{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}\to i_{!}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}\to
on
\pi^{-1}_{s}\pi_{s}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})
, we see that the action of
\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R})\cap B_{y})\cong\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\mu})
on the stalk
i_{y}^{*}(\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\Delta_{s})
is via
\psi
. To show (
6.17
), it remains to show that

(6.18)

\widetilde{i}_{\mu}^{*}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s})
is free-monodromic of rank 1 in degree
-p_{\mu}=-p_{\lambda}
.

Let
A=(ZG)^{\circ}
, then we have an isogeny
A\times\operatorname{SL}_{2}\to G
defined over
\mathbb{R}
with kernel either trivial or of order two. It is then sufficient to prove (
6.18
) for
A\times\operatorname{SL}_{2}
(by pullback from
\widetilde{X}
), and eventually for
G_{\mathbb{R}}=\operatorname{SL}_{2,\mathbb{R}}
(just for the statement (
6.18
)). In the rest of this paragraph we assume
G=\operatorname{SL}_{2}
. In this case, the statement (
6.18
) becomes

(6.19)

For
\widetilde{y}\in\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}-\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R}))
,
i_{\widetilde{y}}^{*}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\textup{triv}}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s})\cong{\mathbf{k}}[1]
.

We identify
G/U\cong\mathbb{A}^{2}\backslash\{0\}
hence
G/UT^{>0}\cong S^{3}
(unit sphere in
\mathbb{C}^{2}
). The preimage
\widetilde{O}_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}\subset S^{3}
consists of
(z_{1},z_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
with
|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}=1
and
z_{1}/z_{2}\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}
. It is easy to see that
\widetilde{O}_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
is a
2
-dimensional torus. Let
\widetilde{y}=(y_{1},y_{2})\in S^{3}
. Then

(6.20)

i_{\widetilde{y}}^{*}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s})\cong H^{*}(\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda},\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}[1]\otimes\epsilon^{*}\mathcal{L}[2]).

Here
\mathcal{L}
is the rank one free-monodromic local system on
S^{1}
, and
\epsilon:\widetilde{O}_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}\to S^{1}
sends
(z_{1},z_{2})
to
(z_{1}y_{2}-z_{2}y_{1})/|z_{1}y_{2}-z_{2}y_{1}|
, and
\epsilon^{*}\mathcal{L}[2]
is the contribution of
\widetilde{\Delta}_{s}
to the the fiber of the convolution. Consider the
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant embedding
\theta:S^{1}\to\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
sending
u+iv\mapsto(u,v)
. Then
\theta^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}
is trivial since
G(\mathbb{R})
acts transitively on
S^{1}
. However, calculation shows that
\epsilon\circ\theta:S^{1}\to S^{1}
is a homeomorphism. These facts combined imply that
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}\otimes\epsilon^{*}\mathcal{L}
is a rank one free-monodromic local system on the
2
-dimensional torus
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
. Using (
6.20
) we see that (
6.19
) holds. Now (
6.17
) is proved.

Combining (
6.16
) and (
6.17
) we get the distinguished triangle (
6.1
).

The same argument for showing (
6.16
) shows

(6.21)

\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\nabla}_{s})\cong\mathcal{L}_{\lambda,\chi}[p_{\lambda}+1].

Here we are using that for
y\in\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
,
i_{y}^{*}(\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\star\nabla_{s})\cong H^{*}(\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R})-\{y\},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi})[p_{\lambda}+1]\cong{\mathbf{k}}[p_{\lambda}+1]
. On the other hand, we have

\widetilde{i}_{\mu}^{*}({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{s})\cong\widetilde{i}_{\mu}^{*}({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}),

which is isomorphic to
\mathcal{L}_{\mu,\psi}[p_{\mu}]
by (
6.17
). This together with (
6.21
) imply a distinguished triangle

\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu,\psi}\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}[1]\to

Replacing
(\lambda,\chi)
by
(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s
, and shift by
[-1]
, we get

(6.22)

\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu,\psi}[-1]\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}[-1]\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s}\to

Now we convolve (
6.1
) with
{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}
, rotating it and using (
6.14
) we obtain a distinguished triangle

{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}[-1]\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\psi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s}\to.

Combined with (
6.22
), observing that there is nontrivial nonzero extension between
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
and
\widetilde{\Delta}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s}
, we get the asserted distinguished triangle (
6.3
).

To prove (
6.2
), we take the Verdier dual of (
6.15
) to get
i_{\lambda}^{!}(\nabla_{\lambda,\chi}\overline{\star}\nabla_{s})\cong{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s}[p_{\lambda}]
. Using (
6.10
) we get
\widetilde{i}_{\lambda}^{!}(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\nabla}_{s})\cong\mathcal{L}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s}[p_{\lambda}]
. The calculation of
\widetilde{i}_{\mu}^{!}(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\nabla}_{s})
boils down to the same thing as
\widetilde{i}_{\mu}^{*}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s})
, and we have
\widetilde{i}_{\mu}^{!}(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\nabla}_{s})\cong\mathcal{L}_{\mu,\psi}[p_{\mu}]
. Combining these facts we get the distinguished triangle (
6.2
). Then the same deduction from (
6.1
) to (
6.3
) allows us to deduce (
6.4
) from (
6.2
).

Finally consider the third option. In this case
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu^{+}}
and
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu^{+}}
are the two hemispheres of
\mathbb{P}^{1}-\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
, which we denote by
\mathbb{H}^{+}
and
\mathbb{H}^{-}
. The calculations made for (
6.1
) shows (
6.5
); the argument for (
6.3
) gives a distinguished triangle

\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu^{+},\psi^{+}}[-1]\oplus\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu^{-},\psi^{-}}[-1]\to\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu^{+},\psi^{+}}\star\widetilde{\nabla}_{s}\oplus\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu^{-},\psi^{-}}\star\widetilde{\nabla}_{s}\to\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}^{\oplus 2}\to.

To deduce (
6.6
), it remains to show that
\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\mu^{+}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu^{+},\psi^{+}}\star\widetilde{\nabla}_{s})=0
, or
i_{\mu^{+}}^{*}(\Delta_{\mu^{+},\psi^{+}}\overline{\star}\nabla_{s})=0
. For
y\in\mathbb{H}^{+}
,
i^{*}_{y}(\Delta_{\mu^{+},\psi^{+}}\overline{\star}\nabla_{s})\cong H^{*}_{c}(\mathbb{H}^{+},j_{*}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}})[2]
where
j:\mathbb{H}^{+}-\{y\}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}^{+}
is the inclusion. The vanishing of
H^{*}_{c}(\mathbb{H}^{+},j_{*}{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}})
is clear.

The proofs of (
6.7
) and (
6.8
) are similar to those of (
6.2
) and (
6.4
). We omit details.
∎

Let
\mathrm{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})\subset{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
be the additive subcategory of free-monodromic tilting objects. The category
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
is closed under convolution. See
[
10
, Proposition 4.3.4]
and
[
8
, Lemma 7.8, Remark 7.9]
. Hence
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
(as an additive category) inherits a monoidal structure from
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
.

Proposition 6.4
.

For
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
in
\mathrm{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{2}
in
\mathrm{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
the convolution product
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{2}
is in
\mathrm{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
. In other words,
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
is a module category for
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
under convolution.

Proof.

Every object
{\mathcal{T}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
is a direct summand of successive convolutions of
{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
for simple reflections
s\in{\mathbf{W}}
(see
[
10
, Corollary 5.2.3]
and
[
8
, Remark 7.9]
). Hence, it is sufficient to assume that
{\mathcal{T}}_{2}={\mathcal{T}}_{s}
. We start by checking that for any
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
, the convolution product
{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
is a successive extension of the objects of the form
{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\psi}[-n]
with
(\mu,\psi)\in\widetilde{I}
and
n\geq 0
.

If
\lambda
and
s
are in position of one of the options of Lemma
6.3
we use the distinguished triangle
\tilde{\delta}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\to{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{s_{\alpha}}\to
and the calculation of
{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s}
in Lemma
6.3

to obtain needed filtration. Otherwise, we consider the distinguished triangle
{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\to\tilde{\delta}\to
and use the calculation of
{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s}
in Lemma
6.3
to conclude.

It follows that
{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
is a successive extension of the objects of the form
{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\psi}[-n]
,
n\geq 0
. By considering a standard filtration on
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
, we see that the same is true for
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
. In particular,

(6.23)

For each
\mu\in I
, the restriction
\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\mu}({\mathcal{T}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s})
is a bounded complex of free-monodromic local systems concentrated in degrees
\geq-p_{\mu}
.

Similarly, using the calculations in Lemma
6.3
, the convolution product
{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
is a successive extension of the objects of the form
{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu,\psi}[\geq 0]
and
(\mu,\psi)\in\widetilde{I}
. By considering the costandard filtration on
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
, we see that the same is true for
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
. In particular,

(6.24)

For each
\mu\in I
,
\widetilde{i}_{\mu}^{!}({\mathcal{T}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s})
is in degrees
\leq-p_{\mu}
.

By Lemma
3.10
(1),
\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\mu}{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu^{\prime},\psi}
lies in degrees
\leq-p_{\mu}
. Since
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
is a successive extension of
{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu^{\prime},\psi}[\geq 0]
,
\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\mu}({\mathcal{T}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s})
also lies in degrees
\leq-p_{\mu}
. Combined with (
6.23
), we conclude that
\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\mu}({\mathcal{T}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s})
is concentrated in degree
-p_{\mu}
and is free-monodromic.

By Lemma
3.10
(2),
\widetilde{i}^{!}_{\mu}\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu^{\prime},\psi}
is a complex of free-monodromic local systems in degrees
\geq-p_{\mu}
. Since
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
is a successive extension of
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu^{\prime},\psi}[\leq 0]
,
\widetilde{i}^{!}_{\mu}({\mathcal{T}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s})
is also a complex of free-monodromic local systems in degrees
\geq-p_{\mu}
. Combined with (
6.24
), we conclude that
\widetilde{i}^{!}_{\mu}({\mathcal{T}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s})
is concentrated in degree
-p_{\mu}
and is free-monodromic.

Combining the last two paragraphs, we conclude that
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
is a free-monodromic tilting sheaf.

∎

Lemma 6.5
.

Recall the assumptions and notations of Lemma
6.3
. Then

(1)

If
\alpha_{s}
is complex and
\sigma\alpha_{s}>0
, then
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
contains
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda\cdot s,\chi}
as a direct summand with multiplicity one.

(2)

If
\alpha_{s}
is real and
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\lambda,\chi}
extends to a
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant local system on
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})
. Then

•

If
\alpha_{s}
is type II real, then
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
contains
{\mathcal{T}}_{\mu,\psi}
as a direct summand with multiplicity one.

•

If
\alpha_{s}
is type I real, then
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
contains
{\mathcal{T}}_{\mu^{+},\psi^{+}}\oplus{\mathcal{T}}_{\mu^{-},\psi^{-}}
as a direct summand with multiplicity one.

Proof.

Let
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}=\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})-O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
(which is a union of two orbits in type I real case), and let
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}
be its preimage under
\pi
. Let
\widetilde{i}_{\mu}:\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}
be the inclusion. Then
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}
is the open stratum in the support of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
. By support considerations, we have

\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\mu}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\chi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s})\cong\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\mu}(\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s}).

By Lemma
6.3
, the above is
\mathcal{L}_{\mu,\chi}[p_{\mu}]
in case (1),
\mathcal{L}_{\mu,\psi}[p_{\mu}]
in case (2) type II, and
\mathcal{L}_{\mu^{+},\psi^{+}}[p_{\mu^{+}}]\oplus\mathcal{L}_{\mu^{-},\psi^{-}}[p_{\mu^{-}}]
in case (2) type I. The conclusion follows.
∎

The Hecke action on
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
enjoys the following self-adjunction property.

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
s\in W
be a simple reflection and
{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
the corresponding tilting object of
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
. Then the convolution endo-functor
-\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
on
\mathrm{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
is self-adjoint. Namely for
{\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2}
in
\mathrm{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
we have a canonical isomorphism functorial in
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}

\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s},{\mathcal{T}}_{2})\cong\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}).

Proof.

To construct a unit and counit of the adjunction it suffices to construct maps
u\colon\tilde{\delta}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
and
c\colon{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\to\tilde{\delta}
in
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
, such that both compositions

(6.25)

\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u\star{\rm id}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\star c}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{s}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\star u}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c\star{\rm id}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{s}}

are equality to the identity of
{\mathcal{T}}_{s}
.

It is known that the Soergel’s functor
\mathbb{V}
(recalled in Section
8.6
) provides a fully-faithful embedding of
\mathrm{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
into the category of Soergel bimodules (i.e.
[
8
, Proposition 11.2]
in the present setting). It is thus sufficient to construct
u
and
c
in the category of Soergel bimodules, where we have
\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{s})={\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}
. Note that
{\mathcal{R}}^{s=-1}
, as an
{\mathcal{R}}^{s}
-bimodule, is isomorphic to the regular bimodule. Let us fix an element
x_{s}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{s=-1}
such that multiplication by
x_{s}
gives an isomorphism
{\mathcal{R}}^{s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathcal{R}}^{s=-1}
of
{\mathcal{R}}^{s}
-bimodules. We get a splitting
{\mathcal{R}}={\mathcal{R}}^{s}\oplus x_{s}{\mathcal{R}}^{s}
as
{\mathcal{R}}^{s}
-bimodules.

We then have a splitting

\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{s}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s})={\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}={\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}({\mathcal{R}}^{s}\oplus x_{s}{\mathcal{R}}^{s})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}=

={\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}^{s}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}\oplus{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}x_{s}{\mathcal{R}}^{s}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}.

We put
c\colon{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}\to{\mathcal{R}}
for a composition of the projection onto the second summand
{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}x_{s}{\mathcal{R}}^{s}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}\simeq{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}
and the multiplication map
{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}\to{\mathcal{R}}
. We put
u\colon{\mathcal{R}}\to{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}
for the composition of the map
{\mathcal{R}}\to{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}
given by
1\mapsto x_{s}\otimes 1+1\otimes x_{s}
and the inclusion of the first summand
{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}^{s}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}\simeq{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}.
The verification of the adjunction identities is now straightforward.

∎

6.7.
Generation

The following generation property for the
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
-module
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
will play an important role later.

Recall there is a unique closed
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
in
X
and its preimage
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}:=\pi^{-1}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})
in
\widetilde{X}
.

Proposition 6.8
.

The category
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
is generated under taking direct sums and summands and applying the convolution action of
\mathrm{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
by the free-monodromic local systems supported on
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
.

Proof.

Let
\textup{Tilt}^{\prime}\subset\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
be the full subcategory generated under taking direct sums and summands and the convolution action of
\mathrm{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
by the free-monodromic local systems supported on
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
. Suppose for purpose of contradiction that
\textup{Tilt}^{\prime}
is not the whole
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
, let
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
be such that
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\chi}\notin\textup{Tilt}^{\prime}
and that
\dim O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
is minimal among such
(\lambda,\chi)
.

Let
B
be a Borel corresponding to a point in
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
and let
{\mathfrak{b}}
be its Lie algebra and
{\mathfrak{t}}\subset{\mathfrak{b}}
its
\sigma
-invariant subtorus. Let
\alpha
be a simple root of
{\mathfrak{t}}
in
{\mathfrak{g}}
positive with respect to
{\mathfrak{b}}
. Lemma
6.5
implies that if
\alpha
is noncompact imaginary or
\alpha
is complex and
\sigma\alpha
is negative, there is
(\mu,\psi)\in\widetilde{I}
with
\mu<\lambda
such that
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\chi}
is a direct summand in
{\mathcal{T}}_{\mu,\psi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{s_{\alpha}}
. This would contradict the minimality assumption.

From the above we conclude that any simple root
\alpha
of
({\mathfrak{b}},{\mathfrak{t}})
either satisfies
\sigma\alpha>0
, or
\alpha
is compact imaginary. By Lemma
6.9
below, in this situation
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
is closed, therefore
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
is already in
\textup{Tilt}^{\prime}
. We arrived at a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
\lambda\in I
. The orbit
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
is closed if and only if any simple root
\alpha\in\Phi_{\lambda}
either satisfies
\sigma\alpha>0
, or is compact imaginary.

Proof.

The ‘‘only if’’ part follows from Lemma
4.12
as the intersections with the
\alpha
-lines has to be closed subvarieties of
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
.

Let us proof the ‘‘if’’ part. Under the Matsuki correspondence, the closed
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit corresponds to the unique open
K
-orbit in
X
. Consider now the corresponding
K
-orbit
O^{K}_{\lambda}
under the Matsuki correspondence. Let
{\mathfrak{k}}
be the Lie algebra of
K
and let
\theta
be the corresponding Cartan involution. We choose
B\in C_{\lambda}=O^{K}_{\lambda}\cap O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
. We would like to prove that
{\mathfrak{b}}+{\mathfrak{k}}={\mathfrak{g}}
, which then implies that
O^{K}_{\lambda}
is open and
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
is closed.

Translating the constraints on the roots in terms of
\theta
, we see that for each simple root
\alpha
of
({\mathfrak{b}},{\mathfrak{t}})
, either
\theta\alpha<0
, or
\alpha
is compact imaginary (i.e.,
\theta\alpha=\alpha
and
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\pm\alpha}\subset{\mathfrak{k}}
).

We want to show that for each root
\alpha
we have
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\alpha}\subset{\mathfrak{k}}+{\mathfrak{b}}
. We proceed by downward induction on the height of
\alpha
. If
ht(\alpha)>0
then
\alpha>0
and
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}\subset\mathfrak{b}
. If
ht(\alpha)<0
and suppose
\mathfrak{g}_{\beta}\subset{\mathfrak{k}}+\mathfrak{b}
for all
ht(\beta)>ht(\alpha)
. Write
\alpha
as a sum of simple roots
\alpha_{i}
, and each
\alpha_{i}
either satisfies
\theta\alpha_{i}<0
or
\theta\alpha_{i}=\alpha_{i}
. Therefore
ht(\theta\alpha)\leq ht(\alpha)
.

If
ht(\theta\alpha)<ht(\alpha)
then by inductive hypothesis we have
\mathfrak{g}_{\theta\alpha}\subset{\mathfrak{k}}+{\mathfrak{b}}
. On the other hand,
(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\theta\alpha})^{\theta}\subset{\mathfrak{k}}
. Therefore
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}\subset\mathfrak{g}_{\theta\alpha}+(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\theta\alpha})^{\theta}\subset{\mathfrak{b}}+{\mathfrak{k}}
.

If
ht(\theta\alpha)=ht(\alpha)
, then this can happen only when
\alpha
is compact imaginary. In this case,
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}\subset{\mathfrak{k}}
. This completes the induction.
∎

We define a measure of complexity of an indecomposable free-monodromic tilting sheaf
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
.

Definition 6.10
.

Let
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
. Let
\ell(\lambda)
be the minimal non-negative integer
n
such that there exists
\psi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
and
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
such that
\ell(w)=n
and
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
is a direct summand of
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda_{0},\psi}\star\mathcal{T}_{w}
.

For example,
\ell(\lambda_{0},\chi)=0
for any
\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
.

Lemma 6.11
.

For any
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
, we have

\ell(\lambda,\chi)=\operatorname{codim}_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\lambda}.\quad\mbox{(codimension in $X$)}

In particular,
\ell(\lambda,\chi)
depends only on
\lambda\in I
, and we denote it by
\ell(\lambda)
.

Proof.

For
\lambda\in I
, let
q_{\lambda}=(d_{\lambda}-n_{\lambda})/2
. By (
5.2
), we see
q_{\lambda}-q_{\lambda_{0}}=\operatorname{codim}_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\lambda}
(where
\lambda_{0}
indexes the closed
G_{\mathbb{R}}
-orbit). Therefore we only need to show that
\ell(\lambda,\chi)=q_{\lambda}-q_{\lambda_{0}}
.

We first show
\ell(\lambda,\chi)\geq q_{\lambda}-q_{\lambda_{0}}
. For
\mu<\lambda
, we have
q_{\mu}<q_{\lambda}
because
q_{\mu}-q_{\lambda}=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\mu}-\dim_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\lambda}
. For an indecomposable tilting sheaf
\mathcal{T}_{\mu,\psi}
and a simple reflection
s\in{\mathbf{W}}
, the support of
\mathcal{T}_{\mu,\psi}\star\mathcal{T}_{s}
is
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(\overline{O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}})
. Suppose
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\subset\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(\overline{O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}})
, then either
\mu^{\prime}\leq\mu
, in which case
q_{\mu^{\prime}}\leq q_{\mu}
, or
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu^{\prime}}
is open in
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu^{\prime}})
, which contains a smaller orbit
\mu^{\prime\prime}\leq\mu
. By examining each case in Lemma
4.12
, we see that
q_{\mu^{\prime}}-q_{\mu^{\prime\prime}}=1
. Therefore
q_{\mu^{\prime}}\leq q_{\mu}+1
. In other words, if
\mathcal{T}_{\mu^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}}
is a direct summand of
\mathcal{T}_{\mu,\psi}\star\mathcal{T}_{s}
, then
q_{\mu^{\prime}}\leq q_{\mu}+1
. Applying this repeatedly, we see that if
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
appears as a direct summand of
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda_{0},\psi}\star\mathcal{T}_{w}
, then
q_{\lambda}-q_{\lambda_{0}}\leq\ell(w)
. This implies
\ell(\lambda,\chi)\geq q_{\lambda}-q_{\lambda_{0}}
.

We now show
\ell(\lambda,\chi)\leq q_{\lambda}-q_{\lambda_{0}}
. We prove this by induction on
q_{\lambda}
. If
q_{\lambda}=q_{\lambda_{0}}
, then
\lambda
is the closed orbit, hence
\ell(\lambda,\chi)=0
so the equality holds. Now suppose
\ell(\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})\leq q_{\lambda^{\prime}}-q_{\lambda_{0}}
holds for all
q_{\lambda^{\prime}}<q_{\lambda}
. Since
\lambda
is not the closed orbit, by Lemma
6.9
, there is a simple root
\alpha_{s}\in\Phi_{\lambda}
that is either complex and
\sigma\alpha_{s}<0
, or non-compact imaginary. In either case there is a smaller orbit
\lambda^{\prime}<\lambda
inside
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})
with
q_{\lambda^{\prime}}=q_{\lambda}-1
, by checking the cases listed in Lemma
4.12
. By Lemma
6.5
, there exists
\chi^{\prime}\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
such that
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
is a direct summand of
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}}\star\mathcal{T}_{s}
. This implies
\ell(\lambda,\chi)\leq\ell(\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})+1
. By inductive hypothesis, we conclude that
\ell(\lambda,\chi)\leq q_{\lambda^{\prime}}-q_{\lambda_{0}}+1=q_{\lambda}-q_{\lambda_{0}}
. This proves the inequality
\ell(\lambda,\chi)\leq q_{\lambda}-q_{\lambda_{0}}
.

Combining both inequalities we conclude that
\ell(\lambda,\chi)=q_{\lambda}-q_{\lambda_{0}}=\operatorname{codim}_{\mathbb{C}}O^{K}_{\lambda}
.
∎

Remark 6.12
.

Using the multiplicity one statement in Lemma
6.5
, the above argument shows the following stronger statement: for any
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
, and any
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
of length equal to
\ell(\lambda)
such that
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
is a direct summand of
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda_{0},\psi}\star\mathcal{T}_{w}
for some
\psi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
,
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
appears in
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda_{0},\psi}\star\mathcal{T}_{w}
with multiplicity one.

7.
Localization

This section will play a technical role in the proof of the main results in Section
9
. The localization procedure here can be viewed as the counterpart of equivariant localization under Koszul duality.

7.1.
Preliminaries

Let
V=\operatorname{Spec}{\mathcal{R}}
. Let
{\mathbf{T}}^{\vee}=\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\mathbb{X}_{*}({\mathbf{T}}),\mathbb{G}_{m,{\mathbf{k}}})=\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\pi_{1}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}),\mathbb{G}_{m,{\mathbf{k}}})
be the dual torus of
{\mathbf{T}}
over
{\mathbf{k}}
. Then the formal version
\textup{Spf}\ {\mathcal{R}}
of
V
can be canonically identified with the formal completion of
{\mathbf{T}}^{\vee}
at the identity element. We have a natural
{\mathbf{W}}
-action on
V
.

For any
\lambda\in I
let
\overline{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\lambda}
be the completion of the group ring
{\mathbf{k}}[\pi_{1}(\overline{{\mathbf{T}}}^{c}_{\lambda})]
at the augmentation ideal. Then
V_{\lambda}:=\operatorname{Spec}\overline{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\lambda}\subset V
is a closed subscheme. By Lemma
4.6
(1), the abstract Cartan
{\mathbf{T}}
carries a real structure
\sigma_{\lambda}
. Let
\theta_{\lambda}
be the Cartan involution on
{\mathbf{T}}
corresponding to the real structure
\sigma_{\lambda}
(so
\theta_{\lambda}
is the composition of
\sigma_{\lambda}
with the compact real form
\sigma_{c}
of
{\mathbf{T}}
). The Cartan involution
\theta_{\lambda}
acts on
{\mathbf{T}}^{\vee}
hence on
V
. Write
-\theta_{\lambda}
to be
\theta_{\lambda}
composed with inversion. Then
V_{\lambda}
is the fixed points subscheme
V^{-\theta_{\lambda}}
of
V
. Again the formal version
\textup{Spf}\ \overline{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\lambda}
of
V_{\lambda}
can be identified with the formal completion of
({\mathbf{T}}^{\vee})^{-\theta_{\lambda}}
at the identity element. The group
{\mathbf{W}}^{\theta_{\lambda}}={\mathbf{W}}^{\sigma_{\lambda}}
acts on
V_{\lambda}
. By Lemma
4.15
, we have
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda}\subset{\mathbf{W}}^{\sigma_{\lambda}}
, which then acts on
V_{\lambda}
.

Recall
\lambda_{0}\in I
indexes the closed
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit.
Let
{\mathcal{S}}=\overline{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\lambda_{0}}
. We have
V_{\lambda_{0}}=\operatorname{Spec}{\mathcal{S}}\subset V
. Let
T_{0}
be a maximally split
\sigma
-stable Cartan. By Lemma
4.15
,
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
can be identified with the real Weyl group
W(G(\mathbb{R}),T(\mathbb{R}))
for a maximally split
\sigma
-stable torus
T
, which by Lemma
4.14
is also
W^{\sigma}=W(\mathbb{R})
for the induced real structure on
W=W(G,T)
. We have a natural map
{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathbf{W}}\to{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}={\mathcal{S}}^{W(\mathbb{R})}
. Define

(7.1)

({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}:=\textup{Im}({\mathcal{R}}^{\mathbf{W}}\to{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}).

Then
({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}
is a subring of
{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})
that has the same fraction field with
{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})
. It is shown in
[
15
]
that
({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}={\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
outside
4
exceptional cases in type
E
