. We note that the only quasi-split simple group for which
({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}\neq{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
is the non-split quasi-split form of
E_{6}
.

We consider the projection map

\operatorname{pr}_{V}:V_{\lambda_{0}}\times_{V\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}{\mathbf{W}}}V=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{S}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R})\to V=\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{R}.

Let
V_{r}
be the scheme-theoretic image of
\operatorname{pr}_{V}
; this is a
{\mathbf{W}}
-stable closed subscheme of
V
. It is easy to see that the reduced structure of
V_{r}
is the union

V^{\textup{red}}_{r}=\bigcup_{w\in{\mathbf{W}}}wV_{\lambda_{0}}\subset V.

Equivalently,
V^{\textup{red}}_{r}
is the union of
V_{\lambda}
for those
\lambda
attached to a fixed maximally split
\sigma
-stable torus
T_{0}
.

The category
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
is linear over
{\mathcal{R}}
, i.e.,
{\mathcal{R}}
acts on the identity functor
{\rm id}_{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})}
.

Lemma 7.2
.

The action of
{\mathcal{R}}
on
{\rm id}_{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})}
factors through the quotient
\mathcal{O}(V_{r})
(regular functions on
V_{r}
). The action of
{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}
on
{\rm id}_{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})}
factors through
({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By definition,
\mathcal{O}(V_{r})
is the image of the natural map
\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{S}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}
sending
x\mapsto 1\otimes x
. Taking
{\mathbf{W}}
-invariants,
\mathcal{O}(V_{r})^{{\mathbf{W}}}
is the image of the natural map
\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}\to\mathcal{S}
which lands in
\mathcal{S}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
. Hence
\mathcal{O}(V_{r})^{{\mathbf{W}}}=({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}
. From this we see that the second assertion follows from the first.

We prove the first assertion. By Proposition
6.8
, it suffices to check that the action of
{\mathcal{R}}
on
{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
(via right monodromy on
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
) factors through
\mathcal{O}(V_{r})
, for
{\mathcal{T}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
supported on
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
, and
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
. The action of
{\mathcal{R}}\otimes{\mathcal{R}}
on
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
by the left and right monodromy factors through
{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
by Proposition
8.7
, and the first copy of
\mathcal{R}
-action is the same as the right monodromy on
{\mathcal{T}}
. For
{\mathcal{T}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
supported on the closed orbit,
{\mathcal{R}}
acts on
{\mathcal{T}}
through the quotient
{\mathcal{S}}
, hence the
{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
-action on
{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
factors through
{\mathcal{S}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
. Therefore the second copy of
{\mathcal{R}}
-action factors through the image of
{\mathcal{R}}\to{\mathcal{S}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
(
r\mapsto 1\otimes r
), which is
\mathcal{O}(V_{r})
by definition.
∎

7.3.
Localization

We fix a Borel
B_{0}\in O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
and a
\sigma
-stable maximal torus
T_{0}\subset B_{0}
. Let
P_{0}\supset B_{0}
be the minimal
\sigma
-stable parabolic subgroup containing
B_{0}
. Let
A_{0}\subset T_{0}
be the subtorus corresponding to the split part of
T_{0,\mathbb{R}}
. Via the isomorphism
\iota_{B_{0}}:T_{0}\subset B_{0}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbf{T}}
,
A_{0}
gets identified with the subtorus
{\mathbf{A}}\subset{\mathbf{T}}
, which is the neutral component of
{\mathbf{T}}^{-\theta_{\lambda_{0}}}
. We denote by
{\mathbf{A}}^{\vee}
the
{\mathbf{k}}
-torus dual to
{\mathbf{A}}
. Then
\textup{Spf}\ {\mathcal{S}}
(the formal version of
V_{\lambda_{0}}
) is canonically identified with the completion of
{\mathbf{A}}^{\vee}
at the identity.

Consider the restricted root system
\Phi(G,A_{0})
, with basis given by
B_{0}
. Via the isomorphism
\iota_{B_{0}}:A_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{A}}
, we view
\alpha\in\Phi(G,A_{0})
as characters on
{\mathbf{A}}
, and the corresponding coroot
\alpha^{\vee}
as characters on
{\mathbf{A}}^{\vee}
. Let
J
be a subset of simple roots in
\Phi(G,A_{0})
. Let
{\mathbf{A}}_{J}\subset{\mathbf{A}}
and
A_{J}\subset A_{0}
be the neutral component of
\cap_{j\in J}\ker(\alpha_{j})
. Similarly, let
{\mathbf{A}}^{\vee}_{J}\subset{\mathbf{A}}^{\vee}
be the neutral component of
\cap_{j\in J}\ker(\alpha^{\vee}_{j})
. Let
V_{J}\subset V_{\lambda_{0}}
be a closed subscheme provided by the inclusion
{\mathbf{A}}^{\vee}_{J}\subset{\mathbf{A}}^{\vee}
. It is identified with the completion of the torus
{\mathbf{A}}^{\vee}_{J}
at the identity again considered as a scheme and not a formal scheme.

Let
{\mathcal{K}}_{J}
be the localization of
V
at the generic point of
V_{J}
. The category
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
is linear over
{\mathcal{R}}
. We define the localization of
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
at the generic point of
V_{J}
, denoted
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}
, as the idempotent completion of the
{\mathcal{K}}_{J}
-linear additive category whose objects are the same as those in
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
, and the morphisms are defined as

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2}):=\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})}({\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}.

7.4.
Passing to a Levi

We continue with the above notations. Let
L:=L_{J}=C_{G}(A_{J})\subset G
and
P:=P_{J}\supset P_{0}
be the unique parabolic subgroup of
G
containing
P_{0}
with
L
as a Levi subgroup. Then
L
and
P
are also
\sigma
-stable hence defined over
\mathbb{R}
.

Let
X_{L}
be the flag variety of
L_{J}
and let
\widetilde{X}_{L}
be the
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-torsor over
X_{L}
. We have the monodromic category
{\mathcal{M}}_{L_{\mathbb{R}}}
and the subcategory of free-monodromic tilting sheaves
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{L_{\mathbb{R}}})
. Applying Lemma
7.2
to
L_{\mathbb{R}}
we see that the action of
{\mathcal{R}}
on
{\rm id}_{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{L_{\mathbb{R}}})}
also factors through
\mathcal{O}(V_{r})
(in fact we can replace
V_{r}
by a further subscheme, which is the image of
V_{\lambda_{0}}\times_{V\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}W_{L}}V\to V
). Therefore it makes sense to define the localization
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{L_{\mathbb{R}}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}
.

Let
p_{L}\colon P\to L
be the projection, whose kernel is the unipotent radical
U_{P}
of
P
. We have a closed embedding

i_{P}:X_{L}\hookrightarrow X

sending
B^{\prime}\in X_{L}
to
p_{L}^{-1}(B^{\prime})\in X
. The image of
i_{P}
is the set of Borel subgroups of
G
contained in
P
. Similarly we have a closed embedding of enhanced flag varieties

\widetilde{i}_{P}:\widetilde{X}_{L}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}

covering
i_{P}
. Consider the diagram of real algebraic stacks

(7.2)

\textstyle{L_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\widetilde{X}_{L}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces P_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\widetilde{X}_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\iota}_{P}}
\textstyle{G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\widetilde{X}}

where
\widetilde{\pi}_{P}
is induced by the projection
p_{L,\mathbb{R}}:P_{\mathbb{R}}\to L_{\mathbb{R}}
(so
\widetilde{\pi}
is a
U_{P,\mathbb{R}}
-gerbe) and
\widetilde{\iota}_{P}
is induced by
\widetilde{i}_{P}
. Similarly, we have a diagram

\textstyle{L_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash X_{L}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces P_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\widetilde{X}_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{P}}
\textstyle{G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash X}

Lemma 7.5
.

The maps of real algebraic stacks
\iota_{P}:P_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash X_{L}\to G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash X
and
\widetilde{\iota}_{P}:P_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\widetilde{X}_{L}\to G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\widetilde{X}
are closed embeddings.

Proof.

The embedding
\widetilde{i}_{P}
induce isomorphisms
G\times^{P}\widetilde{X}_{L}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\widetilde{X}
. We have a closed embedding
G_{\mathbb{R}}\times^{P_{\mathbb{R}}}\widetilde{X}_{L}\hookrightarrow G\times^{P}\widetilde{X}_{L}
. Composing the two maps we get a closed embedding
G_{\mathbb{R}}\times^{P_{\mathbb{R}}}\widetilde{X}_{L}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}
. Quotienting by
G_{\mathbb{R}}
we get the desired closed embedding
\widetilde{\iota}_{P}
. Since
\iota_{P}
is
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-equivariant, quotienting by
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
shows that
\iota_{P}
is also a closed embedding.
∎

Recall
d_{\lambda_{0}}
(resp.
d_{\mu_{0}}
) is the real dimensions of the closed
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit in
X
(resp. closed
L(\mathbb{R})
-orbit on
X_{L}
). Note that
n_{\lambda_{0}}=n_{\mu_{0}}
. Let

\delta^{G}_{L}=\lfloor\frac{d_{\lambda_{0}}+n_{\lambda_{0}}}{2}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{d_{\mu_{0}}+n_{\mu_{0}}}{2}\rfloor.

Note that
d_{\lambda_{0}}-d_{\mu_{0}}=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(G/P)
. When
\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(G/P)
is even,
\delta^{G}_{L}=\frac{1}{2}\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(G/P)
; when
\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(G/P)
is odd,
\delta^{G}_{L}
is one of the integers closest to
\frac{1}{2}\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(G/P)
.

Proposition 7.6
.

Consider the functor

\gamma:=\widetilde{\iota}_{P*}\widetilde{\pi}^{*}_{P}[\delta^{G}_{L}]:{\mathcal{M}}_{L_{\mathbb{R}}}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}.

(1)

\gamma
is an
\mathcal{H}_{L}
-equivariant full embedding.

(2)

\gamma
restricts to a
\textup{Tilt}(\mathcal{H}_{L})
-equivariant full embedding of tilting sheaves

\gamma_{\textup{Tilt}}:\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{L_{\mathbb{R}}})\to\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}).

(3)

\gamma_{\textup{Tilt}}
induces an equivalence after localization

\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{L_{\mathbb{R}}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}.

The proof will be given after some preparations.

Lemma 7.7
.

Recall
J
is a subset of simple roots of
\Phi(G,A_{0})
that cut out
A_{J},{\mathbf{A}}_{J},{\mathbf{A}}^{\vee}_{J}
and
V_{J}
. For a fixed
\lambda\in I
we have
V_{J}\subset V_{\lambda}
if and only if the intersection
O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}\cap i_{P}(X_{L})
is nonempty, i.e. if and only if there exists a Borel subgroup
B^{\prime}\subset P\subset G
contained in
O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
as a point of
X
.

Proof.

Note that the following are equivalent:

(7.3)

V_{J}\subset V_{\lambda}\iff{\mathbf{A}}_{J}\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{-\theta_{\lambda}}.

Suppose
B\subset P
is a Borel subgroup and
B_{L}=B\cap L
. Let
T\subset B_{L}
be a
\sigma
-stable torus. Note that
A_{J}
is the split center of
L
, hence
A_{J}\subset T
. Consider the image of
A_{J}
under
\iota_{B}:T\subset B\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbf{T}}
. On the one hand,
\iota_{B}
and
\iota_{B_{0}}
restricts to the same map
A_{J}\to{\mathbf{T}}
(because they differ by
{\mathbf{W}}_{L}
), hence
\iota_{B}(A_{J})={\mathbf{A}}_{J}
. On the other hand,
A_{J}
is contained in complexification of the split part of
T_{\mathbb{R}}
, hence
\iota_{B}(A_{J})\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{-\theta_{\lambda}}
. Therefore
{\mathbf{A}}_{J}\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{-\theta_{\lambda}}
, and
V_{J}\subset V_{\lambda}
by (
7.3
).

Conversely, suppose
V_{J}\subset V_{\lambda}
, hence
{\mathbf{A}}_{J}\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{-\theta_{\lambda}}
by (
7.3
). Let
B\in O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
and
T\subset B
be a
\sigma
-stable maximal torus. Let
A_{1}\subset T
be the complexification of the split part of
T_{\mathbb{R}}
. Changing
(T,B)
by
G(\mathbb{R})
-conjugacy, we may assume
A_{1}\subset A_{0}
. Under
\iota_{B}:T\subset B\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbf{T}}
, we have
\iota_{B}(A_{1})={\mathbf{T}}^{-\theta_{\lambda},\circ}\supset{\mathbf{A}}_{J}
. Let
A^{\prime}_{J}=\iota^{-1}({\mathbf{A}}_{J})\subset A_{1}
.

Now we have two subtori
A_{J},A^{\prime}_{J}
of
A_{0}
, which is in turn in
T_{0}
. Via
\iota_{B_{0}}
and
\iota_{B}
respectively, they map isomorphically to
{\mathbf{A}}_{J}
. Let
a^{\prime}_{J}\in A^{\prime}_{J}
be a generic element, and let
a_{J}\in A_{J}
such that
\iota_{B}(a^{\prime}_{J})=\iota_{B_{0}}(a_{J})
. Both
a^{\prime}_{J}
and
a_{J}
are in
T_{0}
and they are in the same conjugacy class of
G
, there exists
w\in W(G,T_{0})
such that
w(a^{\prime}_{J})=a_{J}
. Since
a^{\prime}_{J}
is generic in
A^{\prime}_{J}
,
w
restricts to the isomorphism
A^{\prime}_{J}\xrightarrow{\iota_{B}}{\mathbf{A}}_{J}\xrightarrow{\iota_{B_{0}}^{-1}}A_{J}
. Since
a_{J},a^{\prime}_{J}\in A_{0}
are the same
W(G,T_{0})
-orbit, they are also in the same
W(G,A_{0})
-orbit. Let
u\in W(G,A_{0})
be such that
u(a^{\prime}_{J})=a_{J}
, then
u|_{A^{\prime}_{J}}=w|_{A^{\prime}_{J}}
. Since
W(G,A_{0})=W(G(\mathbb{R}),T_{0,\mathbb{R}})
, we can lift
u
to
\dot{u}\in G(\mathbb{R})
normalizing
T_{0}
. We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{A_{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{Ad}(\dot{u})B}}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}_{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\scriptstyle{w}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{B}}
\textstyle{A_{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{B_{0}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{T}}

By Lemma
7.8
below,
B_{0}
and
\operatorname{Ad}(\dot{u})B
are in the same
L=C_{G}(A_{J})
-orbit of
X
. Now
B_{0}\in i_{P}(X_{L})
, which is the
L
-orbit through
B_{0}
, we have
\operatorname{Ad}(\dot{u})B\in i_{P}(X_{L})\cap O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
.
∎

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
A\subset G
be a torus. Consider the map
\kappa:X^{A}\to\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(A,{\mathbf{T}})
sending
B\in X^{A}
(i.e., a Borel subgroup
B
containing
A
) to the map
\iota_{B}:A\subset B\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbf{T}}
. Then each non-empty fiber of
\kappa
is stable under the Levi subgroup
C_{G}(A)
of
G
, and is
C_{G}(A)
-equivariantly isomorphic to the flag variety of
C_{G}(A)
.

Proof.

The map
\kappa
is equivariant under
N_{G}(A)
, therefore each fiber has an action by
C_{G}(A)
.

Let
B_{1},B_{2}\in X^{A}
with the same image under
\kappa
. Let
T_{i}\subset B_{i}
be a maximal torus containing
A
,
i=1,2
. Let
\iota_{i}:T_{i}\subset B_{i}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbf{T}}
be the isomorphisms induced by
B_{i}
. The fact that
\kappa(B_{1})=\kappa(B_{2})
implies
\iota_{1}|_{A}=\iota_{2}|_{A}
. Let
g\in G
be such that
\operatorname{Ad}(B_{1})=B_{2}
and
\operatorname{Ad}(T_{1})=T_{2}
. Then
\iota_{2}\circ\operatorname{Ad}(g)=\iota_{1}\in\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(T_{1},{\mathbf{T}})
. Restricting to
A
we get that
\iota_{2}(\operatorname{Ad}(g)a)=\iota_{1}(a)
, which is
\iota_{2}(a)
for all
a\in A
. Therefore
\operatorname{Ad}(g)a=a
hence
g\in C_{G}(A)
. This shows that each non-empty fiber of
\kappa
is a homogeneous space for
C_{G}(A)
.

The stabilizers of
C_{G}(A)
on
X^{A}
are clearly Borel subgroups of
C_{G}(A)
. Hence each non-empty fiber of
\kappa
is isomorphically to the flag variety of
C_{G}(A)
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
7.6
.

(1) Since
\widetilde{\pi}_{P}
is a gerbe for a unipotent group,
\widetilde{\pi}_{P}^{*}:{\mathcal{M}}_{L_{\mathbb{R}}}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{P_{\mathbb{R}}}
is an equivalence. Since
\widetilde{\iota}_{P}
is a closed embedding by Lemma
7.5
,
\widetilde{\iota}_{P*}:{\mathcal{M}}_{P_{\mathbb{R}}}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
is a full embedding. Therefore
\gamma
is a full embedding. It is
\mathcal{H}_{L}
-equivariant because both
\widetilde{\pi}_{P}^{*}
and
\widetilde{\iota}_{P*}
are.

(2)
Let
I_{L}
and
\widetilde{I}_{L}
be the counterparts of
I
and
\widetilde{I}
for
L_{\mathbb{R}}
. For
\mu\in I_{L}
, let
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{L,\mu}\subset X_{L}
be the corresponding orbit. The diagram (
7.2
) induces a map

\kappa:I_{L}\hookrightarrow I
sending
\mu
to the unique
\lambda\in I
such that
i_{P}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{L,\mu})\subset O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
. Since
\iota_{P}
is a closed embedding by Lemma
7.5
,
\kappa
is injective.

For any
\mu\in I_{L}
, we have a canonical isomorphism
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\mu}\cong{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\kappa(\mu)}
, therefore the injection
\kappa
lift canonically to an injection
\widetilde{\kappa}:\widetilde{I}_{L}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{I}
such that the following diagram is Cartesian

\textstyle{\widetilde{I}_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\kappa}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{I_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa}
\textstyle{I}

For any
(\mu,\psi)\in\widetilde{I}_{L}
, with
\widetilde{\kappa}(\mu,\psi)=(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
. Restricting the diagram (
7.2
) to
P(\mathbb{R})\backslash\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{L,\mu}
we get maps

\textstyle{L(\mathbb{R})\backslash\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{L,\mu}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces P(\mathbb{R})\backslash\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{L,\mu}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}_{P,\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\iota}_{P,\mu}}
\textstyle{G(\mathbb{R})\backslash\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}}

where
\widetilde{\pi}_{P,\mu}
is a
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-equivariant
U_{P,\mathbb{R}}
-gerbe and
\widetilde{\iota}_{P,\mu}
is a
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-equivariant isomorphism by Lemma
7.5
. This implies that
\gamma
sends
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu,\psi}
to a shift of
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
. The perversity functions for
X_{L}
and
X
are related by

p_{\lambda}-p_{\mu}=\lfloor\frac{d_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda}}{2}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{d_{\mu}+n_{\mu}}{2}\rfloor=\lfloor\frac{d_{\lambda_{0}}+n_{\lambda_{0}}}{2}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{d_{\mu_{0}}+n_{\mu_{0}}}{2}\rfloor=\delta^{G}_{L}

because
d_{\lambda}-d_{\mu}=d_{\lambda_{0}}-d_{\mu_{0}}=\dim_{\mathbb{R}}G_{\mathbb{R}}/P_{\mathbb{R}}
(which follows from the fact that
\widetilde{\iota}_{P,\mu}
is an isomorphism),
n_{\lambda}=n_{\mu}
,
d_{\lambda}+n_{\lambda}\equiv d_{\lambda_{0}}+n_{\lambda_{0}}\mod 2
and
d_{\mu}+n_{\mu}\equiv d_{\mu_{0}}+n_{\mu_{0}}\mod 2
. Therefore,
\gamma
sends
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mu,\psi}
to
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
.

For the same reason,
\gamma
sends
{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\mu,\psi}
to
{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda,\chi}
. Therefore
\gamma
sends
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{L_{\mathbb{R}}})
to
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
.

(3) By (2) we see that
\gamma_{{\widetilde{t}}}\otimes\mathcal{K}_{J}
is fully faithful. Let us show it is essentially surjective.

Let
X^{\prime}\subset X
be the union of
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbits that intersect
i_{P}(X_{L})
, and let
\widetilde{X}^{\prime}\subset\widetilde{X}
be its preimage in
\widetilde{X}
. By Lemma
7.5
,
X^{\prime}\cong G_{\mathbb{R}}\times^{P_{\mathbb{R}}}X_{L}
is closed in
X
. The essential image of
\gamma_{\textup{Tilt}}
, hence also the essential image of
\gamma_{\textup{Tilt}}\otimes\mathcal{K}_{J}
, consists of tilting sheaves supported on
\widetilde{X}^{\prime}
.

On the other hand, by Lemma
7.7
, after the localization
-\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}
, objects in
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}
are exactly the images of tilting sheaves supported on
\widetilde{X}^{\prime}
, which is the image of
\gamma_{\textup{Tilt}}\otimes\mathcal{K}_{J}
by the above paragraph. This proves the essential surjectivity of
\gamma_{\textup{Tilt}}\otimes\mathcal{K}_{J}
.

∎

7.9.
Localized Hecke action

We keep working in the above setting. Consider the second projection

\operatorname{pr}_{J,V}\colon V_{J}\times_{V\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}{\mathbf{W}}}V\to V

and let
V_{J,r}
be its scheme-theoretic image. Let
{\mathcal{R}}_{J}
be the localization of
V
at the generic points of the irreducible components of
V_{J,r}
. It is isomorphic to the product of the copies of
{\mathcal{K}}_{J}
indexed by the
{\mathbf{W}}
-orbit of
V_{J}
as a subspace of
V
.

Recall that the Hecke category is a
{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
-linear category. We define the localization of the Hecke category
{\mathcal{R}}_{J}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}
to be the idempotent completion of the
{\mathcal{R}}_{J}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}
-linear category whose objects are the same as the objects of
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
and the morphisms are given by

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{R}}_{J}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2}):={\mathcal{R}}_{J}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})}({\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}.

Note that
{\mathcal{R}}_{J}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}
splits into the direct product of categories numbered by a pair of elements in the
{\mathbf{W}}
-orbit of
V_{J}
.
The
\star
-action of
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
on
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
passes to a functor

(7.4)

\star\colon(\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J})\times({\mathcal{R}}_{J}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J})\to\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}.

The action is compatible with the direct product decompositions.

7.10.
Localization at codimension
0

We continue with the notations of Section
7.3
.
Let
{\mathcal{K}}
be the field of fractions of
{\mathcal{S}}
.
We also put
{\mathcal{Q}}:={\mathcal{K}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
for the field of fractions of
{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
, which is also the field of fractions of
({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}=\textup{Im}({\mathcal{R}}^{\mathbf{W}}\to{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})
. Consider the natural composition
{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathbf{W}}\to{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\to{\mathcal{Q}}
. Then
{\mathcal{S}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathbf{W}}}{\mathcal{Q}}\cong{\mathcal{K}}
.

Recall
T_{0}
is a maximally split real torus and
A_{0}\subset T_{0}
is the split subtorus. Let
I_{0}\subset I
be the set of orbits attached to
T_{0}
in the sense of Definition
4.7
, and
\widetilde{I}_{0}
be the preimage of
I_{0}
in
\widetilde{I}
. Note that for
\lambda\in I_{0}
we have
n_{\lambda}=\dim A_{0}
; for
\lambda\notin I_{0}
we have
n_{\lambda}<\dim A_{0}
. For
\lambda\in I_{0}
,
V_{\lambda}=\operatorname{Spec}\overline{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\lambda}
is a
w
-translate of
V_{\lambda_{0}}=\operatorname{Spec}{\mathcal{S}}\subset\operatorname{Spec}{\mathcal{R}}
for some
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
.

For
\lambda\in I_{0}
, let
{\mathcal{K}}_{\lambda}=\textup{Frac}(\overline{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\lambda})
. We have
\overline{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\lambda}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{Q}}={\mathcal{K}}_{\lambda}
. We have an isomorphism
{\mathcal{K}}\cong{\mathcal{K}}_{\lambda}
unique up to precomposing with the action of
W(\mathbb{R})
. Then
\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{Q}\cong\prod_{\lambda\in I_{0}}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}
.

Consider the localization
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{Q}}
of the category
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
, obtained as the idempotent completion of the additive category whose objects are the same as the objects of
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
and the morphisms are given by

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{Q}}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}):=\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathbf{W}}}{\mathcal{Q}}.

Proposition 7.11
.

(1)

If
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}-\widetilde{I}_{0}
, then
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
and
\widetilde{\nabla}_{\lambda,\chi}
are zero in
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{Q}}}
.

(2)

For
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}_{0}
, we have
\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{Q}}}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi},\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi})\cong{\mathcal{K}}_{\lambda}
.

(3)

The functor

\bigoplus_{(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}_{0}}D^{b}({\mathcal{K}}_{\lambda}\textup{-mod})\to{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{Q}}}

sending
(M_{\lambda,\chi})_{(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}_{0}}
to
\bigoplus_{(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}_{0}}M_{\lambda,\chi}\otimes_{{\mathcal{K}}_{\lambda}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

(1) If
\lambda\notin I_{0}
, the action of
{\mathcal{R}}
on
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
and
\widetilde{\nabla}_{\lambda,\chi}
factor through
{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}
but
\operatorname{Spec}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}
has dimension
n_{\lambda}
which is smaller than the dimension of
\operatorname{Spec}{\mathcal{S}}^{W(\mathbb{R})}
. Therefore the actions of
{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}
on
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
and
\widetilde{\nabla}_{\lambda,\chi}
also factor through a quotient with smaller dimension than
\operatorname{Spec}{\mathcal{S}}^{W(\mathbb{R})}
, hence localizing to the generic point of
\operatorname{Spec}{\mathcal{S}}^{W(\mathbb{R})}
kills
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
and
\widetilde{\nabla}_{\lambda,\chi}
.

(2) If
(\lambda,\chi)
and
(\lambda,\psi)\in\widetilde{I}_{0}
, then by Lemma
2.7
,

\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi},{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\psi})=\begin{cases}\overline{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\lambda},&\chi=\psi,\\
0,&\chi\neq\psi.\end{cases}

Tensoring with
{\mathcal{Q}}
we get

\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{Q}}}}({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi},{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\psi})=\begin{cases}{\mathcal{K}}_{\lambda},&\chi=\psi,\\
0,&\chi\neq\psi.\end{cases}

(3) Since the
\{\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\}_{(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}}
generate
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{Q}}}
, in view of part (1) and (2), it remains to show that
\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{Q}}}}({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi},{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\psi})=0
for distinct orbits
\lambda,\mu\in I_{0}
. Note that the support of
\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi},{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\psi})
as an
{\mathcal{R}}
-module is contained in
V_{\lambda}\cap V_{\mu}
. By Lemma
4.14
and
[
1
, Proposition 12.9 and 12.14]
, that
({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})^{\circ}\neq({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\mu})^{\circ}
as subtori of
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
, hence the intersection
V_{\lambda}\cap V_{\mu}
has smaller dimension than
V_{\lambda_{0}}
. The same holds for the support of
\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi},{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\psi})
as an
{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathbf{W}}
-module. Therefore tensoring with
{\mathcal{Q}}
kills
\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda,\chi},{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\mu,\psi})
.

∎

By Proposition
7.11
, indecomposable objects in the localized category
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})_{{\mathcal{Q}}}
(idempotent completion of the image of
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
in
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{Q}}}
) are of the form
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
where
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}_{0}
(these are objects in the localized tilting category because they are direct summands of
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
after localization). On the other hand, we can define the localized Hecke category
\mathcal{H}_{G,\mathcal{Q}}
and tilting sheaves
\textup{Tilt}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{\mathcal{Q}}
inside it. For
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
,
\widetilde{\Delta}_{w}
is a direct summand of
{\mathcal{T}}_{w}
in
\textup{Tilt}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{\mathcal{Q}}
, hence
\widetilde{\Delta}_{w}\in\textup{Tilt}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{\mathcal{Q}}
. The assignment
w\mapsto\widetilde{\Delta}_{w}
gives a monoidal functor from
{\mathbf{W}}
(viewed as a category with objects
{\mathbf{W}}
and only identity morphisms) to
\textup{Tilt}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{\mathcal{Q}}
. In particular, the
\textup{Tilt}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{\mathcal{Q}}
-action on
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})_{{\mathcal{Q}}}
induces a right
{\mathbf{W}}
-action on the set of isomorphisms classes of indecomposable objects in
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})_{{\mathcal{Q}}}
, and hence induces a right action of
{\mathbf{W}}
on
\widetilde{I}_{0}
.

Lemma 7.12
.

Assume that
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split. The action of
{\mathbf{W}}
on
\widetilde{I}_{0}
defined above is the same as the restriction of the cross action. In other words, in
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{Q}}}
we have an isomorphism
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{w}\cong\widetilde{\Delta}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot w}
for
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}_{0}
and
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
.

Proof.

When
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split and
\lambda\in I_{0}
, all simple roots in
\Phi_{\lambda}
are in case (1) or (3) in Lemma
6.3
. For
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}_{0}
, by Lemma
6.3
we have
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{s}\cong\widetilde{\Delta}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot s}
in
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{Q}}}
(the contribution of orbits
\mu
or
\mu^{\pm}
become zero after localization). Writing any
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
as a product of simple reflections, we see that
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{w}
and
\widetilde{\Delta}_{(\lambda,\chi)\cdot w}
become isomorphic in
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{Q}}}
. This implies the lemma.
∎

8.
Real Soergel functor

In this section we extend Soergel’s functor
\mathbb{V}
from complex groups to quasi-split real groups, and study the behavior of tilting sheaves under the real Soergel functor.

From this section on, we assume that
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split.

8.1.
Regular covector

Let
\mathcal{N}^{*}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
be the nilcone. Also we have a decomposition
{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}={\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{*}\oplus i{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\mathbb{R}}
. Let

i\mathcal{N}^{*}(\mathbb{R}):=\mathcal{N}^{*}\cap i{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\mathbb{R}}.

Let
\mathcal{O}_{{\text{\rm reg}}}\subset\mathcal{N}^{*}
be the regular nilpotent orbit.

Lemma 8.2
.

(1)

We have
i\mathcal{N}^{*}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{O}_{{\text{\rm reg}}}
if and only if
G(\mathbb{R})
is quasi-split.

(2)

Suppose
\xi\in i\mathcal{N}^{*}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{O}_{{\text{\rm reg}}}
, then the Springer fiber
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}
is a single point, and is contained in the closed
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit of
X
.

Proof.

Suppose
\xi\in i\mathcal{N}^{*}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{O}_{{\text{\rm reg}}}
, then the Springer fiber
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}\subset X
is a single point, hence a real point of the flag variety
X
since
\xi
is pure imaginary. In particular, the point
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}
gives a Borel subgroup of
G
defined over
\mathbb{R}
. Since the closed
G(\mathbb{R})-
-orbit in
X
parametrizes Borel subgroups that are defined over
\mathbb{R}
,
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}
is contained in the closed
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit of
X
.

Conversely, assume
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split and
B_{\mathbb{R}}\subset G_{\mathbb{R}}
is a Borel subgroup defined over
\mathbb{R}
. Let
{\mathfrak{n}}_{\mathbb{R}}
be the nilpotent radical of
\textup{Lie}B_{\mathbb{R}}
. Then a generic element
\xi\in i{\mathfrak{n}}_{\mathbb{R}}
(i.e., its projection to each simple root space is nonzero), viewed as an element in
i{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\mathbb{R}}
using the Killing form, is regular.
∎

8.3.
Generic vanishing cycles

In the rest of the section we assume
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split. In this case, the closed
G(\mathbb{R})
orbit
O_{\lambda_{0}}^{\mathbb{R}}\subset X
is the set of real points of
X
.

Consider the moment map of
\widetilde{X}=G/U{\mathbf{T}}^{>0}
for the left action of
G
:

\mu:T^{*}\widetilde{X}\to{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}.

For
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-monodromic sheaves
\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}(\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}
, its singular support
SS(\mathcal{F})
is contained in the image of the pullback
T^{*}X\times_{X}\widetilde{X}\to T^{*}\widetilde{X}
, which is equal to
\mu^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{*})
. On the other hand, if
\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X})
, then
SS(\mathcal{F})\subset\mu^{-1}(i{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\mathbb{R}})
. Let

\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}=\mu^{-1}(i\mathcal{N}^{*}(\mathbb{R}))\subset T^{*}\widetilde{X}.

Then the above discussion shows that for
\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}
,
SS(\mathcal{F})\subset\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}
. Also, since
G(\mathbb{R})\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
has finitely many orbits on
\widetilde{X}
,
\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}
is the union of conormals of the orbits
\{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}\}
:

\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}=\bigcup_{\lambda\in I}T^{*}_{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}}\widetilde{X}.

Let
\xi\in i\mathcal{N}^{*}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{O}_{{\text{\rm reg}}}
and let

\Xi:=\mu^{-1}(\xi)\subset T^{*}\widetilde{X}.

Then by Lemma
8.2
, the Springer fiber
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}
is a single point
x
contained in the closed
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}\subset X
that is a real form of
X
. Hence projection to
\widetilde{X}
is an isomorphism
\Xi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi^{-1}(x)\subset\widetilde{X}
(a single
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-orbit). Moreover,
\Xi\subset T^{*}_{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\widetilde{X}
but is disjoint from the conormals of other
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
.

For a manifold
M
and a submanifold
N\subset M
, and
\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}(M)
, we use
\mu_{N}\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}(T^{*}_{N}M)
to denote the microlocalization of
\mathcal{F}
along
N
. See
[
16
, Definition 4.3.1]
.

For
\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}
, denote the microlocalization
\mu_{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}(\mathcal{F})\in D^{b}(T^{*}_{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}
along
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
simply by
\mu_{\lambda_{0}(\mathcal{F})}
; it is locally constant on the generic part of
T^{*}_{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
. Restricting to
\Xi
gives a functor

D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}\xrightarrow{\mu_{\lambda_{0}}}D^{b}(T^{*}_{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}\to D^{b}(\Xi)_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}\cong D^{b}(\pi^{-1}(x))_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}

Passing to completions and shifting so that a free-monodromic local system on the closed orbit in the heart of the
t
-structure is sent to an object concentrated in degree
0
, i.e. just its stalk shifted to degree
0
, we get a functor

\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\xi}:{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}=\widehat{D}^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}\to\widehat{D}^{b}(\Xi)_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}

If we choose a point
\widetilde{x}\in\pi^{-1}(x)
and let
\widetilde{\xi}=(\widetilde{x},\xi)\in\Xi
, the base point
\widetilde{\xi}
trivializes
\Xi
as a
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-torsor, and gives an equivalence
\widehat{D}^{b}(\Xi)_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}\cong D^{b}(\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R})
. Then
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\xi}
induces a functor

(8.1)

\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\widetilde{\xi}}:{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}\to D^{b}(\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R})

that depends on the choice of
\widetilde{\xi}\in T^{*}\widetilde{X}
over the regular
\xi
. When
\widetilde{\xi}
is fixed, we also write the functor as
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}
.

(1)

\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\nabla_{\lambda,\chi})
is concentrated in degree
0
.

(2)

\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\Delta_{\lambda,\chi})
is concentrated in degree
n_{\lambda_{0}}-n_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

(1) We argue by induction on
d_{\lambda}=\dim_{\mathbb{R}}O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
. If
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
corresponds to the closed orbit, then
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\nabla_{\lambda,\chi})
is the stalk of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda,\chi}
along
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
(up to a shift), and it is normalized to be in degree
0
. Otherwise, choose a point
x\in O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
(corresponding to a Borel
x
) such that
B
contains a
\sigma
-stable maximal torus
T
, and we can talk about the based root system
\Phi(G,T)
with positive roots defined by
B
. Since
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
is not closed, there is a simple root
\alpha\in\Phi(G,T)
and
\mu<\lambda
, such that
\pi_{\alpha}(O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}})=\pi_{\alpha}(O_{\mu}^{\mathbb{R}})
for the projection
\pi_{\alpha}\colon X\to X_{\alpha}=G/P_{\alpha}
. Since
O_{\lambda}^{\mathbb{R}}
is not closed, we have the following cases according to Lemma
6.9
:

•

\alpha
is complex and
\sigma\alpha<0
. In this case, we have
p_{\lambda}-p_{\mu}=1
, and a distinguished triangle
\pi_{\alpha}^{*}\pi_{\alpha*}\nabla_{\lambda,\chi}\to\nabla_{\lambda,\chi}\to\nabla_{\mu,\psi}\to
for some
\psi:\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\mu})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
.

•

\alpha
is noncompact imaginary and
\pi_{\alpha}^{-1}\pi_{\alpha}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})=O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}\cup O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}
. We have
p_{\lambda}=p_{\mu}
, and a distinguished triangle
\pi_{\alpha}^{*}\pi_{\alpha*}\nabla_{\lambda,\chi}\to\nabla_{\lambda,\chi}\to\nabla_{\mu,\psi}\to
for some
\psi:\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\mu})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
.

•

\alpha
is noncompact imaginary and
\pi_{\alpha}^{-1}\pi_{\alpha}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})=O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}\cup O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\cup O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}
, with
\mu<\lambda
and
\mu<\lambda^{\prime}
. Then
p_{\lambda}=p_{\lambda^{\prime}}=p_{\mu}
, and we have a distinguished triangle
\pi_{\alpha}^{*}\pi_{\alpha*}\nabla_{\lambda,\chi}\to\nabla_{\lambda,\chi}\oplus\nabla_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}}\to\nabla_{\mu,\psi}\to
for some
\chi^{\prime}:\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda^{\prime}})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
and
\psi:\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\mu})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
.

We have
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\pi^{*}_{\alpha}({\mathcal{G}}))=0
for any
{\mathcal{G}}\in D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(X_{\alpha})
, as the covector
\xi
does not lie in the image of the pullback of cotangent bundles
d\pi_{\alpha}:(T^{*}X_{\alpha})\times_{X_{\alpha}}X\to T^{*}X
. From the distinguished triangles above we see that
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\nabla_{\lambda,\chi})
is a direct summand of
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\nabla_{\mu,\psi})
. Since
d_{\mu}<d_{\lambda}
, by inductive hypothesis
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\nabla_{\mu,\psi})
is concentrated in degree
0
, therefore the same is true for
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\nabla_{\lambda,\chi})
.

(2) The argument is similar to the costandard case. In the induction step, we have the following cases

•

\alpha
is complex and
\sigma\alpha<0
. In this case, we have
p_{\lambda}-p_{\mu}=1
, and a distinguished triangle
\Delta_{\mu,\psi}\to\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\to\pi_{\alpha}^{!}\pi_{\alpha!}\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\to
. We conclude that
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\Delta_{\lambda,\chi})\cong\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\Delta_{\mu,\psi})
. Note that
n_{\mu}=n_{\lambda}
in this case.

•

\alpha
is noncompact imaginary and
\pi_{\alpha}^{-1}\pi_{\alpha}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})=O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}\cup O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}
. We have
p_{\lambda}=p_{\mu}
, and a distinguished triangle
\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\to\pi_{\alpha}^{!}\pi_{\alpha!}\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\to\Delta_{\mu,\psi}\to
. We conclude that
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\Delta_{\lambda,\chi})\cong\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\Delta_{\mu,\psi})[-1]
. Note that
n_{\mu}-n_{\lambda}=1
in this case.

•

\alpha
is noncompact imaginary and
\pi_{\alpha}^{-1}\pi_{\alpha}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})=O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}\cup O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\cup O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\mu}
, with
\mu<\lambda
and
\mu<\lambda^{\prime}
. Then
p_{\lambda}=p_{\lambda^{\prime}}=p_{\mu}
, and we have a distinguished triangle
\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\oplus\Delta_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}}\to\pi_{\alpha}^{!}\pi_{\alpha!}\Delta_{\lambda,\chi}\to\Delta_{\mu,\psi}\to
. We conclude that
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\Delta_{\lambda,\chi})
is a summand of
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\Delta_{\mu,\psi})[-1]
. Again
n_{\mu}-n_{\lambda}=1
in this case.

∎

Corollary 8.5
.

For any free-monodromic tilting sheaf
{\mathcal{T}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
,
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})
is concentrated in degree
0
.

8.6.
Soergel functor for the Hecke category

Consider the Hecke category
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}=\widehat{D}^{b}_{G}(\widetilde{X}\times\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}
. The construction of the Soergel functor can be applied to the complex group
G
viewed as a real group
R_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}G
and giving a Soergel functor for
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
. We spell this out.

Consider the doubled moment map
\mu^{(2)}:T^{*}\widetilde{X}\times T^{*}\widetilde{X}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Let
\Delta^{-}(\mathcal{N}^{*})\subset\Delta^{-}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{*}
be the anti-diagonally embedded nilcone. Let

\Lambda:=\mu^{(2),-1}(\Delta^{-}(\mathcal{N}^{*})).

It is well-known that

\Lambda=\bigcup_{w\in W}T^{*}_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{w}}(\widetilde{X}^{2}).

Let
\xi\in\mathcal{N}^{*}\cap\mathcal{O}_{{\text{\rm reg}}}
and consider
(\xi,-\xi)\in\Delta^{-}(\mathcal{N}^{*})
. Let
\Xi=\mu^{-1}(\xi),\Xi^{-}=\mu^{-1}(-\xi)
and consider

\mu^{(2),-1}(-\xi,\xi)=\Xi^{-}\times\Xi\subset T^{*}(\widetilde{X}^{2}).

Since the Springer fiber
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}
is a single point
x\in X
,
\mu^{(2),-1}(-\xi,\xi)
projects isomorphically onto
\pi^{-1}(x)^{2}\subset\widetilde{X}^{2}
, which is a
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-torsor. In particular,
\Xi^{-}\times\Xi
is contained in the conormal bundle of
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}\subset\widetilde{X}^{2}
and not in the closure of the conormals of
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{w}
for
e\neq w\in W
(i.e., it is contained in the generic part of the
T^{*}_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}(\widetilde{X}^{2})
).

For
\mathcal{K}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
, its microlocalization along
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}
is locally constant on the generic part of
T^{*}_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}(\widetilde{X}^{2})
and
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-unipotently monodromic. Restricting to
\Xi^{-}\times\Xi
gives a functor

\mathbb{V}_{(-\xi,\xi)}:{\mathcal{H}}_{G}\to\widehat{D}^{b}(\Xi^{-}\times\Xi)_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}

If we choose
\widetilde{x}\in\pi^{-1}(x)
hence
\widetilde{\xi}=(\widetilde{x},\xi)\in\Xi
and
-\widetilde{\xi}=(\widetilde{x},-\xi)\in\Xi^{-}
, we can then identify
\widehat{D}^{b}(\Xi^{-}\times\Xi)_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}
with
\widehat{D}^{b}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}\cong D^{b}(\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{R}}\otimes{\mathcal{R}})
. Then
\mathbb{V}_{(-\xi,\xi)}
induces a functor

(8.2)

\mathbb{V}_{(-\widetilde{\xi},\widetilde{\xi})}:{\mathcal{H}}_{G}\to\widehat{D}^{b}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}\cong D^{b}(\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{R}}\otimes{\mathcal{R}}).

It will be more convenient to turn right
\mathcal{R}\otimes\mathcal{R}
-modules into
\mathcal{R}
-bimodules. Let
\iota:\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{R}
be the involution given by the inversion on
\pi_{1}({\mathbf{T}}^{c})
. We consider the equivalence

(8.3)

\tau:\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R}\otimes\mathcal{R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{R}\textup{-mod-}\mathcal{R}

given by sending
M\in\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R}\otimes\mathcal{R}
to the same underlying vector space
M
equipped with the left and right actions of
\mathcal{R}
defined by

r_{1}\cdot m\cdot r_{2}=m\cdot(\iota(r_{1})\otimes r_{2}),\quad r_{1},r_{2}\in\mathcal{R},m\in M.

Composing (
8.2
) with the equivalence (
8.3
), we get a functor

(8.4)

{}_{-\widetilde{\xi}}\mathbb{V}_{\widetilde{\xi}}:{\mathcal{H}}_{G}\to D^{b}({\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod-}{\mathcal{R}}).

When
\widetilde{\xi}
is understood from the context we simply denote
{}_{-\widetilde{\xi}}\mathbb{V}_{\widetilde{\xi}}
by
\mathbb{V}
.

Recall in
[
10
, §4.5]
(in the
\ell
-adic setting), a similar functor
\mathbb{V}
was defined using the action of
\mathcal{H}_{G}
on a certain Whittaker category. A topological analogue has been constructed in
[
8
, §11.1]
.

We summarize the main properties of
\mathbb{V}
in the following proposition. Most of the assertions are proved in the literature with an a priori different definition of
\mathbb{V}
.

Proposition 8.7
.

(1)

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{e}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\heartsuit}_{G}

6
6

6

{\mathcal{H}}^{\heartsuit}_{G}
denotes the heart of the
t
-structure defined by
p
for the complex group, which is essentially the middle perversity.
be a projective cover of the constant sheaf on the preimage of
\Delta(X)\subset X\times X
in
\widetilde{X}\times\widetilde{X}
. Then
\mathbb{V}\cong\operatorname{RHom}(\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{e},-)
.

(2)

\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{e}
is isomorphic to the free-monodromic indecomposable tilting sheaf
\mathcal{T}_{w_{0}}
with full support. Therefore the functor
\mathbb{V}\cong\operatorname{RHom}(\mathcal{T}_{w_{0}},-)
.

(3)

We have an isomorphism of
\mathcal{R}
-bimodules
\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{H}}_{G}}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\cong\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}
. In particular, if we let
({\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod-}{\mathcal{R}})_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}
be the category of
{\mathcal{R}}
-bimodules where the actions of
{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}
through the left and right copies of
{\mathcal{R}}
coincide, then
\mathbb{V}
takes values in
D^{b}(({\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod-}{\mathcal{R}})_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}})
.

(4)

\mathbb{V}
is fully faithful on free-monodromic tilting sheaves.

(5)

For
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
,
\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
is a Soergel bimodule (see, for example,
[
8
, Theorem 11.8(1)]
for the definition). Let
SBim
be the category of Soergel
\mathcal{R}
-bimodules. Then
\mathbb{V}|_{\textup{Tilt}}
(shorthand for the restriction of
\mathbb{V}
to
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
) upgrades to a monoidal functor

(8.5)

\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}:\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})\to\textup{SBim}.

Proof.

(1)We only need to check that
\mathbb{V}({\operatorname{IC}}_{w})=0
for
w\neq e
and
\mathbb{V}({\operatorname{IC}}_{e})\cong{\mathbf{k}}
. If
w\neq e
, then
{\operatorname{IC}}_{w}
is pulled back from either
X\times G/P
or
G/P\times X
(where
P
is a parabolic subgroup that is not a Borel). In this case, the singular support of
{\operatorname{IC}}_{w}
along the diagonal lies in the pullback of the conormal to the diagonal of
X\times G/P
or
G/P\times X
, hence the generic vanishing cycle along the diagonal vanishes, i.e.,
\mathbb{V}({\operatorname{IC}}_{w})=0
. The isomorphism
\mathbb{V}({\operatorname{IC}}_{e})\cong{\mathbf{k}}
is clear since
{\operatorname{IC}}_{e}
is the constant sheaf on the diagonal.

(2) See
[
8
, Lemma 6.9(2)]
.

(3) See
[
8
, Theorem 9.1]
.

(4) See
[
8
, Proposition 11.2]
.

(5) See
[
8
, Theorem 11.8(1)]
.
∎

Proposition 8.8
.

The functor
\mathbb{V}
carries a canonical monoidal structure with respect to the convolution product on
\mathcal{H}_{G}
and the tensor product
(-)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}}(-)
on
D^{b}({\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod-}{\mathcal{R}})
.

Proof.

First we construct a functorial isomorphism

c_{\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}:\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{K}\star\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\cong\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{K})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})

for
\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
. Recall
\mathcal{K}\star\mathcal{K}^{\prime}=\operatorname{pr}_{13*}(\operatorname{pr}_{12}^{*}\mathcal{K}\otimes\operatorname{pr}_{23}^{*}\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
. Let
\mathcal{G}=\operatorname{pr}_{12}^{*}\mathcal{K}\otimes\operatorname{pr}_{23}^{*}\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
. Let
\widetilde{\Delta}_{13}=\operatorname{pr}_{13}^{-1}(\widetilde{X}_{e}^{2})\subset\widetilde{X}^{3}
. Let
\pi_{13}:T^{*}_{\widetilde{\Delta}_{13}}(\widetilde{X}^{3})\to T^{*}_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}(\widetilde{X}^{2})
be the natural projection. By Proposition
8.11
(1), we have

\pi_{13*}(\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta}_{13}}\mathcal{G})\cong\mu_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}\operatorname{pr}_{13*}\mathcal{G}.

Recall that
\Lambda\subset T^{*}(\widetilde{X}^{2})
is the union of conormals of
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{w}
. Now observe that
SS(\mathcal{G})\subset(\Lambda\times 0_{\widetilde{X}})+(0_{\widetilde{X}}\times\Lambda)
(here we apply
[
16
, Proposition 5.4.14(i)]
, using that
(\Lambda\times 0_{\widetilde{X}})\cap(0_{\widetilde{X}}\times\Lambda)
is contained in the zero section). This implies that
\pi_{13}^{-1}(\Xi^{-}\times\Xi)\cap SS(\mathcal{G})\subset\Xi^{-}\times 0_{\pi^{-1}(x)}\times\Xi\subset T^{*}_{\widetilde{\Delta}}(\widetilde{X}^{3})
, where
\widetilde{\Delta}\subset\widetilde{X}^{3}
is the preimage of the small diagonal
\Delta(X)\hookrightarrow X^{3}
. Therefore

(8.6)

\pi_{13,\Xi*}(\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta}}(\mathcal{G})|_{\Xi^{-}\times 0_{\pi^{-1}(x)}\times\Xi})\cong(\mu_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}\operatorname{pr}_{13*}\mathcal{G})|_{\Xi^{-}\times\Xi}=\mathbb{V}_{(-\xi,\xi)}(\mathcal{K}\star\mathcal{K}^{\prime}).

Here
\pi_{13,\Xi}:\Xi^{-}\times 0_{\pi^{-1}(x)}\times\Xi\to\Xi^{-}\times\Xi
is the projection.

Let
\delta_{23}:\widetilde{X}^{3}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}^{4}
be the diagonal embedding of the middle two factors. Let
\overline{\delta}_{23}
be the composition
\widetilde{X}^{3}\xrightarrow{\delta_{23}}\widetilde{X}^{4}\to\widetilde{X}^{2}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2})
. Then
\mathcal{G}=\overline{\delta}^{*}_{23}(\mathcal{K}\boxtimes\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
, where we view
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\in D^{b}_{G}(\widetilde{X}^{2})
. Now we are ready to apply Proposition
8.11
(2) (together with Remark
8.12
) to the smooth map
\overline{\delta}_{23}
, the submanifold
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e})\subset\widetilde{X}^{2}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2})
, and its preimage
\widetilde{\Delta}\subset\widetilde{X}^{3}
under
\overline{\delta}_{23}
.
There is a canonical embedding induced from
\overline{\delta}_{23}
(which is an isomorphism in conormal fibers)

\overline{\delta}_{23}^{\natural}:T^{*}_{\widetilde{\Delta}}(\widetilde{X}^{3})\to T^{*}_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e})}(\widetilde{X}^{2}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2})).

By Proposition
8.11
(2), we have

(8.7)

\overline{\delta}_{23}^{\natural*}(\mu_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}\mathcal{K}\boxtimes\mu_{G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}\mathcal{K}^{\prime})=\delta_{23}^{\natural*}\mu_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e})}(\mathcal{K}\boxtimes\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta}}(\mathcal{G}).

Since
\overline{\delta}^{\natural}_{23}
factors as the composition

\overline{\delta}_{23}^{\natural}:T^{*}_{\widetilde{\Delta}}(\widetilde{X}^{3})\xrightarrow{\delta^{\natural}_{23}}T^{*}_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}\times\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e})}(\widetilde{X}^{2}\times\widetilde{X}^{2})\to T^{*}_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e})}(\widetilde{X}^{2}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2}))

where the second map is the quotient by
G
, we can rewrite (
8.7
) as an isomorphism (viewing
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\in D^{b}(\widetilde{X}^{2})
)

\delta_{23}^{\natural*}(\mu_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}\mathcal{K}\boxtimes\mu_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\cong\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta}}(\mathcal{G}).

Restricting both sides to
\Xi^{-}\times 0_{\pi^{-1}(x)}\times\Xi
we get

\displaystyle\delta_{23,\Xi}^{\natural*}(\mathbb{V}_{(-\xi,\xi)}(\mathcal{K})\boxtimes\mathbb{V}_{(-\xi,\xi)}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{23,\Xi}^{\natural*}(\mu_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}\mathcal{K}|_{\Xi^{-}\times\Xi}\boxtimes\mu_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}\mathcal{K}^{\prime}|_{{}_{\Xi^{-}\times\Xi}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta}}(\mathcal{G})|_{\Xi^{-}\times 0_{\pi^{-1}(x)}\times\Xi}.

where
\delta^{\natural}_{23,\Xi}:\Xi^{-}\times 0_{\pi^{-1}(x)}\times\Xi\hookrightarrow\Xi^{-}\times\Xi\times\Xi^{-}\times\Xi
is the restriction of
\delta^{\natural}_{23}
. Combined with (
8.6
) we get a canonical isomorphism

(8.8)

\mathbb{V}_{(-\xi,\xi)}(\mathcal{K}\star\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\cong\pi_{13,\Xi*}\delta_{23,\Xi}^{\natural*}(\mathbb{V}_{(-\xi,\xi)}(\mathcal{K})\boxtimes\mathbb{V}_{(-\xi,\xi)}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})).

Identifying
\Xi^{-}
,
\Xi
and
\pi^{-1}(x)
with
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
using the base points
\pm\widetilde{\xi}
and
\widetilde{x}
, (
8.8
) becomes

(8.9)

\mathbb{V}_{(-\widetilde{\xi},\widetilde{\xi})}(\mathcal{K}\star\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\cong\dot{\operatorname{pr}}_{13*}\dot{\delta}_{23}^{*}(\mathbb{V}_{(-\widetilde{\xi},\widetilde{\xi})}(\mathcal{K})\boxtimes\mathbb{V}_{(-\widetilde{\xi},\widetilde{\xi})}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})).

where
\dot{\delta}_{23}:({\mathbf{T}}^{c})^{3}\hookrightarrow({\mathbf{T}}^{c})^{4}
is the diagonal embedding of the middle factors and
\dot{\operatorname{pr}}_{13}:({\mathbf{T}}^{c})^{3}\to({\mathbf{T}}^{c})^{2}
the projection. If we write
M=\mathbb{V}_{(-\widetilde{\xi},\widetilde{\xi})}(\mathcal{K})
and
M^{\prime}=\mathbb{V}_{(-\widetilde{\xi},\widetilde{\xi})}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
viewed as objects in
D^{b}(\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R}\otimes\mathcal{R})
, then the right side of (
8.9
) becomes
(M\otimes M^{\prime})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}_{23}}{\mathbf{k}}
, where the notation
\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}_{23}}{\mathbf{k}}
means
\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathbf{k}}
taken using the
\mathcal{R}
-action on
M\otimes M^{\prime}
given by
(m,m^{\prime})\cdot r=(m,m^{\prime})\cdot(1\otimes\delta(r)\otimes 1)
, where
\delta:\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{R}\otimes\mathcal{R}
is the comultiplication induced by the diagonal map
\pi_{1}({\mathbf{T}}^{c})\to\pi_{1}({\mathbf{T}}^{c})\times\pi_{1}({\mathbf{T}}^{c})
. Finally note canonical isomorphism of right
\mathcal{R}\otimes\mathcal{R}
-modules (recall
\tau
from (
8.3
))

(M\otimes M^{\prime})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}_{23}}{\mathbf{k}}\cong M\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathbb{L}}\tau M^{\prime}.

which induces a canonical isomorphism of right
\mathcal{R}
-bimodules

\tau((M\otimes M^{\prime})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}_{23}}{\mathbf{k}})\cong\tau M\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathbb{L}}\tau M^{\prime}.

Plugging into (
8.9
) we get the desired isomorphism
c_{\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}
. We omit the verification that
c_{\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}
satisfies the axioms of a monoidal structure on
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
.
∎

8.9.
Module structure on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}

We now establish the relation between
\mathbb{V},\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
and the
\star
-action.

Choose
\xi\in i\mathcal{N}^{*}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{O}_{{\text{\rm reg}}}
, and let
x\in O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
be the unique point in the Springer fiber
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}
. Choose a lifting
\widetilde{x}\in\pi^{-1}(x)
and let
\widetilde{\xi}=(\widetilde{x},\xi)\in T^{*}\widetilde{X}
. We use
\widetilde{\xi}
to define the functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}:=\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\widetilde{\xi}}
as in (
8.1
). On the other hand, we use
(-\widetilde{\xi},\widetilde{\xi})\in T^{*}(\widetilde{X}^{2})
to defined the functor
\mathbb{V}:={}_{-\widetilde{\xi}}\mathbb{V}_{\widetilde{\xi}}
as in (
8.4
).

Proposition 8.10
.

Under the above notations, the functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
intertwines the right
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
-action on
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
by
\star
and the right action of
D^{b}(\mathcal{R}\textup{-mod-}\mathcal{R})
on
D^{b}(\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R})
given by
(M,N)\mapsto M\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}}N
(for
M\in D^{b}(\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R})
and
N\in D^{b}(\mathcal{R}\textup{-mod-}\mathcal{R})
). I.e., there is a functorial isomorphism

(8.10)

\alpha_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{K}}:\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}}\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{K})\to\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{K}).

for
\mathcal{F}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
and
\mathcal{K}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
, such that the following diagram is commutative for any
\mathcal{F}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
and
\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{G}

(8.11)

\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}}\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{K}_{1})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}}\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{K}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{K}_{1}}\otimes{\rm id}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{K}_{1})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}}\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{K}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}}\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{K}_{1}\star\mathcal{K}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\otimes c_{\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{a_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{K}_{1}\star\mathcal{K}_{2}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{K}_{1}\star\mathcal{K}_{2})}

Proof.

We first construct a natural isomorphism (
8.10
) for
\mathcal{F}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
and
\mathcal{K}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{K}\in\widehat{D}^{b}(\widetilde{X}\times\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}
. We know that
SS(\mathcal{F})\subset\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}
(hence
SS(\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F})\subset\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}\times 0_{\widetilde{X}}\subset T^{*}(\widetilde{X}^{2})
), and
SS(\mathcal{K})\subset\Lambda
. One checks that
(\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}\times 0_{\widetilde{X}})\cap\Lambda
is contained in the zero section of
T^{*}(\widetilde{X}^{2})
. By
[
16
, Proposition 5.4.14(i)]
,
SS(\mathcal{G})=SS(\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{K})
is contained in the pointwise addition
\Lambda^{\prime}:=(\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}\times 0_{\widetilde{X}})+\Lambda\subset T^{*}(\widetilde{X}^{2})
. The cone
\Lambda^{\prime}
consists of
((\widetilde{x}_{1},v-w),(\widetilde{x}_{2},w))\in T^{*}(\widetilde{X})^{2}
(where
\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2}\in\widetilde{X}
with image
x_{1},x_{2}\in X
,
v,w\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}
) such that
w\in{}^{x_{1}}\mathfrak{b}^{\bot}\cap{}^{x_{2}}\mathfrak{b}^{\bot}
and
v\in i\mathcal{N}^{*}(\mathbb{R})\cap{}^{x_{1}}\mathfrak{b}^{\bot}
.

By definition,
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{K})
is the restriction of the microlocalization
\mu_{\lambda_{0}}(\operatorname{pr}_{2*}\mathcal{G})
to
\Xi=\mu^{-1}(\xi)\subset T^{*}_{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\widetilde{X}
. By Proposition
8.11
(1), we consider

\textstyle{T^{*}_{\widetilde{X}\times\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}(\widetilde{X}^{2})}
\textstyle{0_{\widetilde{X}}\times T^{*}_{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}(\widetilde{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}
\textstyle{T^{*}_{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}(\widetilde{X})}

Let
\pi_{2,\Xi}:0_{\widetilde{X}}\times\Xi\to\Xi
be the second projection, then we have

(8.12)

\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\xi}(\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{K})\cong(\pi_{2*}(\mu_{\widetilde{X}\times\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{G}))|_{\Xi}\cong\pi_{2,\Xi*}((\mu_{\widetilde{X}\times\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{G})|_{0_{\widetilde{X}}\times\Xi}).

Now
SS(\mathcal{G})\subset\Lambda^{\prime}
. We claim that

(8.13)

\Xi^{\prime}:=\Lambda^{\prime}\cap(0_{\widetilde{X}}\times\Xi)=\{(\widetilde{x}_{1},0),(\widetilde{x}_{2},\xi))|\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2}\in\pi^{-1}(x)\}.

Indeed, a point in
\Lambda^{\prime}
takes the form
((\widetilde{x}_{1},v-w),(\widetilde{x}_{2},w))
. Intersecting with
0_{\widetilde{X}}\times\Xi
means imposing
w=\xi
and
v=w
, which also forces
x_{1}=x_{2}=x
since
w=\xi\in{}^{x_{1}}\mathfrak{b}^{\bot}\cap{}^{x_{2}}\mathfrak{b}^{\bot}
. The projection to
\widetilde{X}^{2}
gives an isomorphism
\Xi^{\prime}\cong\pi^{-1}(x)^{2}
.

Now
(\mu_{\widetilde{X}\times\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{G})|_{0_{\widetilde{X}}\times\Xi}
is supported in
\Xi^{\prime}
. Let
\widetilde{\Delta}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}\subset\widetilde{X}^{2}
be the preimage of the diagonal
\Delta(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}})\subset X^{2}
under the projection
\pi^{2}:\widetilde{X}^{2}\to X^{2}
. By (
8.13
) we have
\Xi^{\prime}\subset T^{*}_{\widetilde{\Delta}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}(\widetilde{X}^{2})
. Therefore

(\mu_{\widetilde{X}\times\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{G})|_{\Xi^{\prime}}\cong(\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{G})|_{\Xi^{\prime}}

and (
8.12
) gives an isomorphism

(8.14)

\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\xi}(\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{K})\cong\operatorname{pr}_{2,\Xi*}((\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{G})|_{\Xi^{\prime}})

where
\operatorname{pr}_{2,\Xi}:\Xi^{\prime}\to\Xi
is the projection.

To compute
(\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{G})|_{\Xi^{\prime}}
, we consider the diagonal embedding
\delta_{12}:\widetilde{X}^{2}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}^{3}
given by
(\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2})\mapsto(\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2})
. Let
\overline{\delta}_{12}:\widetilde{X}^{2}\to\widetilde{X}^{3}\to\widetilde{X}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2})
, where the second map is the quotient map on the second and third factors. Then
\mathcal{G}=\overline{\delta}_{12}^{*}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{K})
, where we view
\mathcal{K}\in D^{b}_{G}(\widetilde{X}^{2})
. We are ready to apply Proposition
8.11
(2) (together with Remark
8.12
) to the smooth map
\overline{\delta}_{12}
, the submanifold
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e})\subset\widetilde{X}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2})
, and its preimage under
\overline{\delta}_{12}
which is
\widetilde{\Delta}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}\subset\widetilde{X}^{2}
.
We have a canonical map induced by
\overline{\delta}_{12}
which is an isomorphism on conormal fibers:

T^{*}_{\widetilde{\Delta}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}(\widetilde{X}^{2})\xrightarrow{\overline{\delta}_{12}^{\natural}}T^{*}_{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e})}(\widetilde{X}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2})).

By Proposition
8.11
(2), we have a canonical isomorphism

(8.15)

\overline{\delta}^{\natural*}_{12}(\mu_{\lambda_{0}}\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mu_{G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}\mathcal{K})\cong\delta_{12}^{\natural*}\mu_{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e})}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{K})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{G}.

Since
\overline{\delta}_{12}^{\natural}
factors as the composition

T^{*}_{\widetilde{\Delta}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}(\widetilde{X}^{2})\xrightarrow{\delta_{12}^{\natural}}T^{*}_{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}\times\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}(\widetilde{X}^{3})\to T^{*}_{\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e})}(\widetilde{X}\times(G\backslash\widetilde{X}^{2}))

where the second map is the quotient map by
G
, we can rewrite (
8.15
) as an isomorphism

(8.16)

\delta^{\natural*}_{12}(\mu_{\lambda_{0}}\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mu_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}\mathcal{K})\cong\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{G}.

Here, when writing
\mu_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}\mathcal{K}
, we are viewing
\mathcal{K}
as an object in
D^{b}(\widetilde{X}^{2})
. Recall
\Xi^{-}=\mu^{-1}(-\xi)
. Then
\delta_{12}^{\natural}(\Xi^{\prime})=(\Xi\times_{\widetilde{X}}\Xi^{-})\times\Xi
, and let
\delta^{\natural}_{12,\Xi}:\Xi^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\Xi\times\Xi^{-}\times\Xi
be the induced embedding. Restricting (
8.16
) to
\Xi^{\prime}
we get

\delta^{\natural*}_{12,\Xi}((\mu_{\lambda_{0}}\mathcal{F})|_{\Xi}\boxtimes(\mu_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}\mathcal{K})|_{\Xi^{-}\times\Xi})\cong(\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{G})|_{\Xi^{\prime}}.

Combined with (
8.14
) we get a canonical isomorphism in
\widehat{D}^{b}(\Xi)_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}

(8.17)

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\xi}(\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{K})\cong\operatorname{pr}_{2,\Xi*}\delta^{\natural*}_{12,\Xi}((\mu_{\lambda_{0}}\mathcal{F})|_{\Xi}\boxtimes(\mu_{\widetilde{X}^{2}_{e}}\mathcal{K})|_{\Xi^{-}\times\Xi})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{pr}_{2,\Xi*}\delta^{\natural*}_{12,\Xi}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\xi}(\mathcal{F})\boxtimes\mathbb{V}_{(-\xi,\xi)}(\mathcal{K})).

If we identify
\Xi
and
\Xi^{-}
with
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
using the liftings
\widetilde{\xi}=(\widetilde{x},\xi)\in\Xi
and
-\widetilde{\xi}=(\widetilde{x},-\xi)\in\Xi^{-}
, then
\delta_{12}^{\natural}
becomes the diagonal embedding
\dot{\delta}_{12}:{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
into the first two factors, and
\operatorname{pr}_{2,\Xi}
becomes the second projection
\dot{\operatorname{pr}}_{2}:{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
. Then (
8.17
) becomes a canonical isomorphism in
\widehat{D}^{b}({\mathbf{T}}^{c})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}\cong D^{b}(\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R})

(8.18)

\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\widetilde{\xi}}(\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{K})\cong\dot{\operatorname{pr}}_{2*}\dot{\delta}_{12}^{*}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\widetilde{\xi}}(\mathcal{F})\boxtimes\mathbb{V}_{(-\widetilde{\xi},\widetilde{\xi})}(\mathcal{K}))\cong\dot{\operatorname{pr}}_{2*}(\dot{\operatorname{pr}}_{1}^{*}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\widetilde{\xi}}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathbb{V}_{(-\widetilde{\xi},\widetilde{\xi})}(\mathcal{K})).

Let
M=\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\widetilde{\xi}}(\mathcal{F})\in D^{b}(\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R})
,
N\in\mathbb{V}_{(-\widetilde{\xi},\widetilde{\xi})}(\mathcal{K})\in D^{b}(\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R}\otimes\mathcal{R})
, hence
\tau N={}_{-\widetilde{\xi}}\mathbb{V}_{\widetilde{\xi}}(\mathcal{K})\in D^{b}(\mathcal{R}\textup{-mod-}\mathcal{R})
(see (
8.3
) for
\tau
). Then
\dot{\operatorname{pr}}_{2*}(\dot{\operatorname{pr}}_{1}^{*}M\otimes N^{\prime})\cong(M\otimes N^{\prime})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}_{12}}{\mathbf{k}}
where
\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}_{12}}{\mathbf{k}}
means the functor
\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathbf{k}}
taken using the following right
\mathcal{R}
-module structure on
M\otimes N^{\prime}
:
(m\otimes n^{\prime})\cdot r=(m\otimes n)\cdot\delta(r)
, where
\delta:\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{R}\otimes\mathcal{R}
is the comultiplication induced by the diagonal map
\pi_{1}({\mathbf{T}}^{c})\to\pi_{1}({\mathbf{T}}^{c})\times\pi_{1}({\mathbf{T}}^{c})
. Here we are using the fact that the pushforward
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\to\textup{pt}
of monodromic sheaves corresponds to the functor
(-)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathbf{k}}:D^{b}(\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{R}})\to D^{b}(\textup{mod-}{\mathbf{k}})
. It is easy to see there is a canonical isomorphism of right
\mathcal{R}
-modules (using the second
\mathcal{R}
-action on
N
and the right
\mathcal{R}
-action on
\tau N
)

(M\otimes N)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathbf{k}}\cong M\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{{\mathcal{R}}}\tau N.

Therefore the right side of (
8.18
) is canonically isomorphic to
M\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{{\mathcal{R}}}\tau N=\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\widetilde{\xi}}(\mathcal{F})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{{\mathcal{R}}}{}_{-\widetilde{\xi}}\mathbb{V}_{\widetilde{\xi}}(\mathcal{K})
as a right
\mathcal{R}
-module. This completes the construction of
\alpha_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{K}}
.

The argument for checking the commutativity of (
8.11
) is similar. We omit it.

∎

We record here the functoriality properties of microlocalization from
[
16
]
that we used in the above proof. Let
f:Y\to X
be a map of manifolds,
M\subset X
a closed submanifold and
N=f^{-1}(M)
. Then there is a natural correspondence between the conormals

\textstyle{T^{*}_{N}Y}
\textstyle{N\times_{M}(T^{*}_{M}X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\natural}_{N}}
\scriptstyle{df_{N}}
\textstyle{T^{*}_{M}X}

We have the microlocalization functors
\mu_{M}:D^{b}(X)\to D^{b}(T^{*}_{M}X)
and
\mu_{N}:D^{b}(Y)\to D^{b}(T^{*}_{N}Y)
.

Proposition 8.11
.

(1)

(
[
16
, Proposition 4.3.4]
) Assume that
f
is transversal to
M
(i.e.
df_{N}
above is an isomorphism). Let
\mathcal{G}\in D^{b}(Y)
and suppose that
f|_{\operatorname{supp}(\mathcal{G})}
is proper, then there is a canonical isomorphism

\mu_{M}(f_{*}\mathcal{G})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}f^{\natural}_{N*}df^{*}_{N}\mu_{N}(\mathcal{G}).

(2)

(
[
16
, Proposition 4.3.5]
) Assume
f
is smooth (which again implies that
df_{N}
above is an isomorphism). Let
\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}(X)
. Then there is a canonical isomorphism

df_{N*}f^{\natural,*}_{N}\mu_{M}(\mathcal{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mu_{N}(f^{*}\mathcal{F}).

Remark 8.12
.

In the arguments for Proposition
8.8
and Proposition
8.10
, we applied Proposition
8.11
(2) in the stacky situation
X=H\backslash\widetilde{X}
for a smooth manifold
\widetilde{X}
with a Lie group
H
acting, and a smooth map
f:Y\to X
. Let
\widetilde{M}\subset\widetilde{X}
be an
H
-stable submanifold and
M=H\backslash\widetilde{M}
,
N=f^{-1}(M)
. Then
\mu_{M}:D^{b}(X)=D^{b}_{H}(\widetilde{X})\to D^{b}(T^{*}_{M}X)=D^{b}_{H}(T^{*}_{\widetilde{M}}\widetilde{X})
is defined, and Proposition
8.11
(2) holds as stated. To deduce this stacky version, we simply apply Proposition
8.11
(2) to the map
\widetilde{f}:\widetilde{Y}\to\widetilde{X}
obtained from
f
by base change along
\pi_{X}:\widetilde{X}\to X
, the submanifold
\widetilde{M}
,
\widetilde{N}=\widetilde{f}^{-1}(\widetilde{M})
and the sheaf
\pi^{*}_{X}\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}(\widetilde{X})
.

9.
Structure theorem for nice quasi-split groups

In this section we assume
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split. In this section we will prove our main result (Theorem
9.5
, Theorem
9.21
and Corollary
9.22
). It is a generalization of Soergel’s Struktursatz to quasi-split real groups satisfying a technical condition called
nice
(which in particular includes quasi-split groups of adjoint type).

9.1.
The algebra
{\mathcal{A}}

We choose
\xi\in i{\mathcal{N}}^{*}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{O}_{{\text{\rm reg}}}
and
\widetilde{\xi}\in T^{*}\widetilde{X}
over
\xi
, and define the functor
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}=\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}},\widetilde{\xi}}
.

We define
{\mathcal{A}}:=\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\textup{Tilt}})^{opp}
for the opposite ring of the endomorphism ring of the functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\textup{Tilt}}
(shorthand for the restriction of
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
to
\mathrm{Tilt}({{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}})
). For an element
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
and a tilting sheaf
{\mathcal{T}}
we put
a_{\mathcal{T}}\in\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}))
for the (right) action of
a
on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})
. Then
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
upgrades to an exact functor

(9.1)

\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sharp}:\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})\to\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{A}}.

To state the extension of Soergel’s structure theorem for real groups, we need to single out a class of quasi-split groups.

Definition 9.2
.

Let
G_{\mathbb{R}}
be a quasi-split connected reductive group over
\mathbb{R}
. We say that
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is
nice
, if for every real parabolic subgroup
P_{\mathbb{R}}\subset G_{\mathbb{R}}
whose Levi factor
L_{\mathbb{R}}
satisfies
L^{\operatorname{ad}}_{\mathbb{R}}\cong{\rm PGL}_{2,\mathbb{R}}
, the map on real points
L(\mathbb{R})\to L^{ad}(\mathbb{R})
is surjective.

The following lemma ensures that in particular adjoint quasi-split groups are nice.

Lemma 9.3
.

Assume
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split, and
H^{1}(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R},Z(G))=1
. Then for any real parabolic
P\subset G
and its Levi factor
L
, the map
L(\mathbb{R})\to L^{ad}(\mathbb{R})
is surjective. In particular,
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is nice.

Proof.

Let
B_{0}
is a Borel subgroup defined over
\mathbb{R}
,
T_{0}\subset B_{0}
is a maximally split real Cartan, and
L
is a standard Levi subgroup with respect to
B_{0}
, i.e., it is the Levi subgroup of a real parabolic subgroup containing
B_{0}
. To show
L(\mathbb{R})\to L^{ad}(\mathbb{R})
is surjective, it suffices to prove that the Galois cohomology
H^{1}(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R},Z(L))
vanishes.

Let
S
be the set of simple roots in
\Phi(G,T_{0})
with respect to the real Borel
B_{0}
. Then
\sigma
acts on
S
by involution. By our assumption,
Z(L)\subset T_{0}
is the vanishing locus of a
\sigma
-stable subset
J^{\prime}\subset S
of simple roots, hence
Z(L)
fits into an exact sequence

1\to Z(G)\to Z(L)\to(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{S-J^{\prime}}\to 1.

Since
\sigma
permutes
S
, the real structure on
(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{S-J^{\prime}}
is a product of
R_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
(one for each nontrivial orbit of
\sigma
on
S-J^{\prime}
), and
\mathbb{G}_{m}
(one for each fixed point of
\sigma
on
S-J^{\prime}
). Since
H^{1}(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R},R_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{m})=1
and
H^{1}(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R},\mathbb{G}_{m})=1
, and
H^{1}(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R},Z(G))
by assumption, we conclude that
H^{1}(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R},Z(L))=1
.
∎

Example 9.4
.

Suppose
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is of the form
\textup{SU}(p,q)/\Gamma
where
|p-q|\geq 1
, and
\Gamma\subset\mu_{p+q}
is a subgroup. If
p+q
is odd, then all such
G_{\mathbb{R}}
are nice. If
p+q
is even, then
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is nice if and only if
\Gamma
contains
-1
.

Theorem 9.5
.

Suppose
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split and nice. Then the functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sharp}
is fully faithful.

The proof will be completed in Section
9.17
.

9.6.
The algebra
\mathcal{A}_{0}

Recall
{\mathcal{R}}=\widehat{{\mathbf{k}}[\pi_{1}({\mathbf{T}}^{c})]}
is the group algebra of the fundamental group of
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
completed at the augmentation ideal. The monodromy action along the fibers of
\pi
induces a ring map

(9.2)

\varphi_{\mathcal{R}}:{\mathcal{R}}\to Z({\mathcal{A}})

to the center of
{\mathcal{A}}
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
be the subalgebra of endomorphisms of
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\textup{Tilt}}
commuting with the Hecke action. More precisely,
a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
if for any
{\mathcal{T}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
and
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
, the endomorphism
a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}}
of
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\cong\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
is equal to
a_{{\mathcal{T}}}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})}
.

Lemma 9.7
.

(1)

The image of
{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}
under the map
\varphi_{{\mathcal{R}}}
lies in
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
.

(2)

Recall the big tilting object
{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
. Let
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
. Then
a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
if and only if for any
{\mathcal{T}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
, the endomorphism
a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}}
of
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\cong\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})
is equal to
a_{{\mathcal{T}}}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})}
.

Proof.

(1) Let
b\in{\mathcal{R}}
and
a=\varphi_{{\mathcal{R}}}(b)\in Z({\mathcal{A}})
. Let
{\mathcal{T}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
and
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
. Then
a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}}
is induced from the action of
b
on
{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
by right
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-monodromy on
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
. We know that the left and right monodromy actions on
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
factor through
{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
(as it is so for
\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
(Proposition
8.7
(
3
)) and
\mathbb{V}|_{\textup{Tilt}}
is fully faithful (Proposition
8.7
(
4
))). Therefore, if
b\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}
, then the right monodromy action of
b
on
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
is the same as left monodromy, and the induced action on
{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
is the same as the action of
b
on
{\mathcal{T}}
by (right)
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
-monodromy. Therefore, both
a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}}
and
a_{{\mathcal{T}}}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})}
on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\cong\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
are given by the action of
b\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}
on the first factor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})
. This shows
a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
.

(2) Suppose
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies
a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}}=a_{{\mathcal{T}}}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})}
for all
{\mathcal{T}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
. Let
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
, we want to show that
a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}}=a_{{\mathcal{T}}}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})}
as endomorphisms of
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\cong\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
. We have a canonical map

(9.3)

\epsilon:\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\to\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})

sending
f\otimes v
to the image of
v\in\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})
under the map
\mathbb{V}(f):\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\to\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
. By Proposition
8.7
(
2
),
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\cong\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
and
\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\cong\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})
. Take
{\rm id}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}}\in\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\cong\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})
, we have
\epsilon(f\otimes{\rm id}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}})=f
for any
f\in\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\cong\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
