. This shows that
\epsilon
is surjective. The similarly defined map

\epsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}:\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\to\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})

can be identified with

\epsilon\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})}:\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\to\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}),

hence is also surjective. By the definition of
{\mathcal{A}}
, we have a commutative diagram

(9.4)

\textstyle{\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\otimes a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})}

Rewriting
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})
as
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})
and
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
as
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
, we similarly we have a commutative diagram

(9.5)

\textstyle{\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\otimes a_{{\mathcal{T}}}\otimes{\rm id}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{{\mathcal{T}}}\otimes{\rm id}}
\textstyle{\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})}

Now compare (
9.4
) and (
9.5
), the left vertical maps are equal in the two diagrams by assumption. Since the horizontal maps are equal and surjective, the right vertical maps are also equal in the two diagrams, i.e.,
a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}}=a_{{\mathcal{T}}}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})}
.
∎

By Lemma
9.7
, we have a ring homomorphism

\varphi:{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\to{\mathcal{A}}

where the image of
{\mathcal{R}}
is central.

Proposition 9.8
.

The ring map
\varphi
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We construct a map in the other direction as follows. Let
w_{0}\in W
be the longest element, and
{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
be the indecomposable free-monodromic tilting object with full support. It is known (Theorem 9.1 in
[
8
]
, Proposition 4.7.3 1) in
[
10
]
) that
\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})={\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
. Consider the functor
\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbb{R}}}:\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})\to{\mathcal{R}}\textup{-mod-}{\mathcal{R}}
given by
\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\mathcal{T}):=\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{T}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})
, with the two
{\mathcal{R}}
-actions given by the left and right monodromy actions on
{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}
. Using Proposition
8.7
(
3
)

(9.6)

\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{T}})\cong\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{T})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\cong\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}

with the first copy of
{\mathcal{R}}
acting on
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{T}})
and the second copy acting on the second tensor factor
{\mathcal{R}}
by multiplication.
From (
9.6
) we see that
\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits(\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbb{R}}})^{opp}\cong{\mathcal{A}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
.

We have an action of
{\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
: for
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{T}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
,
a
acts on
\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{T}})
by
a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}}
. This gives a ring map

\psi^{\prime}:{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits(\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbb{R}}})^{opp}\cong{\mathcal{A}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}.

Claim
.

The image of
\psi^{\prime}
is contained in
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
.

Proof of Claim.

Using the characterization of
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
given in Lemma
9.7
(
2
),
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
consists exactly of those
b\in\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits(\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbb{R}}})
such that
b_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}}=b_{{\mathcal{T}}}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})}
. Therefore, to show
\psi^{\prime}(a)\in{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
for
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
, we need to check for any
{\mathcal{T}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
,

(9.7)

a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{w_{0}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}}=a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{w_{0}})}\in\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{w_{0}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}})).

Here, to distinguish two copies of
{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}
we denote them by
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{w_{0}}
and
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}
. Consider the map
\beta:\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}^{\prime}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}
such that
\mathbb{V}(\beta)
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}

\displaystyle a\otimes b

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle a\otimes 1\otimes b.

Then
\beta
induces
{\rm id}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\star\beta:{\mathcal{T}}\star\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}\to{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}^{\prime}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}
, hence a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{V}({\rm id}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\star\beta)}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}^{\prime}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{w_{0}}\star\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{V}({\rm id}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\star\beta)}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}^{\prime}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}})}

Using the description of
\mathbb{V}(\beta)
, we see that the above diagram can be written as

\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\otimes 1\otimes{\rm id}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{w_{0}}\star\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\otimes 1\otimes{\rm id}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}}

This shows that (
9.7
) holds on the subspace
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes 1\otimes{\mathcal{R}}\subset\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}=\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}^{\prime}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}})
. Since both
a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{w_{0}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}}
and
a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{w_{0}})}
are linear with respect to the three
{\mathcal{R}}
-actions on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}^{\prime}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}})
, we conclude that (
9.7
) holds.
∎

By the Claim, we have a map

\psi:\mathcal{A}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}.

Note that this map is
{\mathcal{R}}
-linear. We check that
\varphi
and
\psi
are inverse to each other. If
a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
, then
\psi\varphi(a)
acts on
\mathbb{U}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{T})=\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})
by
a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}}
. Since
a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
,
a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}}=a_{{\mathcal{T}}}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})}
, which implies
\psi\varphi(a)=a\otimes 1\in{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
. By
{\mathcal{R}}
-linearity, this implies
\psi\varphi={\rm id}
.

On the other hand, to check
\varphi\psi={\rm id}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
, it suffices to show that the composition

{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\psi^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\xrightarrow{m}{\mathcal{A}}

is the identity, where
m
is the multiplication map. We have a canonical map
\epsilon:{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}\to\tilde{\delta}
that induces the multiplication map
\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})={\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\to{\mathcal{R}}=\mathbb{V}(\tilde{\delta})
. It induces a natural transformation of functors
\gamma:\mathbb{U}_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
(
{\mathcal{R}}
-linear with respect to the second
{\mathcal{R}}
-action on
\mathbb{U}_{\mathbb{R}}
). Under the isomorphism
\mathbb{U}_{\mathbb{R}}\cong\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
,
\gamma
corresponds to the multiplication map
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\to\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
. Therefore for any
b\in\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits(\mathbb{U}_{\mathbb{R}})^{opp}\cong\mathcal{A}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}
, we have a commutative diagram for any
{\mathcal{T}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})

(9.8)

\textstyle{\mathbb{U}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{{\mathcal{T}}}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m(b)_{{\mathcal{T}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{U}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})}

On the other hand, by the definition of
{\mathcal{A}}
we have a commutative diagram

(9.9)

\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{{\mathcal{T}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w_{0}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{V}({\rm id}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\star\epsilon)}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{{\mathcal{T}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{U}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{V}({\rm id}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\star\epsilon)}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})}

Now taking
b=\psi^{\prime}(a)
in (
9.8
), it becomes the same diagram as (
9.9
), from which we conclude that
m(\psi^{\prime}(a))=m(b)=a
. This implies
m\psi^{\prime}={\rm id}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
hence
\varphi\psi={\rm id}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
and finishes the proof.
∎

Now we define an action of Soergel bimodules
SBim
on
\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{A}}
as follows:
M\in\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{A}}
and
N\in\textup{SBim}
, the action of
N
on
M
is the tensor product
M\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}N
. Now
M\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}N
is naturally a
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
-module using the (right)
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
-action on
M
and the right
{\mathcal{R}}
-action on
N
. Since
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathcal{A}}
by Proposition
9.8
, we may view
M\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}N
as a right
{\mathcal{A}}
-module.

The following is an immediate consequence of Proposition
8.10
, and the action defined above.

Corollary 9.9
.

The functors
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}
in (
9.1
) and
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}
in (
8.5
) intertwine the convolution action of
\mathrm{Tilt}({{\mathcal{H}}_{G}})
on
\mathrm{Tilt}({{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}})
and the action of
SBim
on
\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{A}}
defined above.

The following is parallel to Proposition
6.6
.

Lemma 9.10
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}_{s}={\mathcal{R}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}{\mathcal{R}}\in\textup{SBim}
for each simple reflection
s\in{\mathbf{W}}
. Then the action of
{\mathcal{B}}_{s}
on
\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{A}}
is self-adjoint: there is an isomorphism functorial in
M_{1},M_{2}\in\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{A}}

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(M_{1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{s},M_{2})\cong\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(M_{1},M_{2}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{s})

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
6.6
, it suffices to give unit and counit maps
u:{\mathcal{R}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{s}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{s}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{s}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{s}\to{\mathcal{R}}
in
SBim
satisfying identities analogous to (
6.25
). The maps
u
and
c
are given in the proof of Proposition
6.6
because
\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}_{s})\cong{\mathcal{B}}_{s}
.
∎

9.11.
Real Soergel functor on localized categories

We are in the setup of Section
7.3
. In particular, we fix
B_{0},T_{0}\supset A_{0}
, and a subset
J
of simple roots in
\Phi(G,A_{0})
. The real Soergel functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
is
{\mathcal{R}}
-linear and so we can define

\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}\colon\mathrm{Tilt}({{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}\to\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}.

Its endomorphism algebra is opposite to
{\mathcal{A}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}
and we also get the functor

\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}\colon\mathrm{Tilt}({{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}\to\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{A}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}.

They enjoy the following properties.

Lemma 9.12
.

The equivalence of Proposition
7.6
intertwines the localized real Soergel functors on both sides.

Proof.

We put
{\mathfrak{p}}\supset{\mathfrak{p}}_{\mathbb{R}}
and
{\mathfrak{l}}\supset{\mathfrak{l}}_{\mathbb{R}}
for the relevant Lie algebras. Let
x\in X_{L}
be a point in the closed orbit and let a regular nilpotent
\xi\in{\mathcal{N}}\cap i{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{*}
be the generic conormal to the closed orbit at
x
inside
X
. Since
x
lies in
X_{L}
, the corresponding Borel is contained in
P
and
\xi\in{\mathcal{N}}\cap{\mathfrak{p}}
we conclude that
\xi
is orthogonal to the nilpotent radical
{\mathfrak{p}}_{P}
of
{\mathfrak{p}}
and, thus,
p_{L}(\xi)
lands in
i{\mathfrak{l}}^{*}_{\mathbb{R}}
. Moreover,
p_{L}(\xi)
is regular inside
i{\mathfrak{l}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{*}
. Indeed, for a nonregular element
e_{0}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{L}
the elements in
e_{0}+{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}
are also not regular. By construction we can also match the fibers
\pi^{-1}(x)
and
\pi_{L}^{-1}(x)
together with the compact part of the stabilizers of
x
inside
G
and
L
. We conclude that for a sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
we have an isomorphism

\mu_{(x,\xi)}(\mathrm{Av}_{G(\mathbb{R})}\circ\widetilde{i}_{P,*}({\mathcal{F}}))\cong\mu_{(x,p_{L}(\xi))}({\mathcal{F}})

of free-monodromic local systems on
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
.

∎

Recall the ring
\mathcal{R}_{J}
from Section
7.9
.

Proposition 9.13
.

Assume that the localized Soergel functor
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}
is fully faithful on
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}
. Then
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}
is fully faithful on
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.8
and the fact that
V_{J}\subset V_{\lambda_{0}}
, the subcategory
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}
generates
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}
under the localized Hecke action (
7.4
). Therefore, by Proposition
6.6
it is sufficient to check that the map

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}\to\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J},\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{2})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J})

is an isomorphism for
{\mathcal{T}}_{2}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{K}}_{J}
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}w({\mathcal{K}}_{J})
, where
w({\mathcal{K}}_{J})\neq{\mathcal{K}}_{J}
is the localization of
V
at the generic point of
w(V_{J})\neq V_{J}
. As an
{\mathcal{R}}
-module
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2})
is supported on
V_{J}\cap w(V_{J})
and, therefore, vanishes after applying
-\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}
. Since
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}
decomposes into the direct sum, so does
{\mathcal{A}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}
. Since
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{2}
belong to the different summands of
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}
, the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}
acts on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1})
and
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{2})
through different direct summands. It follows that
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J}}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J},\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{2})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{J})=0
and we are done.
∎

In the codimension
0
case recall that
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{Q}}=\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{Q}\cong\prod_{\lambda\in I_{0}}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}
and consider the localized functors

\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Q}}:\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})_{{\mathcal{Q}}}\to\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{Q}}

and

\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Q}}:\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})_{{\mathcal{Q}}}\to\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{A}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{Q}.

We observe that

Lemma 9.14
.

For any
(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}_{0}
,
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi})\cong\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}
as an
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{Q}}
-module.

Proof.

The statement is clear for
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
. For general
\lambda\in I_{0}
we have
(\lambda,\chi)=(\lambda_{0},\chi^{\prime})\cdot w
for some
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
. By Lemma
7.12
,
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi})\cong\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda_{0},\chi^{\prime}}\star\widetilde{\Delta}_{w})
is the translation under
w
of
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda_{0},\chi^{\prime}})\cong\mathcal{K}
, which is
\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}
as an
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{Q}}
-module.
∎

We can now check the version of Theorem
9.5
for the localized categories.

Lemma 9.15
.

The functor
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Q}}
is fully-faithful.

Proof.

We need to check that for
{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
, the map

(9.10)

\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{Q}}\to\operatorname{RHom}_{{\mathcal{A}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{Q}}}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{Q}},\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{Q}})

is an isomorphism. Since the image of
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})\to{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{Q}}}
generates
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{Q}}}
by taking direct summands (for
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
is a direct summand of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\chi}
after localization), it suffices to check the case
{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
. By Proposition
6.8
, Proposition
6.6
and Corollary
9.9
we can reduce to the case where
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
is supported on
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
, hence we may assume
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}=\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda_{0},\psi}
.

Now instead of assuming
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
is a free-monodromic tilting sheaf, by Proposition
7.11
it suffices to treat the case
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}=\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
for some
\lambda\in I_{0}
. If
\lambda\neq\lambda_{0}
, then the left side of (
9.10
) is zero by Proposition
7.11
(2), and the right side vanishes for the same reason: the action of
{\mathcal{R}}
on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})
and
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})
has support contained in
\operatorname{Spec}\overline{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\lambda}\cap\operatorname{Spec}\overline{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\lambda_{0}}
, which has dimension less than
\dim{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
. If
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
then
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{F}}_{i})
is just the stalk of
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
at a point in
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
. If
\chi=\psi
, then both sides of (
9.10
) are isomorphically equal to
{\mathcal{K}}
. If
\chi\neq\psi
, then the left side is zero by Proposition
7.11
. By Lemma
9.14
we have
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})\cong\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda_{0},\psi})\cong\mathcal{K}_{\lambda_{0}}
. Since by Proposition
7.11
the objects
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}
and
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda_{0},\psi}
are two different simple objects of the semisimple category
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})_{{\mathcal{Q}}}
we have an element
a\in{\mathcal{A}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{Q}}
acting on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})
and
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda_{0},\psi})
by different elements of
{\mathcal{K}}
. This implies that the right hand side also vanishes. The statement now follows.

∎

The following lemma will allow us to transfer the results from the localized category to the original category.

Lemma 9.16
.

For a free-monodromic tilting object
{\mathcal{T}}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
, the
{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}
-action on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})
factors through the quotient
({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})
is torsion free as an
({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}
-module.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.2
, the action of
{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}
on
{\mathcal{T}}
factors through
({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}
, therefore so does its action on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}})
.

If
{\mathcal{L}}
is a free-monodromic local system supported on the closed orbit
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
and extended by zero to
\widetilde{X}
, then
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{L}})
is the same as the stalk of
{\mathcal{L}}
along
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
, hence a free
{\mathcal{S}}
-module. The
({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}
-action on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{L}})
comes from the inclusion
({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{S}}
, hence
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{L}})
is torsion free over
({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}
.

Now for any
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
,
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{L}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\cong\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{L}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
. Since
\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
is a Soergel bimodule, it is free as a left
{\mathcal{R}}
-module. Therefore, as an
({\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}
-module,
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{L}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}}\mathbb{V}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
is a direct sum of
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{L})
, which is again torsion free. Finally, by Proposition
6.8
each free-monodromic tilting object is the direct summand of one of
{\mathcal{L}}\star{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
, the statement holds for all free-monodromic tilting objects.
∎

9.17.
Proofs of Theorem
9.5

In this subsection we assume
G
is nice.

By Proposition
6.8
, Proposition
6.6
and Lemma
9.10
, to show the full faithfulness of
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sharp}
, it is sufficient to check that
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
induces and isomorphism

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2})\cong\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1}),\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{2}))

for
{\mathcal{T}}_{2}
supported on the preimage
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
of the closed orbit. Let
\widetilde{i}:\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}
be the inclusion. We may assume
{\mathcal{T}}_{2}={\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}\cong\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}
for some character
\chi
of
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda_{0}})
.

By adjunction we have

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})=\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}(\widetilde{i}_{*}\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})=\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}(\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi},{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}).

Here
(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi}
is the direct summand of
\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
where
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda_{0}})
acts by
\chi
.

We claim that the natural map

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}(\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi},{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})\to\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi}),\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}))

is an isomorphism. Indeed, it suffices to replace
\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi}
by
{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}
and show
\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits({\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})\cong\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}))
. Now the left side is
{\mathcal{S}}
. As
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
is the stalk functor when restricted to local systems on
\widetilde{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
, the right side is
\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{S}})
where
{\mathcal{S}}
is viewed as an
{\mathcal{A}}
-module via the
{\mathcal{R}}
-algebra homomorphism

\textup{ev}_{\chi}:{\mathcal{A}}\to\overline{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\lambda_{0}}={\mathcal{S}}.

given by the action of
{\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})\cong{\mathcal{S}}
. Since
{\mathcal{R}}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{S}}
,
\textup{ev}_{\chi}
is surjective, hence
\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{S}})={\mathcal{S}}
, which coincides with the left side.

We denote
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\widetilde{\nabla}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})
by
{\mathcal{S}}_{\chi}
to emphasize that it is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{S}}
as an
{\mathcal{R}}
-module, and
{\mathcal{A}}
acts on it via
\textup{ev}_{\chi}
. Therefore, it remains to check that the map

(9.11)

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi}),{\mathcal{S}}_{\chi})\to\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1}),{\mathcal{S}}_{\chi})

induced by the unit map
u:{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\to\widetilde{i}_{*}\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\to\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi}
is an isomorphism. The left side of (
9.11
) is

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{S}}}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi}),{\mathcal{S}})

since the action of
{\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi})
factors through
{\mathcal{S}}
via
\textup{ev}_{\chi}
. The right side of (
9.11
) can be identified with

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{S}}}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1})\otimes_{{\mathcal{A}},\textup{ev}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{S}}).

Therefore (
9.11
) comes from the map of
{\mathcal{S}}
-modules

\rho:\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1})\otimes_{{\mathcal{A}},\textup{ev}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi})

by taking the
{\mathcal{S}}
-linear dual. Since
{\mathcal{S}}
is regular, to show (
9.11
) is an isomorphism, it suffices to show

(9.12)

\ker(\rho)
is a torsion
{\mathcal{S}}
-module;

(9.13)

\textup{coker}(\rho)
has support of codimension
\geq 2
in
\operatorname{Spec}{\mathcal{S}}=V_{\lambda_{0}}
.

To check (
9.12
), we localize
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
at the generic point of
V_{\lambda_{0}}=\operatorname{Spec}{\mathcal{S}}\subset\operatorname{Spec}{\mathcal{R}}=V
as in Section
7.10
and use Lemma
9.15
. We conclude that both
\ker(\rho)
and
\textup{coker}(\rho)
are torsion
{\mathcal{S}}
-modules.

To check (
9.13
), we first observe that the cokernel of
\rho
is supported on
\cup_{\lambda\in I_{1}}(V_{\lambda}\cap V_{\lambda_{0}})
as an
{\mathcal{S}}
-module, where

I_{1}=\{\lambda\in I|n_{\lambda}=n_{\lambda_{0}}-1\}.

Indeed, let
{\mathcal{K}}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}}
fit into the distinguished triangle
{\mathcal{K}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\to\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi}\to{\mathcal{K}}[1]
. Then
{\mathcal{K}}
is a successive extension of
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\psi}
where
(\lambda,\psi)\neq(\lambda_{0},\chi)
. Taking
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
, using that
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1})
is concentrated in degree
0
, we get an exact sequence

0\to H^{0}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{K}})\to\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1})\to\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi})\to H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{K}})\to 0.

We have
\textup{coker}(\rho)=H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{K}})
. As an
{\mathcal{R}}
-module, the support of
H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{K}})
is contained in the union of supports of
H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\psi})
, which can be nonzero only when
\lambda\in I_{1}
by Proposition
8.4
. Therefore,
\operatorname{supp}_{{\mathcal{R}}}(H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{K}}))\subset\cup_{\lambda\in I_{1}}V_{\lambda}
. Since
H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{K}})
is also a quotient of
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi})
which is supported on
V_{\lambda_{0}}
as an
{\mathcal{R}}
-module, we conclude that
\textup{coker}(\rho)=H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{K}})
is supported on
\cup_{\lambda\in I_{1}}(V_{\lambda}\cap V_{\lambda_{0}})
as an
{\mathcal{S}}
-module.

Therefore, to show (
9.13
), it suffices to show that for any
\lambda\in I_{1}
such that
V_{\lambda}\subset V_{\lambda_{0}}
, letting
\eta_{\lambda}
be the generic point of
V_{\lambda}\subset V_{\lambda_{0}}
and
\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\eta_{\lambda}}
be the completed local ring of
{\mathcal{S}}
at
\eta_{\lambda}
, the map

(9.14)

\widehat{\rho}_{\eta_{\lambda}}=\rho\otimes_{{\mathcal{S}}}{\rm id}_{\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\eta_{\lambda}}}:\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1})\otimes_{{\mathcal{A}},\textup{ev}_{\chi}}\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\eta_{\lambda}}\to\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{1})_{\chi})\otimes_{{\mathcal{S}}}\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\eta_{\lambda}}

is surjective.

Proposition
7.6
, Lemma
9.12
and Proposition
9.13
allow to reduce the localized statement to the case of Levi subgroup
L=L_{J}
with
J
a subset of simple roots cutting out
V_{\lambda}\subset V_{\lambda_{0}}
. Such Levi is quasi-split and
L^{ad}
has split rank
1
. Our general strategy for dealing with
L
is to relate it to the case of the adjoint group
L^{ad}
, making use of the niceness assumption of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
, and then deal with quasi-split adjoint groups of split rank
1
case by case.

Let
T_{L}=L/L^{der}
so that
L
fits into an exact sequence

1\to\Gamma_{L}\to L\to L^{ad}\times T_{L}\to 1.

Here
\Gamma_{L}=Z(L^{der})
. We are going to use the discussions in Section
10.1
, Section
10.3
and Section
10.7
. Let
\omega:\Gamma_{L}(\mathbb{R})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
and extend it to
\widetilde{\omega}:\Gamma_{L}\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
, which corresponds to a rank one local system
\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
on
({\mathbf{T}}^{ad})^{c}\times T_{L}^{c}
. By (
10.9
) we get

{\mathcal{M}}^{\omega}_{L_{\mathbb{R}}}\cong({\mathcal{M}}_{L^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}}\times T_{L,\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}})^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{L}}

where
{\mathfrak{S}}_{L}
is dual to the cokernel
(L^{ad}(\mathbb{R})\times T_{L}(\mathbb{R}))\mathrm{Im}(L(\mathbb{R}))
. The irreducible
T_{L}(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant local systems on
T_{L}^{c}
that appear in
{\mathcal{M}}_{T_{L,\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
are either empty, in which case there is nothing to prove, or are indexed by a
\pi_{0}(T_{L}(\mathbb{R}))^{*}
-torsor
\Xi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
. We have

(9.15)

{\mathcal{M}}_{L^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}}\times T_{L,\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\simeq\bigoplus_{\chi\in\Xi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}{\mathcal{M}}_{L^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}}\widehat{\otimes}\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}

where
\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
is the completed group ring of
\pi_{1}(T_{L}^{c}/\mathrm{Im}(T_{L}(\mathbb{R})))
.

By our assumption of niceness,
L(\mathbb{R})\to L^{ad}(\mathbb{R})
is surjective (although the niceness assumption only involves the case
L^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}}\cong{\rm PGL}_{2,\mathbb{R}}
, the surjectivity is automatic in all other cases as
L^{ad}(\mathbb{R})
will be connected). Therefore we have a surjection
\pi_{0}(T_{L}(\mathbb{R}))\twoheadrightarrow{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}
, and dually an injection
{\mathfrak{S}}\hookrightarrow\pi_{0}(T_{L}(\mathbb{R}))^{*}
. The
{\mathfrak{S}}
-action on
{\mathcal{M}}_{L^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}}\times T_{L,\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
corresponds to the permutation of summands on the right side of (
9.15
) via the injective map
{\mathfrak{S}}\hookrightarrow\pi_{0}(T_{L}(\mathbb{R}))^{*}
and the
\pi_{0}(T_{L}(\mathbb{R}))^{*}
-action on
\Xi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
. Therefore, taking
{\mathfrak{S}}
-invariants we get

(9.16)

{\mathcal{M}}^{\omega}_{L_{\mathbb{R}}}\cong\bigoplus_{\chi\in\Xi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}/{\mathfrak{S}}}{\mathcal{M}}_{L^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}}\widehat{\otimes}\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}.

This allows us to reduce the proof of the surjectivity of
\rho
for
L
to the same statement in each summand of (
9.16
), which is clearly equivalent to the surjectivity of
\rho
in the case of
L^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Thus we reduce to showing the surjectivity of
\rho
for indecomposable tilting sheaves
\mathcal{T}_{1}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}}
,
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is adjoint and quasi-split of split rank one (so
G_{\mathbb{R}}\cong\textup{PU}(2,1),R_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}{\rm PGL}_{2}
or
{\rm PGL}_{2,\mathbb{R}}
), and
\mathcal{L}
is a rank one local system on
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
extendable to
G
.

Lemma 9.18
.

Under the above assumptions on
G_{\mathbb{R}}
, the map
\rho
is surjective.

Proof.

We should go over the cases of Section
5.8
. In each case, we need to show that for any indecomposable tilting
\mathcal{T}_{1}
, the restriction map to the closed orbit induces a surjection

\widetilde{\rho}:\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\mathcal{T}_{1})\to\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\widetilde{i}_{*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{1})_{\chi})

for any character
\chi
of
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda_{0}})
.

If
G_{\mathbb{R}}=R_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}{\rm PGL}_{2}
, we have
G={\rm PGL}_{2}\times{\rm PGL}_{2}
. The
\mathcal{L}
that makes
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}}\neq 0
is either trivial or nontrivial on both factors of
G
. In both cases,
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}}
is equivalent to the Hecke category
{\mathcal{H}}_{{\rm PGL}_{2}}
, and the surjectivity of
\rho
is well-known.

If
G_{\mathbb{R}}={\rm PGL}_{2,{\mathbb{R}}}
and
\mathcal{L}
is nontrivial, all objects in
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}}
are supported on the closed orbit, in which case there is nothing to prove. Below we assume
\mathcal{L}
is trivial and use notations from Section
5.8
. The functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
is equal to
\ker(s_{\mathrm{triv}}+s_{\mathrm{sgn}})
. We should consider the case
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}={\mathcal{T}}_{h}
. We have
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{0,\chi})=\ker({\mathbf{k}}[[x]]\oplus{\mathbf{k}}[[x]]\to{\mathbf{k}})
. On the other hand,
\widetilde{i}_{0,*}(\widetilde{i}^{*}_{0}{\mathcal{T}}_{h})\simeq{\mathcal{T}}_{0,\textup{triv}}\oplus{\mathcal{T}}_{0,\operatorname{sgn}}
. For
\chi=\textup{triv},\operatorname{sgn}
we have
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{0,\chi})={\mathbf{k}}[[x]]
. The map
\widetilde{\rho}:\ker({\mathbf{k}}[[x]]\oplus{\mathbf{k}}[[x]]\to{\mathbf{k}})\to{\mathbf{k}}[[x]]
is induced by projection on one of the summands. We conclude that it is indeed surjective.

Consider the case
G_{\mathbb{R}}=\textup{PU}(2,1)
. If
\mathcal{L}
is nontrivial, by looking at the stabilizers of orbits we see that
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}}=0
. Below we consider the case
\mathcal{L}
is trivial. When
\mathcal{T}_{1}
is not supported on the open orbit, the calculation is the same as in the case of
{\rm PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, which we omit. Therefore we assume that the support of
\mathcal{T}_{1}
is open, i.e.,
\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{\lambda}
for
\lambda\in\{-|+-,+|++,+|+-\}
. Using notation
\mathcal{C}_{\lambda}=\ker(\mathcal{T}_{\lambda}\to\widetilde{i}_{*}\widetilde{i}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{\lambda})
, we reduce to show that
H^{>0}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{C}_{\lambda})=0
. Short exact sequences (
5.9
), (
5.10
), (
5.11
) after applying
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
yield long exact sequences of cohomology. They imply in particular
H^{>1}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{C}_{\lambda})=0
and a surjection
H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda})\twoheadrightarrow H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{C}_{\lambda})
. For
\lambda=-|+-
, using the local model of a transversal slice to the closed orbit, we see that
SS({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda})|_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
is fiberwise one-dimensional (in the conormal direction of the real hypersurface
\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{0|+-}
). Therefore
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{\lambda})=0
. This implies
H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{C}_{\lambda})=0
. Similarly argument works for
\lambda=+|++
.

In case of
\lambda=+|+-
, using (
5.11
) and Proposition
8.4
we get the exact sequence:

H^{0}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{+|+0}\oplus{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{0|+-})\to H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{+|+-})\to H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{C}_{+|+-})\to 0.

It is therefore sufficient to verify the surjectivity of the first map. Since
{\widetilde{\Delta}}_{+|+-}
is the pullback of
\Delta_{+|+-}
on
X
, we have
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\widetilde{\Delta}}_{+|+-})\cong\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\Delta_{+|+-})
. Hence it suffices to show the surjectivity of the map

H^{0}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\Delta_{+|+0}\oplus\Delta_{0|+-})\to H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\Delta_{+|+-}),

where the standard sheaves are now on
X
. The latter map coincides with the boundary homomorphism obtained by applying
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
to the standard filtration of
\nabla_{+|+-}
, which we know to be surjective as
H^{1}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\nabla_{+|+-})=0
by Proposition
8.4
.

∎

This finishes the proof of (
9.13
), and completes the argument for Theorem
9.5
.

9.19.
The algebras
{\mathcal{B}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}

In this subsection we assume
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split but not necessarily nice.

Let
\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}=\{\chi:\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda_{0}})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}\}
, the set of rank one
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant local systems on the closed orbit
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
. We have an action of
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
on
\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
by the cross action. The group
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
acts on
\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
via the cross action. It also acts on
{\mathcal{S}}
compatibly with the action of
{\mathbf{W}}
on
\mathcal{R}
. Define an action of
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
on
\oplus_{\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{S}
as follows: for
f\in\mathcal{S}
put in summand
\chi
, denoted
f_{\chi}
,
w(f_{\chi}):=(wf)_{\chi\cdot w^{-1}}
for
w\in{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
. Let

{\mathcal{B}}_{0}=(\bigoplus_{\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{S})^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}.

By definition,
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
is an
{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
-algebra. View it as an
\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}
-algebra via the natural map
\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}\to{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
. Define another algebra

{\mathcal{B}}:={\mathcal{B}}_{0}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}.

Remark 9.20
.

The algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
is related to the block variety of
[
9
]
in the following way. The orbits of
\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
under the cross action of
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
are called blocks. This coincides with the notion of blocks for
(\mathfrak{g},K)
-modules with a fixed regular integral infinitesimal character, see
[
9
, Claim 2.2]
. Then
{\mathcal{B}}
defined above is the direct product of completions of the algebras of functions on the block varieties
\mathfrak{B}_{mon}
from
[
9
, Remark 2.3]
for all blocks.

The action of
{\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})
for
\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
gives a homomorphism

\textup{act}_{\lambda_{0}}:{\mathcal{A}}\to\bigoplus_{\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}))^{opp}=\bigoplus_{\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{S}.

Here we use that
\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}))^{opp}\cong\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{S})^{opp}=\mathcal{S}
.

Recall from Proposition
9.8
that we have
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{A}}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}{\mathcal{R}}
, where the subalgebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
is exactly the endomorphisms commuting with the Hecke action.

Theorem 9.21
.

Let
G_{\mathbb{R}}
be any quasi-split real group. The map
\textup{act}_{\lambda_{0}}
restricts to an
\mathcal{S}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
-algebra isomorphism
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
. In particular, we have an isomorphism of
\mathcal{R}
-algebras
\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{B}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.8
the map
\textup{act}_{\lambda_{0}}
restricted to
\mathcal{A}_{0}
is injective: if
a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
acts by zero on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})
for all
\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
, it will act by zero on all
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}\star\mathcal{T}_{w})
for all
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
, which contain
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\psi})
as a direct summand for all
(\lambda,\psi)\in\widetilde{I}
, hence
a=0
. In particular,
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
is torsion-free as an
\mathcal{S}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
-module.

Let us prove that
\textup{act}_{\lambda_{0}}
sends
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
to the
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
-invariants of
\oplus_{\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{S}
. Since
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
is
\mathcal{S}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
-torsion free, it suffices to show the statement after tensoring with
\mathcal{Q}
. In fact we will show that
\textup{act}_{\lambda_{0}}
induces an isomorphism

(9.17)

\textup{act}_{\lambda_{0},\mathcal{Q}}:\mathcal{A}_{0,\mathcal{Q}}:=\mathcal{A}_{0}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{Q}\to(\oplus_{\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{Q})^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}=\mathcal{B}_{0,\mathcal{Q}}.

Consider the localized category
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})_{\mathcal{Q}}
under the action of the base-changed Hecke category
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})_{\mathcal{Q}}
. Then
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{Q}}:=\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{S}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}}\mathcal{Q}
is the endomorphism ring of the functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Q}}:\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})_{\mathcal{Q}}\to\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{Q}}
, and
\mathcal{A}_{0,\mathcal{Q}}
is the subalgebra of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{Q}}
commuting with the Hecke action. By Lemma
9.14
we can compute
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{Q}}
explicitly as

(9.18)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{Q}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\bigoplus_{(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}_{0}}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda},

where the
(\lambda,\chi)
-factor is the action of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{Q}}
on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Q}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi})\cong\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}
. By Lemma
7.12
, the part of
\mathcal{A}
that commutes with the Hecke action corresponds to the
{\mathbf{W}}
-invariants of the right side of (
9.18
) under the cross action. Since
{\mathbf{W}}
acts transitively on
I_{0}
, we may rewrite the
{\mathbf{W}}
-invariants of the right side as
(\oplus_{\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}}\mathcal{K})^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}=\mathcal{B}_{0,\mathcal{Q}}
. This proves (
9.17
). In particular,
\textup{act}_{\lambda_{0}}
restricts to a ring homomorphism

\textup{act}^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}:\mathcal{A}_{0}\to\mathcal{B}_{0}

that is injective and becomes an isomorphism after tensoring with
\mathcal{Q}
.

It remains to show that
\textup{act}^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
is surjective. Given a collection
b=(b_{\chi})_{\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}
, we would like to construct
a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
that acts on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})=\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})
by
b_{\chi}
. For
\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
and
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
, define an endomorphism
a_{\chi,w}
of
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w})\cong\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{T}_{w})
by
b_{\chi}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{T}_{w})}
. For any morphism
\varphi:{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi^{\prime}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w^{\prime}}
in
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
, we claim that the following diagram is commutative

(9.19)

\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{a_{\chi,w}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\varphi)}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi^{\prime}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{\chi^{\prime},w^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi^{\prime}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w^{\prime}})}

Indeed, after tensoring with
{\mathcal{Q}}
the above diagram is commutative since
\mathcal{A}_{0,\mathcal{Q}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{B}_{0,\mathcal{Q}}
. Since
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi^{\prime}}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w^{\prime}})
is torsion-free as an
(\mathcal{S}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}})^{\prime}
-module by Lemma
9.16
, the diagram is commutative. Let
\textup{Tilt}^{\prime}\subset\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
be the full subcategory whose objects are finite direct sums of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}\star{\mathcal{T}}_{w}
for various
\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
and
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
. By the commutativity of (
9.19
), the collection
\{a_{\chi,w}\}_{\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}},w\in{\mathbf{W}}}
gives an endomorphism of
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\textup{Tilt}^{\prime}}
. By Proposition
6.8
, all objects in
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
are direct summands of objects in
\textup{Tilt}^{\prime}
, therefore the
\{a_{\chi,w}\}
defines an endomorphism of
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
, i.e., an element
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
. By construction,
a
acts on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi})
by
b_{\chi}
, and
a
commutes with the Hecke action. Therefore
a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
satisfies
\textup{act}_{\lambda_{0}}(a)=b
.

∎

Combining Theorem
9.5
and Theorem
9.21
, we get:

Corollary 9.22
.

Suppose
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split and nice. The enhanced real Soergel functor gives a fully faithful embedding

\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sharp}:\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})\to\textup{mod-}\mathcal{B}.

10.
Structure theorem for general quasi-split groups

In this section we state and prove the Soergel structure theorem for general quasi-split groups without assuming they are nice. To treat the general case we first need to consider variants of the previous setup: allowing non-unipotent monodromy along
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
and fixing a central character.

We put ourselves in the situation of Section
5.1
.

10.1.
Twisted version of
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}

We recall the notion of sheaves on
\widetilde{X}
equivariant under
{\mathbf{T}}
with respect to a local system, following
[
19
, §2.1, 2.5]
.

Let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a rank one local system on
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
with finite monodromy (in
{\mathbf{k}}
-coefficients). Such local systems all come from the following construction: take a finite isogeny
\nu\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
and a character
\kappa\colon\ker(\nu)\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
, then take
{\mathcal{L}}
to be the rank one direct summand of
\nu_{*}\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}
where
\ker(\nu)
acts via
\kappa
.

Consider the equivariant category
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X}/\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{c})
with
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
acting on
\widetilde{X}
through
\nu
. Since the finite abelian group
\ker(\nu)
acts trivially on
X
, it acts on the identity functor of
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X}/\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{c})
. This allows us to decompose
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X}/\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{c})
into a direct sum of full triangulated subcategories according to
characters of
\ker(\nu)
. Let
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(X)_{{\mathcal{L}}}
be the direct summand of
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X}/\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{c})
corresponding to
\kappa
. One can check that
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(X)_{{\mathcal{L}}}
depends only on the local system
\mathcal{L}
on
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
and not on the choices of the isogeny
\nu:\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
and
\kappa
.

Let
\textup{LS}_{\lambda,\mathcal{L}}
be the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant local systems on
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
that appear in
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(X)_{{\mathcal{L}}}
. We describe
\textup{LS}_{\lambda,\mathcal{L}}
more explicitly. Choose a finite isogeny
\nu\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
such that
{\mathcal{L}}
corresponds to a character
\kappa\colon\ker(\nu)\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}=\nu^{-1}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})
. Then there is a natural bijection

(10.1)

\textup{LS}_{\lambda,\mathcal{L}}\cong\{\widetilde{\chi}:\pi_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times};\widetilde{\chi}|_{\ker(\nu)}=\kappa\}.

In particular,
\textup{LS}_{\lambda,\mathcal{L}}\neq\varnothing
if and only if the restriction of
\kappa
to the kernel of the map
\ker(\nu)\subset\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})
is trivial; and when this happens,
\textup{LS}_{\lambda,\mathcal{L}}
is a torsor under
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})^{*}
.

We define the monodromic category
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X})_{({\mathbf{T}}^{c},{\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{mon}}
as the full subcategory of
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X})
generated by the image of
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(X)_{{\mathcal{L}}}
via pullback. Finally, we can define the completed version

{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}}:=\widehat{D}^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(\widetilde{X})_{({\mathbf{T}}^{c},{\mathcal{L}})\textup{-mon}}

as in Section
2
.

We define the
{\mathcal{L}}
-twisted version of the algebra
\mathcal{B}
as follows. Recall
\lambda_{0}\in I
is the index of the closed
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit. Let

\mathcal{B}_{0,\mathcal{L}}=(\bigoplus_{\widetilde{\chi}\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0},\mathcal{L}}}\mathcal{S})^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}},

and let

\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{L}}=\mathcal{B}_{0,\mathcal{L}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}.

Applying the twisting construction to the setting of Section
6.1
we define
{\mathcal{L}}
-twisted monoidal Hecke category

{\mathcal{H}}_{G,{\mathcal{L}}}:=\widehat{D}^{b}_{G}(\widetilde{X}\times\widetilde{X})_{({\mathbf{T}}^{c}\times{\mathbf{T}}^{c},{\mathcal{L}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{L}}^{-1})-\mathrm{mon}}.

This is equivalent to the one defined in
[
19
, §2.7]
.

In the following we are only interested in the case where
{\mathcal{L}}
extends to a rank one local system
\mathcal{L}_{G}
on
G
. This happens if and only if one can choose a finite isogeny
\nu^{\prime}:\widetilde{G}\to G
inducing the isogeny of abstract Cartan groups
\nu:\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{c}
such that
{\mathcal{L}}
is attached to some character
\kappa
of
\ker(\nu)
. In this case, tensoring with
\mathcal{L}_{G}
induces a monoidal equivalence

(10.2)

{\mathcal{H}}_{G}\simeq{\mathcal{H}}_{G,{\mathcal{L}}}.

The following theorem generalizes Theorem
9.5
and Corollary
9.22
with the same proof (where we use (
10.2
)).

Theorem 10.2
.

Let
G_{\mathbb{R}}
be quasi-split and nice. Let
\mathcal{L}
be a rank one local system on
{\mathbf{T}}
that extends to
G
. Then the real Soergel functor naturally lifts to a fully faithful embedding

\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}:\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}})\to\textup{mod-}\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{L}}.

10.3.
Central characters

Let
\Gamma\subset ZG(\mathbb{R})
be a finite subgroup. Let
\omega:\Gamma\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
be a character. We define
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})(\Gamma,\omega)}(X)
to be the full subcategory of
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})\Gamma}(X)
consisting of objects
\mathcal{F}
on which
\Gamma
acts via the character
\omega
(note that
\Gamma
acts trivially on
X
, hence acts on every
\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})\Gamma}(X)
). Similarly define
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})(\Gamma,\omega)}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})
for each
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
.

For
\lambda\in I
, the group
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}
contains the compact part of
Z(G)
, hence contains
\Gamma
. Therefore, the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible local systems in
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})(\Gamma,\omega)}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})
is in natural bijection with the set of characters

(10.3)

\textup{LS}_{\lambda}^{\omega}:=\{\chi:\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times};\chi|_{\Gamma}=\omega\}.

In particular,
\textup{LS}_{\lambda}^{\omega}\neq\varnothing
if and only if
\omega
is trivial on the kernel of the map
\Gamma\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda})
; and when this happens,
\textup{LS}_{\lambda}^{\omega}
is a torsor under the dual group
\textup{coker}(\Gamma\to\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{\lambda}))^{*}
.

We define
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})(\Gamma,\omega)}(\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}
to be the full subcategory of
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})\Gamma}(\widetilde{X})
generated by the pullbacks of
D^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})(\Gamma,\omega)}(X)
. We can define the completed version as in Section
2
:

{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{\omega}:=\widehat{D}^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})(\Gamma,\omega)}(\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}.

Note that
\textup{LS}_{\lambda}^{\omega}
is stable under the cross action of
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda}
. Define
{\mathcal{B}}^{\omega}_{0}=(\oplus_{\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}^{\omega}}{\mathcal{S}})^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
, and
{\mathcal{B}}^{\omega}={\mathcal{B}}_{0}^{\omega}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{A}^{\omega}
be opposite to the endomorphism ring of
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{\omega})}
. Then Theorem
9.21
implies an isomorphism
\mathcal{A}^{\omega}\cong\mathcal{B}^{\omega}
. In particular,
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{\omega})}
is enhanced to a functor

(10.4)

\mathbb{V}^{\sharp,\omega}_{\mathbb{R}}:\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{\omega})\to\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}^{\omega}.

However, this functor is not fully faithful in general. To remedy, we will modify this functor by replacing the right side with an equivariant and twisted version of
\mathcal{B}
for the adjoint group of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
.

10.4.
De-equivariantization

We recall the procedure of de-equivariantization, as explained in
[
12
, 21 and 22]
. Let
\Gamma
be a finite abelian group such that
|\Gamma|
is prime to
\text{ch}({\mathbf{k}})
. Assume
{\mathbf{k}}
contains enough roots of unity such that
\Gamma^{*}=\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\Gamma,{\mathbf{k}}^{\times})
has the same cardinality as
\Gamma
. Assume
\Gamma
acts on a
{\mathbf{k}}
-linear idempotent complete category
\mathcal{C}
. Let
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}^{\Gamma}
be the category of
\Gamma
-equivariant objects in
\mathcal{C}
: an object
d\in\mathcal{D}
is tuple
(c,\{\alpha_{\gamma}\}_{\gamma\in\Gamma})
where
X\in\mathcal{C}
and
\alpha_{\gamma}:\gamma(c)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}c
are isomorphisms indexed by
\gamma\in\Gamma
satisfying
\alpha_{1}={\rm id}_{c}
and
a_{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}=\alpha_{\gamma_{1}}\circ\gamma_{1}(\alpha_{\gamma_{2}})
. Then there is an action of the dual group
\Gamma^{*}
on
\mathcal{D}
as follows: for
\chi\in\Gamma^{*}
and
d=(c,\{\alpha_{\gamma}\})\in\mathcal{D}
, define
\chi(d)=(c,\{\alpha^{\prime}_{\gamma}\})
where
\alpha^{\prime}_{\gamma}
is
\alpha_{\gamma}
multiplied by
\langle{\chi,\gamma}\rangle\in{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
. Then we can recover
\mathcal{C}
as the category of
\Gamma^{*}
-equivariant objects in
\mathcal{D}
. We give the functors as follows. There is a functor
\textup{av}_{\Gamma}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
sending
c
to the object
\textup{av}_{\Gamma}(c)=\oplus_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\gamma(c)
with its obvious
\Gamma
-equivariant structure. Then
\textup{av}_{\Gamma}(c)\in\mathcal{D}
in fact carries a canonical
\Gamma^{*}
-equivariant structure and hence lifts to an object
\textup{av}_{\Gamma}(c)^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{D}^{\Gamma^{*}}
. The assignment
c\mapsto\textup{av}_{\Gamma}(c)^{\sharp}
gives a functor
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}^{\Gamma^{*}}
. On the other hand, let
(d,\{\alpha_{\chi}\}_{\chi\in\Gamma^{*}})\in\mathcal{D}^{\Gamma^{*}}
, with
d=(c,\{\alpha_{\gamma}\}_{\gamma\in\Gamma})\in\mathcal{D}
. The data of
\alpha_{\chi}
means automorphisms
\beta_{\chi}\in\mathop{\sf Aut}\nolimits(c)
that form a
\Gamma^{*}
-action on
c
compatible with
\{\alpha_{\gamma}\}_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
. In particular, we can extract the direct summand
c_{1}=c^{\Gamma^{*}}\subset c
corresponding to the trivial character of
\Gamma^{*}
. The assignment
(d,\{\alpha_{\chi}\}_{\chi\in\Gamma^{*}})\mapsto c
gives a functor
\mathcal{D}^{\Gamma^{*}}\to\mathcal{C}
. These two functors are inverse to each other and give an equivalence
\mathcal{C}\cong\mathcal{D}^{\Gamma^{*}}
.

10.5.
Monodromic categories for isogenous groups

We first treat a more general situation and then see how we can apply it to the non-adjoint group case.

Consider a short exact sequence of real algebraic groups

1\xrightarrow{}\Gamma\xrightarrow{}G_{1}\xrightarrow{p}G_{2}\xrightarrow{}1

with
\Gamma
being finite. Fix a character
\omega\in\Gamma({\mathbb{R}})^{*}
and choose an extension
\widetilde{\omega}
to
\Gamma
. Let
\pi_{i}\colon\widetilde{X}_{i}\to X_{i}
be the respective flag varieties and let
{\mathbf{T}}_{i}
be the respective abstract Cartan groups. Finally, put
{\mathfrak{S}}
for the dual group of the cokernel of the map
p({\mathbb{R}})\colon G_{1}({\mathbb{R}})\to G_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
.

We have the canonical equivalence of categories given by the forgetful functor from right to left

(10.5)

{\mathcal{M}}^{\omega}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}=\widehat{D}^{b}_{G_{1}({\mathbb{R}})(\Gamma(\mathbb{R}),\omega)}(\widetilde{X}_{1})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{1}\textup{-mon}}\simeq\widehat{D}^{b}_{G_{1}({\mathbb{R}})(\Gamma,\widetilde{\omega})}(\widetilde{X}_{1})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{1}\textup{-mon}}.

Here the centrally twisted categories are defined in Section
10.3
.

Let
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\omega}}
be the rank
1
local system on
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{2}
defined by the finite isogeny
p|_{{\mathbf{T}}_{1}^{c}}:{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{1}\to{\mathbf{T}}_{2}^{c}
and the character
\widetilde{\omega}
on
\Gamma=\ker(p|_{{\mathbf{T}}_{1}^{c}})
. Note that
\mathcal{L}
becomes trivial after pulling back to
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{1}
.

Lemma 10.6
.

Pullback along
p
induces an equivalence

(10.6)

\widehat{D}^{b}_{p(G_{1}({\mathbb{R}}))}(\widetilde{X}_{2})_{({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{2},\mathcal{L})\textup{-mon}}\simeq\widehat{D}^{b}_{G_{1}({\mathbb{R}})(\Gamma,\widetilde{\omega})}(\widetilde{X}_{1})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{1}\textup{-mon}}.

Proof.

It suffices to check the same statement before completion. By definition,
D^{b}_{G_{1}({\mathbb{R}})(\Gamma,\widetilde{\omega})}(\widetilde{X}_{1})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{1}\textup{-mon}}\subset D^{b}_{G_{1}(\mathbb{R})\Gamma}(\widetilde{X}_{1})
is the full subcategory generated by pullbacks of objects in
D^{b}_{G_{1}({\mathbb{R}})(\Gamma,\widetilde{\omega})}(X_{1})
. On the other hand, pullback along
p
identifies
D^{b}_{p(G_{1}({\mathbb{R}}))}(\widetilde{X}_{2})_{({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{2},\mathcal{L})\textup{-mon}}
with the full subcategory of
D^{b}_{G_{1}(\mathbb{R})\Gamma}(\widetilde{X}_{1})
generated by pullbacks from
D^{b}_{p(G_{1}(\mathbb{R}))}(X_{2})_{\mathcal{L}}
. It therefore suffices to identify simple perverse sheaves in
D^{b}_{p(G_{1}(\mathbb{R}))}(X_{2})_{\mathcal{L}}
and in
D^{b}_{G_{1}({\mathbb{R}})(\Gamma,\widetilde{\omega})}(X_{1})
.

Note that
X_{1}=X_{2}
. A
G_{1}(\mathbb{R})
-orbit
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}\subset X_{1}
is the same as a
p(G_{1}(\mathbb{R}))
-orbit on
X_{2}
. For such an orbit, the irreducible local systems in both
D^{b}_{G_{1}({\mathbb{R}})(\Gamma,\widetilde{\omega})}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})
and in
D^{b}_{p(G_{1}(\mathbb{R}))}(O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda})_{\mathcal{L}}
are parametrized by characters of
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}_{1,\lambda}^{c})
whose pullback to
\Gamma
is
\widetilde{\omega}
(compare (
10.1
) and (
10.3
)). This finishes the proof.
∎

Note that
G_{2}({\mathbb{R}})/p(G_{1}({\mathbb{R}}))\cong{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}
and, hence, there is an action of
{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}
on
\widehat{D}^{b}_{p(G_{1}({\mathbb{R}}))}(\widetilde{X}_{2})_{({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{2},\mathcal{L})\textup{-mon}}
, such that

(\widehat{D}^{b}_{p(G_{1}({\mathbb{R}}))}(\widetilde{X}_{2})_{({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{2},\mathcal{L})\textup{-mon}})^{{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}}\simeq\widehat{D}^{b}_{G_{2}({\mathbb{R}})}(\widetilde{X}_{2})_{({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{2},\mathcal{L})\textup{-mon}}={\mathcal{M}}_{G_{2,\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}}.

Applying the observations of Section
10.4
to this action, we have an action of
{\mathfrak{S}}
on
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{2,\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}}
and a canonical equivalence

(10.7)

\widehat{D}^{b}_{p(G_{1}({\mathbb{R}}))}(\widetilde{X}_{2})_{({\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{2},\mathcal{L})\textup{-mon}}\simeq({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{2,\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}})^{\mathfrak{S}}.

Combining (
10.5
), (
10.6
) and (
10.7
), we have verified that

(10.8)

{\mathcal{M}}^{\omega}_{G_{1,\mathbb{R}}}\simeq({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{2,\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}})^{\mathfrak{S}}.

We remark that this equivalence depends on the choice of the extension
\widetilde{\omega}
of
\omega
to
\Gamma
(which determines the local system
\mathcal{L}
on
{\mathbf{T}}^{c}_{2}
).

10.7.
Structure theorem for general quasi-split groups

Now let
G
be a connected reductive algebraic group over
\mathbb{C}
with real form
G_{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
X,\widetilde{X}
be defined in terms of
G
. Let
G^{ad}
be the adjoint form of
G
, which carries a real form
G_{\mathbb{R}}^{ad}
compatible with
G_{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
X^{ad},\widetilde{X}^{ad}
be defined as in Section
3.1
in terms of
G^{ad}
. In particular,
\pi^{ad}:\widetilde{X}^{ad}\to X^{ad}
is a
({\mathbf{T}}^{ad})^{c}
-torsor.

Let
T^{\prime}=G/G^{der}
be the abelianization of
G
. We apply the result of Section
10.5
to the short exact sequence

1\xrightarrow{}\Gamma\xrightarrow{}G\xrightarrow{p}G^{ad}\times T^{\prime}\xrightarrow{}1,

with
\Gamma=Z(G^{der)}
. Since

{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}=\bigoplus_{\omega:\Gamma(\mathbb{R})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\omega}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}},

we will consider one summand
{\mathcal{M}}^{\omega}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
at a time. So we fix a character
\omega:\Gamma(\mathbb{R})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
, and choose an extension of
\omega
to
\widetilde{\omega}:\Gamma\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
. The map
p
induces a short exact sequence
1\to\Gamma\to{\mathbf{T}}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{\operatorname{ad}}\times T^{\prime}\to 1
. Using this and
\widetilde{\omega}
we get a rank one local system
\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
on
({\mathbf{T}}^{\operatorname{ad}})^{c}\times T^{\prime c}
as in Section
10.1
. By (
10.8
) we get
an equivalence

(10.9)

{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{\omega}\simeq({\mathcal{M}}_{G^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}}\times T^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}})^{\mathfrak{S}},

where
{\mathfrak{S}}:=(G^{ad}(\mathbb{R})\times T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})/\mathop{\sf Im}\nolimits(G(\mathbb{R})))^{*}
.

The category on the right side is the completed tensor product of
{\mathcal{M}}_{G^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{T^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}=\widehat{D}^{b}_{T^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}})}(T^{\prime c})_{(T^{\prime c},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})\textup{-mon}}
. The latter category is zero if
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}|_{T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})}
is a nontrivial local system. If this happens then
\widehat{D}^{b}_{G(\mathbb{R})(\Gamma(\mathbb{R}),\omega)}(\widetilde{X})_{{\mathbf{T}}^{c}\textup{-mon}}
and there is nothing to prove. If
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}|_{T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})}
is a trivial local system, then irreducible local systems that appear in
{\mathcal{M}}_{T^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
are parametrized by a torsor
\Xi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
of
\pi_{0}(T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}))^{*}
. By Corollary
3.7
, we get a decomposition

{\mathcal{M}}_{T^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\simeq\bigoplus_{\chi\in\Xi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}D^{f}(\textup{mod-}\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime})

where
\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
is the completed group ring of
\pi_{1}(\overline{T}^{\prime c})
, and
\overline{T}^{\prime c}:=T^{\prime c}/\mathrm{Im}(T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}))
.

By definition,
{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}
is a quotient of
\pi_{0}(G^{ad}(\mathbb{R}))\times\pi_{0}(T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}))
. In particular, we have a homomorphism
\pi_{0}(T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}))\to{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}
, hence dually
{\mathfrak{S}}\to\pi_{0}(T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}))^{*}
. Therefore
{\mathfrak{S}}
acts on
\Xi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
via the map to
\pi_{0}(T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}))^{*}
. We thus get an
{\mathfrak{S}}
-equivariant equivalence

(10.10)

{\mathcal{M}}_{G^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}}\times T^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\simeq\bigoplus_{\chi\in\Xi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}{\mathcal{M}}_{G^{\operatorname{ad}}_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbf{k}}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}

where
{\mathfrak{S}}
permutes the summands according to its action on
\Xi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
.

By Corollary
9.22
or rather its generalization Theorem
10.2
, the enhanced real Soergel functor

\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}^{ad,\sharp}:\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}})\to\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}}

is fully-faithful. Here
\mathcal{B}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}}=\mathcal{B}_{0,\mathcal{L}}^{ad}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{ad,{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}^{ad}
is the algebra
\mathcal{B}
for the group
G^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}}
and the twisting
\mathcal{L}
. For a point
x
in the closed orbit
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
of
X^{ad}
we have a map
\pi_{0}(({\mathbf{T}}^{\operatorname{ad}}_{\lambda_{0}})^{c})\to\pi_{0}(G^{ad}(\mathbb{R}))\to{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}
. Dually,
{\mathfrak{S}}
acts on the set
\textup{LS}^{ad}_{\lambda_{0},\mathcal{L}}
of
G^{ad}(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant irreducible local systems on
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda_{0}}
that appear in
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}^{ad},\mathcal{L}}
. This yields a
{\mathfrak{S}}
-action on
{\mathcal{B}}^{ad}_{0,\mathcal{L}}
and on
{\mathcal{B}}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}}
, such that the functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}^{ad,\sharp}
respects the
{\mathfrak{S}}
action on
{\mathcal{M}}_{G^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}}}
and on
\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}}
.

We conclude that:

Theorem 10.8
.

Consider the functor
7
7

7

The notation emphasizes the dependence on the extension
\widetilde{\omega}
of
\omega
, which defines
\mathcal{L}
.

\mathbb{V}^{\sharp,\widetilde{\omega}}_{\mathbb{R}}\colon\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{\omega}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})\xrightarrow{}\left(\bigoplus_{\chi\in\Xi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}}\widehat{\otimes}\overline{{\mathcal{R}}}^{\prime}\right)^{{\mathfrak{S}}},

obtained by composing the equivalences (
10.9
), (
10.10
) and taking
\mathbb{V}^{ad,\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}\otimes{\rm id}_{\overline{{\mathcal{R}}}^{\prime}}
on each summand
{\mathcal{M}}_{G^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{L}}\widehat{\otimes}\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
. Here
{\mathfrak{S}}
acts simultaneously on
{\mathcal{B}}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}}
as explained above and permuting the summands via its action on
\Xi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
.

Then
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp,\widetilde{\omega}}_{\mathbb{R}}
is fully-faithful.

In particular, if
G
is semisimple (so that
\Gamma=Z(G)
and
T^{\prime}=1
), then the functor

(10.11)

\mathbb{V}^{\sharp,\widetilde{\omega}}_{\mathbb{R}}\colon\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{\omega}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})\to(\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}})^{{\mathfrak{S}}}

is fully faithful.

10.9.
Relation between
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp,\omega}_{\mathbb{R}}
and
\mathbb{V}^{ad,\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}

In this subsection we assume
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split and semisimple. In this case we want to describe
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}
more directly in terms of the vanishing cycles functors for the conormals to the closed
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit on
X
. Recall a choice of the generic conormal
\xi
to the open orbit and its lift
\widetilde{\xi}
made in Section
8.3
in order to define
\mathbb{V}^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}}=\mathbb{V}^{ad}_{{\mathbb{R}},\widetilde{\xi}}
. For an element
\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}
pick its lift to
G^{ad}(\mathbb{R})
and let
\sigma(\widetilde{\xi})
be the image of
\widetilde{\xi}
under the adjoint action of this lift. There is a natural
{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}
-action on the direct sum of functors
\bigoplus_{\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}}\mathbb{V}^{\sharp,\omega}_{{\mathbb{R}},\sigma(\widetilde{\xi})}
on
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{\omega}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
and each of the summands lands in
\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}_{\omega}
(see (
10.4
)).

Recall the descriptions of
\textup{LS}^{ad}_{\lambda_{0},\mathcal{L}}
and
\textup{LS}^{\omega}_{\lambda_{0}}
in (
10.1
) and (
10.3
). By Lemma
4.6
,
{\mathbf{T}}
carries a canonical real form
{\mathbf{T}}^{\sigma_{\lambda_{0}}}
such that
{\mathbf{T}}_{\lambda_{0}}^{c}=\textup{Im}({\mathbf{T}}^{\sigma_{\lambda_{0}}}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{c})
. We simply denote the real points of this real form by
{\mathbf{T}}(\mathbb{R})
, keeping in mind that it is the real form attached to the closed orbit. Similarly we define
{\mathbf{T}}^{ad}(\mathbb{R})
from the real form of
{\mathbf{T}}^{ad}
attached to the closed orbit.

Let
{\mathbf{T}}(\mathbb{R})^{\prime}\subset{\mathbf{T}}
be the preimage of
{\mathbf{T}}^{ad}(\mathbb{R})
under the projection
\nu:{\mathbf{T}}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{ad}
. Then we have a short exact sequence

1\to ZG\to{\mathbf{T}}(\mathbb{R})^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{ad}(\mathbb{R})\to 1.

Then
\textup{LS}^{ad}_{\lambda_{0},\mathcal{L}}
is the subset of
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}(\mathbb{R})^{\prime})^{*}
of characters whose restriction to the image of
ZG
is
\widetilde{\omega}
. On the other hand,
\textup{LS}^{\omega}_{\lambda_{0}}
is the subset of
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}(\mathbb{R}))^{*}
of characters whose restriction to the image of
ZG(\mathbb{R})
is
\omega
. We have a short exact sequence

(10.12)

1\to{\mathbf{T}}(\mathbb{R})\to{\mathbf{T}}(\mathbb{R})^{\prime}\to{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}\to 1,

and
ZG\cap{\mathbf{T}}(\mathbb{R})=ZG(\mathbb{R})
. Therefore we have a restriction map

(10.13)

\rho_{\lambda_{0}}:\textup{LS}^{ad}_{\lambda_{0},\mathcal{L}}\to\textup{LS}^{\omega}_{\lambda_{0}}

that is surjective. When
\textup{LS}^{\omega}_{\lambda_{0}}\neq\varnothing
,
\textup{LS}^{ad}_{\lambda_{0},\mathcal{L}}
is a torsor under
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{ad}(\mathbb{R}))^{*}
while
\textup{LS}^{\omega}_{\lambda_{0}}
is a torsor under
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}(\mathbb{R})/ZG(\mathbb{R}))^{*}
, and the map
\rho_{\lambda_{0}}
is compatible with their torsor structures via the surjection
\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}^{ad}(\mathbb{R}))^{*}\to\pi_{0}({\mathbf{T}}(\mathbb{R})/ZG(\mathbb{R}))^{*}
whose kernel is
{\mathfrak{S}}
by (
10.12
). This means the map
\rho_{\lambda_{0}}
is a
{\mathfrak{S}}
-torsor. Comparing the definitions of
\mathcal{B}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\omega}
, we conclude that

({\mathcal{B}}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}})^{\mathfrak{S}}\cong{\mathcal{B}}^{\omega}.

Using this isomorphism we get a functor

(10.14)

(\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}})^{{\mathfrak{S}}}\to\bigoplus_{\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}}\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}^{\omega}

sending a
{\mathfrak{S}}
-equivariant
{\mathcal{B}}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}}
-module
M
to
\oplus_{\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}}M(\sigma)
, where
M(\sigma)
is the
\sigma
-isotypical summand of
M
which is a module over
({\mathcal{B}}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}})^{{\mathfrak{S}}}\cong{\mathcal{B}}^{\omega}
.

Proposition 10.10
.

The following diagram is commutative

\textstyle{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{\omega}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bigoplus_{\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}}\mathbb{V}^{\sharp,\omega}_{{\mathbb{R}},{\sigma(\widetilde{\xi})}}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}}\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}^{\omega}}
\textstyle{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{L}}})^{\mathfrak{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{V}^{ad,\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
(
10.9
)
\textstyle{(\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}})^{{\mathfrak{S}}},\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
10.14
)

Proof.

The diagram is compatible with the
{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}
-actions, therefore it is sufficient to verify the commutativity after passing to the
{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}
-equivariant objects. It then becomes the diagram

\textstyle{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{\omega}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})^{{\mathfrak{S}}^{*}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{V}^{\sharp,\omega}_{{\mathbb{R}}}}
\textstyle{\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}^{\omega}}
