\textstyle{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G^{ad}_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{L}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{V}^{ad,\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}},\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

which is commutative tautologically, as these are the same vanishing cycles functors.

∎

11.
Koszul duality for quasi-split groups

In this section we reprove the main result of
[
9
]
, Koszul duality for quasi-split real groups, using a more geometric argument based on our study of tilting sheaves on
G_{\mathbb{R}}
-orbits.

11.1.
Vogan duality

Recall from
[
27
, Theorem 8.5]
that the blocks of
G({\mathbb{R}})
-representations are in bijection with
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
-orbits on
\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
under the cross action. Let
a\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}/{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
. Then the corresponding block (direct summand)
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{a}\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
is generated (as a triangulated category) by summands of
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}\star\mathcal{K}
for
\chi\in a
and
\mathcal{K}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
. Note that all objects in
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{a}
have the same central character, say
\omega:ZG(\mathbb{R})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
, therefore
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{a}
is a direct summand of
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{\omega}
.

Now we pass to the Langlands dual group
\check{G}
. Denote its flag variety by
\check{X}
. For a symmetric subgroup
\check{K}\subset\check{G}
, consider the equivariant derived category
\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\check{K}}:=D^{b}_{\check{K}}(\check{X})
. We have the equivariant Hecke category

\check{\mathcal{H}}_{\check{G}}:=D^{b}_{\check{G}}(\check{X}\times\check{X})

that acts on
\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\check{K}}
by convolution on the right.

Let
O_{\check{\lambda}_{0}}^{\check{K}}\subset\check{X}
be the open
\check{K}
-orbit. There is also the notion of blocks for
\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\check{K}}
, and the blocks are parametrized by
{\mathbf{W}}_{\check{\lambda}_{0}}
-orbits on the set
\textup{LS}_{\check{\lambda}_{0}}
of isomorphism classes of
\check{K}
-equivariant irreducible local systems on
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}_{0}}
under the cross action. For
\check{a}\in\textup{LS}_{\check{\lambda}_{0}}/{\mathbf{W}}_{\check{\lambda}_{0}}
, denote the corresponding block (direct summand) by
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}\subset\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\check{K}}
; it is generated by direct summands of
{\operatorname{IC}}(\mathcal{L}_{\check{\lambda}_{0},\chi})\star\mathcal{K}
for
\chi\in\check{a}
and
\mathcal{K}\in\check{\mathcal{H}}_{\check{G}}
.

To the pair
(G_{\mathbb{R}},a)
, where
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is a real form of
G
and
a\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}/{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
, Vogan’s duality of
[
27
]
associates a pair
(\check{K},\check{a})
consisting of a symmetric subgroup
\check{K}\subset\check{G}
and
\check{a}\in\textup{LS}_{\check{\lambda}_{0}}/{\mathbf{W}}_{\check{\lambda}_{0}}
. We say the corresponding blocks
{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
and
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
are in
Vogan duality
.

When
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split, the block
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
is what is called
principal
, i.e.,
\check{a}
contains the trivial local system, or equivalently,
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
contains the constant sheaf on
\check{X}
. In this case, it is proved in
[
27
]
and
[
9
, Proposition 3.6]
that
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
is generated as a triangulated category by
{\operatorname{IC}}_{\check{\lambda}}
for
closed
orbits
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}\subset\check{X}
under the
\check{\mathcal{H}}_{\check{G}}
-action and taking direct summands.

11.2.
Koszul duality–additive category version

We continue with the notations above. Fix a block
{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
for
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
and its Vogan dual (principal) block
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
for
\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\check{K}}
.

Let
\check{T}
be the abstract Cartan for
\check{G}
. Let

\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}=H^{*}_{\check{{\mathbf{T}}}}(\textup{pt},{\mathbf{k}})\cong\mathop{\textrm{Sym}}\nolimits(\mathbb{X}_{*}(\check{T})\otimes{\mathbf{k}}[-2]),

viewed as a formal dg algebra in even non-negative degrees.
We have the ring homomorphism

\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}=\mathop{\textrm{Sym}}\nolimits(\mathbb{X}_{*}({\mathbf{T}})\otimes{\mathbf{k}})\to\mathcal{R}=\widehat{{\mathbf{k}}[\mathbb{X}_{*}({\mathbf{T}})]}

sending
x\in\mathbb{X}_{*}({\mathbf{T}})
to
\log(x)=\sum_{n\geq 1}(-1)^{n-1}\frac{(x-1)^{n}}{n}\in\mathcal{R}
. This identifies
\mathcal{R}
with the completion of
\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}
along the ideal
\mathcal{R}^{>0}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{R}\cong\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{R}^{n}
.

The category
\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\check{K}}
is enriched over (right) graded
\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}
-modules in the following way: for
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\check{K}}
, the Ext group

\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}):=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\check{K}\backslash\check{X}}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2})

is equipped with a graded action of
H^{*}_{\check{K}}(\check{X},{\mathbf{k}})
, hence a graded module over
\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}
via the map
\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}\to H^{*}_{\check{K}}(\check{X},{\mathbf{k}})
obtained as the pullback along
\check{K}\backslash\check{X}\to\textup{pt}/\check{B}\to\textup{pt}/\check{{\mathbf{T}}}
.

Let
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
be the full subcategory of
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
consisting of semisimple complexes, i.e., direct sums of shifts of simple perverse sheaves. This is an additive category equipped with the cohomological shift endo-functor
[1]
. We denote this shift functor by
\{1\}
, and call it the
internal
degree shift functor on
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
. For
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\in\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
, we use the notation

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}):=\oplus_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\{m\})

instead of
\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2})
; it is a graded
\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}
-module.

The following can be deduced from the equivariant-monodromic equivalence
[
10
, Theorem 5.2.1]
. There is a canonical monoidal functor

(11.1)

\Phi_{G}:\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{H}}_{\check{G}})\to\textup{Tilt}(\mathcal{H}_{G})

that sends the simple perverse sheaf
{\operatorname{IC}}_{w}
to the free-monodromic tilting sheaf
\mathcal{T}_{w}
for any
w\in W
. Indeed, let
\textup{SBim}^{gr}
be the category of graded Soergel
\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}
-bimodules. Then Soergel’s classical result gives a monoidal equivalence
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{H}}_{\check{G}})\cong\textup{SBim}^{gr}
(as categories enriched in
\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}\textup{-mod-}
). Now
\textup{Tilt}(\mathcal{H}_{G})\cong\textup{SBim}
, then functor
\Phi_{G}
corresponds to the monoidal functor

(-)\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}\cong\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}(-):\textup{SBim}^{gr}\to\textup{SBim}.

The following is a reformulation of the main result of
[
9
]
.
It essentially states that
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
can be viewed as a graded version of
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
. We will give a geometric proof using our results on
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
and the results on
\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\check{K}}
proved in
[
9
]
.

Theorem 11.3
.

(cf.
[
9
]
)
Fix a regular covector
\xi\in i\mathcal{N}^{*}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{O}_{{\text{\rm reg}}}
and its lift
\widetilde{\xi}
as in Section
8.3
, and fix an extension
\widetilde{\omega}
of
\omega
(the central character of the block
a
) to
ZG
. With these choices, there is a canonical functor

\kappa:\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})\to\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})

satisfying the following properties:

(1)

\kappa
is invariant under the internal shift
\{1\}
on
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
.

(2)

For
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\in\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
, the natural maps
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\{n\})\to\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\kappa(\mathcal{F}_{1}),\kappa(\mathcal{F}_{2}))
induce an isomorphism of
\mathcal{R}
-modules

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\kappa(\mathcal{F}_{1}),\kappa(\mathcal{F}_{2})).

(3)

\kappa
intertwines the actions of
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{H}}_{\check{G}})
and
\textup{Tilt}(\mathcal{H}_{G})
on both sides under the equivariant-monodromic functor (
11.1
).

(4)

\kappa
is essentially surjective, and induces a bijection

\displaystyle|\kappa|

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\{\mbox{simple perverse sheaves in $\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}$}\}/

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\{\mbox{indecomp. free mono. tilting sheaves in ${\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}$}\}/\cong.

Proof.

Let
\omega:ZG(\mathbb{R})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
be the restriction of any
\chi\in a
to the real center
ZG(\mathbb{R})
. Let
\widetilde{\omega}:ZG(\mathbb{C})\to{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
be an extension of
\omega
. Recall the
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
-equivariant map
\rho_{\lambda_{0}}:\textup{LS}^{ad}_{\lambda_{0},\mathcal{L}}\to\textup{LS}^{\omega}_{\lambda_{0}}
that is a
{\mathfrak{S}}
-torsor when both are non-empty. Let
\widetilde{a}=\rho^{-1}_{\lambda_{0}}(a)
, with the action of
{\mathfrak{S}}
.

Let
\mathcal{B}^{ad,a}_{0,\mathcal{L}}=(\prod_{\chi\in\widetilde{a}}{\mathcal{S}})^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}
and
\mathcal{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}}=\mathcal{B}^{ad,a}_{0,\mathcal{L}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}
. This is a direct factor of
\mathcal{B}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}}
that is stable under the action of
{\mathfrak{S}}
. The
SBim
action on
\textup{mod-}\mathcal{B}^{ad}_{\mathcal{L}}
restricts to an action on
\textup{mod-}\mathcal{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}}
commuting with the
{\mathfrak{S}}
-action. The fully faithful functor (
10.11
) restricts to full embedding

(11.2)

\mathbb{V}^{\sharp,a}_{\mathbb{R}}\colon\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})\to(\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}})^{{\mathfrak{S}}}.

For
\chi\in a
, denote by
{\mathcal{S}}_{\chi}={\mathcal{S}}
the
{\mathcal{B}}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}}
-module on which
{\mathcal{B}}^{ad,a}_{0,\mathcal{L}}
acts via the
\chi
-factor
{\mathcal{S}}
and
{\mathcal{R}}
acts via the projection to
{\mathcal{S}}
. Denote by
\mathbb{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}}
be the full subcategory of
\textup{mod-}{\mathcal{B}}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}}
generated by
\{{\mathcal{S}}_{\chi}\}_{\chi\in\widetilde{a}}
under the action of Soergel bimodules
SBim
and taking direct summands. Then
{\mathfrak{S}}
acts on
\mathbb{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}}
.
The full embedding (
11.2
) induces an equivalence of
\mathcal{R}
-linear additive categories

(11.3)

\mathbb{V}^{a}_{\mathcal{L}}:\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})\simeq(\mathbb{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}})^{{\mathfrak{S}}}.

As indicated in the notation, the functor
\mathbb{V}^{a}_{\mathcal{L}}
depends on the choice of
\widetilde{\omega}
(same datum as
\mathcal{L}
) extending
\omega
.

Now consider the dual side. Our category
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
is what’s denoted by
{\mathcal{S}}^{gr}
in
[
9
, 3.1]
. In
[
9
]
, the authors introduced the counterpart
(\check{\mathcal{B}}^{sc,\check{a},\bullet},\check{\mathfrak{S}})
of
(\mathcal{B}^{ad,a},{\mathfrak{S}})
on the equivariant side. Here
\check{\mathcal{B}}^{sc,\check{a},\bullet}=H^{*}_{\check{K}^{\circ}}(\check{X},{\mathbf{k}})
(where
\check{K}^{\circ}
is the neutral component of
\check{K}
) as graded
\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}
-algebras, and
\check{\mathfrak{S}}=\pi_{0}(\check{K})
. The pair
(\check{\mathcal{B}}^{sc,\check{a},\bullet},\check{\mathfrak{S}})
is denoted by
({\mathfrak{B}},{\mathfrak{S}})
in
[
9
, 3.9]
. Note that

\check{\mathcal{B}}^{sc,\check{a},\bullet}\cong\check{\mathcal{B}}^{sc,\check{a},\bullet}_{0}\otimes_{(\mathcal{R}^{\bullet})^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}^{\bullet},\quad\mbox{ where }\check{\mathcal{B}}^{sc,\check{a},\bullet}_{0}=H^{*}_{\check{K}^{\circ}}(\textup{pt},{\mathbf{k}}).

Let
(\check{K}^{\circ}\backslash\check{X})_{cl}
be the set of closed
\check{K}^{\circ}
-orbits on
\check{X}
. For
\check{\lambda}\in(\check{K}^{\circ}\backslash\check{X})_{cl}
, denote the corresponding
\check{K}^{\circ}
-orbit by
O^{\check{K}^{\circ}}_{\check{\lambda}}
, then we have

\mathcal{S}^{\bullet}_{\check{\lambda}}:=H^{*}_{\check{K}^{\circ}}(O^{\check{K}^{\circ}}_{\check{\lambda}},{\mathbf{k}})\in\textup{grmod-}\check{\mathcal{B}}^{sc,\check{a},\bullet}.

Let
\check{\mathbb{B}}^{sc,\check{a},gr}
be the full subcategory of graded right
\check{\mathcal{B}}^{sc,\check{a},\bullet}
-modules generated by
\{{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}_{\check{\lambda}}\}
(
\check{\lambda}\in(\check{K}^{\circ}\backslash\check{X})_{cl}
) under the action of
\textup{SBim}^{gr}
and taking direct summands. Note that
\check{\mathfrak{S}}
acts on
\check{\mathbb{B}}^{sc,\check{a},gr}
. Then
[
9
, Theorem 3.12]
gives an equivalence of
\textup{grmod-}\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}
-linear categories

\mathbb{H}^{\check{a}}:\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})\simeq(\check{\mathbb{B}}^{sc,\check{a},gr})^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}.

By
[
9
, Theorem 2.6]
, there is a canonical isomorphism
\check{\mathfrak{S}}\cong{\mathfrak{S}}
and an
\mathcal{R}
-algebra isomorphism

(11.4)

\check{\mathcal{B}}^{sc,\check{a}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}}

equivariant under the respective actions of
\check{\mathfrak{S}}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}
. It induces a functor

c^{\prime}:\textup{grmod-}\check{\mathcal{B}}^{sc,\check{a}}\to\textup{mod-}\mathcal{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}}

sending a graded
\check{\mathcal{B}}^{sc,\check{a}}
-module
M=\oplus_{n}M^{n}
(where
M^{n}=0
for
n\ll 0
) to its completion
\widehat{M}=\prod_{n}M^{n}
, which is a
\mathcal{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}}
-module via (
11.4
).

We claim that
c^{\prime}
sends
\check{\mathbb{B}}^{sc,\check{a},gr}
to
\mathbb{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}}
. To check this we may assume
G
is adjoint. Then as mentioned in the proof of
[
9
, Theorem 2.4]
, there is a canonical bijection

(11.5)

(\check{K}\backslash\check{X})_{cl}\leftrightarrow a.

Moreover, if
\check{\lambda}\in(\check{K}\backslash\check{X})_{cl}
corresponds to
\chi\in a
, then
{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}_{\check{\lambda}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}\cong{\mathcal{S}}_{\chi}
as
\mathcal{B}^{a}
-modules. Since
\check{\mathbb{B}}^{sc,\check{a},gr}
(resp.
\mathbb{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}}
) is generated by
{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}_{\check{\lambda}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{S}}_{\chi}
) under the
\textup{SBim}^{gr}
(resp.
SBim
) action and taking direct summands, we conclude that
c^{\prime}
sends
\check{\mathbb{B}}^{sc,\check{a},gr}
to
\mathbb{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}}
.

It is easy to see that
c^{\prime}
is equivariant under
\check{\mathfrak{S}}\cong{\mathfrak{S}}
.
Therefore,
c^{\prime}
induces a functor

c:(\check{\mathbb{B}}^{sc,\check{a},gr})^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\to(\mathbb{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}})^{{\mathfrak{S}}}.

We then define
\kappa
to be the composition

\textstyle{\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{H}^{\check{a}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{(\check{\mathbb{B}}^{sc,\check{a},gr})^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{(\mathbb{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}})^{{\mathfrak{S}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathbb{V}^{a}_{\mathcal{L}})^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}).}

Now we check the required properties of
\kappa
. Properties (
1
) and (
2
) follows easily from the similar properties of the completion functor
c
. Property (
3
) follows from the fact that
c
intertwines the
\textup{SBim}^{gr}
-action on
(\check{\mathbb{B}}^{sc,\check{a},gr})^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
with the
SBim
-action on
(\mathbb{B}^{ad,a}_{\mathcal{L}})^{{\mathfrak{S}}}
via the completion functor
\textup{SBim}^{gr}\to\textup{SBim}
.

For (
4
), we first show that if
\mathcal{F}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
is a simple perverse sheaf, then
\kappa(\mathcal{F})
is indecomposable. Indeed,
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F})
is concentrated in non-negative degrees with degree zero part being
{\mathbf{k}}
. Therefore, by (
2
),
\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits(\kappa(\mathcal{F}))\cong\prod_{n\geq 0}\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F})
, hence is a local ring with residue field
{\mathbf{k}}
. This implies
\kappa(\mathcal{F})
is indecomposable. Therefore the map
|\kappa|
in property (
4
) is defined.

We show
|\kappa|
is injective. If
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
are simple perverse sheaves and
\alpha:\kappa(\mathcal{F}_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\kappa(\mathcal{F}_{2})
is an isomorphism with inverse
\beta
. Using that
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\kappa(\mathcal{F}_{1}),\kappa(\mathcal{F}_{2}))\cong\prod_{n\geq 0}\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2})
, let
\alpha_{0}
and
\beta_{0}
be the degree zero projections of
\alpha
and
\beta
. Then
\alpha_{0}
and
\beta_{0}
define inverse isomorphisms between
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
. This proves that
|\kappa|
is injective.

To show
|\kappa|
is surjective, it suffices to consider the case
G
is adjoint hence
\check{G}
is simply-connected; the case of general semisimple
G
follows by quotienting by
\check{\mathfrak{S}}\cong{\mathfrak{S}}
on both sides of
|\kappa|
. Now assume
G
is adjoint. Let
\mathcal{T}
be an indecomposable free-monodromic tilting sheaf in
{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
. By Proposition
6.8
,
\mathcal{T}
is a direct summand of
{\mathcal{S}}_{\chi}\star\mathcal{K}
for some
\chi\in a
and
\mathcal{K}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})
. Let
\check{\lambda}\in(\check{K}\backslash\check{X})_{cl}
correspond to
\chi\in a
under (
11.5
). Let
\check{\mathcal{K}}\in\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{H}}_{\check{G}})\cong\textup{SBim}^{gr}
be an object that maps to
\mathcal{K}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{H}}_{G})\cong\textup{SBim}
under
\Phi_{G}
. Let
{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}_{\check{\lambda}}\star\check{\mathcal{K}}=\oplus_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{F}_{i}
be a decomposition into shifted simple perverse sheaves. We have proved that each
\kappa(\mathcal{F}_{i})
is an indecomposable free-monodromic tilting sheaf. Since
\oplus_{i=1}^{N}\kappa(\mathcal{F}_{i})\cong\kappa({\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}_{\check{\lambda}}\star\check{\mathcal{K}})\cong{\mathcal{S}}_{\chi}\star\mathcal{K}
, and the latter contains
\mathcal{T}
as an indecomposable summand, hence by Corollary
5.6
(3),
\mathcal{T}\cong\kappa(\mathcal{F}_{i})
for some
i
. This proves that
|\kappa|
is surjective. We have finished checking property (
4
).

∎

Recall the function
\ell:I\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
from Definition
6.10
. For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, let
X_{\leq n}
be the union of
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}
such that
\ell(\lambda)\leq n
. Since the similar union
\cup_{\ell(\lambda)\leq n}O^{K}_{\lambda}\subset X
is open by Lemma
6.11
,
X_{\leq n}
is closed in
X
. Let
{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},\leq n}\subset{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
be the full subcategory of complexes supported on
X_{\leq n}
.

On the dual side, for the principal block
\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\check{K}}^{\check{a}}
, let
\widetilde{\check{I}^{\check{a}}}
be the set of isomorphisms classes of simple perverse sheaves in
\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\check{K}}^{\check{a}}
. Let
\check{I}
be the set of
\check{K}
-orbits on
\check{X}
. Then we have the map
\widetilde{\check{I}^{\check{a}}}\to\check{I}
sending
\mathcal{F}
to the unique dense orbit in
\operatorname{Supp}(\mathcal{F})
. We define a function
\check{\ell}:\widetilde{\check{I}^{\check{a}}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
in a similar way as
\ell
: for
\mathcal{F}\in\widetilde{\check{I}^{\check{a}}}
, let
\check{\ell}(\mathcal{F})
be the minimal non-negative integer
n
such that a shift of
\mathcal{F}
appears as a direct summand of
{\operatorname{IC}}_{\check{\lambda}}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{w}
for some
\check{\lambda}\in(\check{K}\backslash\check{X})_{cl}
and
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
with
\ell(w)=n
. It is shown in the argument of
[
9
, Proposition 3.6]
that

(11.6)

\check{\ell}(\mathcal{F})=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\operatorname{Supp}(\mathcal{F})-\check{d}_{\min}

where
\check{d}_{\min}
is the complex dimension of any closed
\check{K}
-orbit on
\check{X}
. In particular,
\check{\ell}
factors through
\check{I}
, which we still denote by
\check{\ell}:\check{T}\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
. For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, let
\check{X}_{\leq n}
be the union of
\check{K}
-orbits
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}
such that
\check{\ell}(\check{\lambda})\leq n
. Then
\check{X}_{\leq n}
is closed in
\check{X}
. Let
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K},\leq n}\subset\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
be the full subcategory of complexes supported on
\check{X}_{\leq n}
.

Corollary 11.4
.

(1)

The bijection
|\kappa|
preserves the length functions on both sides: if
\mathcal{F}
has support
\overline{O}^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}
for some
\check{\lambda}\in\check{I}
, and it corresponds to
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
under
|\kappa|
, then
\check{\ell}(\check{\lambda})=\ell(\lambda)
.

(2)

The functor
\kappa
in Theorem
11.3
sends
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K},\leq n})
surjectively to
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},\leq n})
for any
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.

Proof.

(1) Comparing the definitions of
\check{\ell}
and
\ell
, we see
\check{\ell}(\mathcal{F})=\ell(\lambda)
because for a closed
\check{K}
-orbit
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\mu}}
,
\kappa({\operatorname{IC}}_{\check{\mu}})\cong\mathcal{T}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}
for some
\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
, and
\kappa
is equivariant under Hecke actions by Theorem
11.3
(
3
).

(2) By Lemma
6.11
, objects in
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},\leq n})
are direct sums of
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
with
\ell(\lambda)\leq n
. By (
11.6
), objects in
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K},\leq n})
are direct sums of shifts of simple perverse sheaves supported on
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}
with
\check{\ell}(\check{\lambda})\leq n
. Therefore (2) follows from (1).
∎

11.5.
Koszul duality–triangulated category version

To extend the Koszul duality functor in Theorem
11.3
to triangulated categories, we recall how
{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
and
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
are recovered from the additive subcategories
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
and
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
respectively.

First consider the monodromic side. The following constructions and results make sense in the general setting of Section
3.1
, i.e. in the case of a Lie group
H
acting on a real analytic space
X
satisfying the assumptions in Section
3.2
.

Recall from Lemma
3.13
(1) that
\operatorname{Ext}^{>0}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}}({\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2})=0
. Denote

{\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus}:=\bigoplus_{(\lambda,\chi)\in\widetilde{I}}{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\chi}

for the sum of all indecomposable free-monodromic tilting sheaves in
{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}
and let

E=\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus})^{opp}.

Also, put
K^{b}(\mathrm{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}))
for the homotopy category of bounded complexes in
\mathrm{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X})
.

Proposition 11.6
.

(1)

Let
\operatorname{Proj}(E\textup{-mod})
be the category of finitely generated projective
E
-modules. Then the functor

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus},-):\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X})\to\operatorname{Proj}(E\textup{-mod})

is an equivalence of categories.

(2)

The natural functor
K^{b}(\mathrm{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}))\to{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

(3)

Combining (1) and (2), there is a canonical equivalence of triangulated categories

{\mathcal{M}}_{H,X}\cong\textup{Perf}(E\textup{-mod}):=K^{b}(\operatorname{Proj}(E\textup{-mod}))

under which indecomposable free-monodromic tilting sheaves correspond to indecomposable projective
E
-modules.

Proof.

(1) The functor lands in projective
E
-modules because all objects in
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{H,X})
are direct summands of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus}^{n}
for some
n
by Proposition
3.14
. The left adjoint of the functor is given by
M\mapsto{\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus}\otimes_{E}M
. One checks that these functors are inverse to each other.

(2) Follows from Proposition
3.14
and Lemma
3.13
(1) as in
[
3
, 1.5]
(see also
[
10
, Proposition B.1.7]
).
∎

On the dual side, we introduce the notation

\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}:=K^{b}(\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})).

This can be viewed as a mixed (in the sense of mixed sheaves or mixed Hodge modules) version of
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
. Similarly we define the graded version of the Hecke category

\check{\mathcal{H}}^{gr}_{\check{G}}:=K^{b}(\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{H}}_{\check{G}}))\cong K^{b}(\textup{SBim}^{gr}).

that acts on
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}
.

The internal degree shift
\{1\}
on
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
induces an endo-functor on
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}
which we still denote by
\{1\}
. We denote the cohomological shift in
K^{b}(\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}))
by
[1]
. For
\mathcal{F}\in\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
viewed as an object in
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}
, we write it as
\mathcal{F}\{0\}
.

For
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}
, define a bigraded
\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}
-module

\textup{HOM}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}):=\oplus_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\{m\}[n]).

For simple perverse sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
with support
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}
, let
\Delta_{\mathcal{F}}=i_{\check{\lambda},!}i_{\check{\lambda}}^{*}\mathcal{F}
and
\nabla_{\mathcal{F}}=i_{\check{\lambda},*}i_{\check{\lambda}}^{*}\mathcal{F}
.

The following proposition makes precise the meaning that
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}
is a graded version of
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
.

Proposition 11.7
.

There is a canonical triangulated functor

\varphi:\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}=K^{b}(\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}))\to\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}

extending the natural embedding
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})\hookrightarrow\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
and satisfying the following properties:

(1)

\varphi\circ\{1\}\cong[1]\circ\varphi
.

(2)

For
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}
, natural maps induce a graded
\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}
-linear isomorphism

\displaystyle\textup{HOM}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}(\varphi(\mathcal{F}_{1}),\varphi(\mathcal{F}_{2}))

that maps
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\{m\}[n])
to
\operatorname{Ext}^{m+n}(\varphi(\mathcal{F}_{1}),\varphi(\mathcal{F}_{2}))
, for
m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

(3)

The functor
\varphi
intertwines the actions of
\check{\mathcal{H}}^{gr}_{\check{G}}
and
\check{\mathcal{H}}_{\check{G}}
via the similarly defined monoidal functor
\varphi_{\check{G}}:\check{\mathcal{H}}^{gr}_{\check{G}}\to\check{\mathcal{H}}_{\check{G}}
in the case of complex groups.

(4)

For a simple perverse sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
, there exists
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}
and a map
\imath^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}:\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}\to\mathcal{F}\{0\}
whose image under
\varphi
is the canonical map
\imath_{\mathcal{F}}:\Delta_{\mathcal{F}}\to\mathcal{F}
; such a pair
(\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}},\imath_{\mathcal{F}}^{gr})
is unique up to a unique isomorphism. Similarly, there exists
\nabla^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}
and a map
\jmath^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{F}\to\nabla^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}
whose image under
\varphi
is the canonical map
\jmath_{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{F}\to\nabla_{\mathcal{F}}
; such a pair is unique up to a unique isomorphism.

Proof.

The functor
\varphi
is constructed in
[
9
, 5.1]
; the essential point being that for
\check{L}=\oplus_{\widetilde{\check{I}^{\check{a}}}}\mathcal{F}
, the dg algebra
\operatorname{RHom}(\check{L},\check{L})
is formal, which is proved by a standard purity argument. Properties (1)-(3) for
\varphi
are easy to see.

Now we prove (4). We only give the argument for
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}
, and the case of
\nabla^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}
can be proved similarly by replacing all
\Delta
’s by
\nabla
’s.

We first construct
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}
. Note that graded liftings
\Delta^{gr}_{w}\in\check{\mathcal{H}}_{\check{G}}^{gr}
of
\Delta_{w}\in\check{\mathcal{H}}_{\check{G}}
exist for any
w\in{\mathbf{W}}
(in the Soergel bimodule setup, they are the Rouquier complexes). We construct
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}
by induction on
\check{\ell}(\mathcal{F})
. For
\check{\ell}(\mathcal{F})=0
,
\Delta_{\mathcal{F}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{F}
, hence we can take
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}
to be
\mathcal{F}\{0\}
. Now suppose
\mathcal{F}
is not supported on a closed orbit, and suppose
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}
together with the map
\imath_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}:\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
has been constructed for all simple perverse sheaves
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
with
\check{\ell}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})<\check{\ell}(\mathcal{F})
(i.e.,
\dim\operatorname{Supp}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})<\dim\operatorname{Supp}(\mathcal{F})
). Let
\mathcal{F}={\operatorname{IC}}(O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}},{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\psi})
for some
\check{K}
-equivariant local system
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\psi}
on
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}
. Since
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}
is not a closed
\check{K}
-orbit, there exists a simple root
\check{\alpha}_{s}
in the root system
\Phi_{\check{\lambda}}
such that
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}
is open in (but not equal to)
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}})
. We have the following cases:
8
8

8

The calculations of convolutions
\star\Delta_{s}
that appear in these cases are analogs of Lemma
6.3
, which can be found in
[
18
, Lemma 3.5]
, or can be deduced from Lemma
6.3
by applying the Matsuki equivalence.

•

If
\check{\alpha}_{s}
is a complex root, then
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}})-O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}
is a unique orbit
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\mu}}
, and the local system
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\check{\lambda},\psi}
extends to
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}})
, whose restriction to
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\mu}}
we still denote by
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\check{\mu},\psi}
. We have
\Delta_{\mathcal{F}}=\Delta_{\check{\lambda},\psi}\cong\Delta_{\check{\mu},\psi}\star\Delta_{s}
. By inductive hypothesis,
\Delta^{gr}_{\check{\mu},\psi}
has already been constructed. We define
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}:=\Delta^{gr}_{\check{\mu},\psi}\star\Delta^{gr}_{s}
.

•

If
\check{\alpha}_{s}
is a real root,
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\check{\lambda},\psi}
extends to a local system
\widetilde{{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\check{\lambda},\psi}}
on
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}})
, and
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}})-O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}
is a single orbit
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\mu}}
. Denote the restriction of
\widetilde{{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\check{\lambda},\psi}}
to
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\mu}}
again by
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\check{\mu},\psi}
. Then there is a distinguished triangle
\Delta_{\check{\lambda},\psi}\oplus\Delta_{\check{\lambda},\psi^{\prime}}\to\Delta_{\check{\mu},\psi}\star\Delta_{s}\to\Delta_{\check{\mu},\psi}[1]
(where
\psi^{\prime}\neq\psi
is another local system on
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}
). Note that
\Delta^{gr}_{\check{\mu},\psi}
has already been constructed by inductive hypothesis. Since
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\Delta_{\check{\mu},\psi}\star\Delta_{s},\Delta_{\check{\mu},\psi}[1])
is one-dimensional over
{\mathbf{k}}
, by Proposition
11.7
(2), there is a unique
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and a nonzero map
f:\Delta^{gr}_{\check{\mu},\psi}\star\Delta^{gr}_{s}\to\Delta^{gr}_{\check{\mu},\psi}\{m\}[1-m]
(unique up to scalar). Let
C=Cone(f)[-1]\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}
, then
\varphi(C)\cong\Delta_{\check{\lambda},\psi}\oplus\Delta_{\check{\lambda},\psi^{\prime}}
. By Proposition
11.7
(2), idempotents of
\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits(\varphi(C))
lift to idempotents of
\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits(C)
, therefore
C
decomposes into two summands
C_{1}\oplus C_{2}
whose images under
\varphi
are
\Delta_{\check{\lambda},\psi}
and
\Delta_{\check{\lambda},\psi^{\prime}}
respectively. We define
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}:=C_{1}
.

•

If
\check{\alpha}_{s}
is a real root,
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\check{\lambda},\psi}
extends to a local system
\widetilde{{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\check{\lambda},\psi}}
on
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}})
, and
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}})-O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}=O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\mu}^{+}}\sqcup O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\mu}^{-}}
. Let
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\check{\mu}^{+},\psi^{+}}
and
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\check{\mu}^{-},\psi^{-}}
be the restrictions of
\widetilde{{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}_{\check{\lambda},\psi}}
to
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\mu}^{+}}
and
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\mu}^{-}}
respectively. Then we have a distinguished triangle
\Delta_{\check{\lambda},\psi}\to\Delta_{\check{\mu}^{+},\psi^{+}}\star\Delta_{s}\to\Delta_{\check{\mu}^{-},\psi^{-}}[1]
. As in the previous case, there is a unique
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}
such that there is a nonzero map
f:\Delta^{gr}_{\check{\mu}^{+},\psi^{+}}\star\Delta^{gr}_{s}\to\Delta^{gr}_{\check{\mu}^{-},\psi^{-}}\{m\}[1-m]
. We define
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}:=Cone(f)[-1]
.

•

If
\check{\alpha}_{s}
is a real root and
\psi
does not extend to
\pi_{s}^{-1}\pi_{s}(O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}})
. If this happens, then there must exist another simple root
\check{\alpha}_{t}
that falls into the previous cases. This is proved in the argument of
[
9
, Proposition 3.6]
. Therefore there is no need to consider this case.

In each of the cases,
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}
is defined to be equipped with a map to
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}\star\Delta_{s}
for some simple perverse sheaf
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
with
\check{\ell}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})=n-1
and simple reflection
s\in{\mathbf{W}}
. We then define
\imath_{\mathcal{F}}
to be the composition

\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}\to\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}\star\Delta_{s}\xrightarrow{\imath_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}\star\imath_{{\operatorname{IC}}_{s}}}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\{0\}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{s}\{0\}\to\mathcal{F}\{0\}

where the last map is the projection of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{s}
to the unique summand isomorphic to
\mathcal{F}
. It is easy to see that
\imath_{\mathcal{F}}
is sent to the canonical map
\Delta_{\mathcal{F}}\to\mathcal{F}
under
\varphi
.

Finally we prove the uniqueness of the pair
(\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}},\imath_{\mathcal{F}})
. Let
(\mathcal{E},i:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{F}\{0\})
and
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},i^{\prime}:\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}\{0\})
be two such pairs. Let
\epsilon:\varphi(\mathcal{E})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\varphi(\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be the unique isomorphism compatible with
\varphi(i)
and
\varphi(i^{\prime})
. By Proposition
11.7
(2), for a unique
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\epsilon
lifts to a map
\epsilon^{gr}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\{m\}[-m]
. Now both
i:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{F}
and
i^{\prime}\circ\epsilon^{gr}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\{m\}[-m]\to\mathcal{F}\{m\}[-m]
map to the canonical map
\Delta_{\mathcal{F}}\to\mathcal{F}
under
\varphi
. By Proposition
11.7
(2) again, we conclude that
m=0
and
i=i^{\prime}\circ\epsilon^{gr}
. This proves the uniqueness of the pair up to unique isomorphism.
∎

Theorem 11.8
.

Under the same notations and choices of
\xi,\widetilde{\xi}
and
\widetilde{\omega}
as in Theorem
11.3
, there is a canonical triangulated functor

\widetilde{\kappa}:\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}\to{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}

extending the functor
\kappa
and satisfying the following properties:

(1)

\widetilde{\kappa}\circ\{1\}\cong\widetilde{\kappa}
.

(2)

For
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}
, natural maps induce a graded
\mathcal{R}
-linear isomorphism

\displaystyle\textup{HOM}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}(\widetilde{\kappa}(\mathcal{F}_{1}),\widetilde{\kappa}(\mathcal{F}_{2}))

that sends
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\{m\}[n])
to
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(\widetilde{\kappa}(\mathcal{F}_{1}),\widetilde{\kappa}(\mathcal{F}_{2}))
, for
m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

(3)

The functor
\widetilde{\kappa}
intertwines the actions of
{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
and
\check{\mathcal{H}}^{gr}_{\check{G}}
via the monoidal functor
\check{\mathcal{H}}^{gr}_{\check{G}}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{\check{G}}}\check{\mathcal{H}}_{\check{G}}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\Phi}}\mathcal{H}_{G}
. Here
\widetilde{\Phi}
is defined similarly as
\widetilde{\kappa}
in the case of complex groups.

(4)

Suppose
|\kappa|
sends a simple perverse sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
to
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
as in Theorem
11.3
(
4
). Then
\widetilde{\kappa}
sends the graded lift of the standard object
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}
(resp. the costandard object
\nabla^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}
, see Proposition
11.7
(
4
)) to the free-monodromic standard object
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
(resp. free-monodromic costandard object
\widetilde{\nabla}_{\lambda,\chi}
).

Proof.

By Proposition
11.6
(2),
{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}\cong K^{b}(\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}))
. We define the functor
\widetilde{\kappa}
to be
K^{b}(\kappa)
. Properties (1)-(3) follow from the similar properties for
\kappa
proved in Theorem
11.3
.

It remains to check (
4
); we give the argument for
\widetilde{\kappa}(\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}})\cong\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
, and the other isomorphism
\widetilde{\nabla}(\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}})\cong\widetilde{\nabla}_{\lambda,\chi}
is proved similarly. Let
n=\check{\ell}(\mathcal{F})
. Then the object
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}
together with the canonical map
\imath_{\mathcal{F}}:\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}\to\mathcal{F}\{0\}
constructed in Proposition
11.7
(
4
) can be characterized as the unique such pair
(\mathcal{E},i:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{F})
such that
\mathcal{E}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}
such that
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\mathcal{E},\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K},<n})=0
and
i
induces an isomorphism in the Verdier quotient
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}/\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K},<n}
(this follows from the construction of
\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}
). Similarly,
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
together with the canonical map
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}\to\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
can be characterized as the unique pair
(\mathcal{G},i:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi})
such that
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\mathcal{G},{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},<n})=0
and
i
induces an isomorphism in the Verdier quotient
{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}/{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}},<n}
. This proves
\widetilde{\kappa}(\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}})\cong\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}
.
∎

Remark 11.9
.

The above theorem shows that
\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a},gr}_{\check{K}}
is a graded version of
{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
. It would be interesting to define a graded version of
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
intrinsically using only the geometry of the
G_{\mathbb{R}}
-action on
\widetilde{X}
. If we use the Matsuki equivalence to identify
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
with
\widehat{D}_{K}(G/U)_{{\mathbf{T}}\textup{-mon}}
. Such a graded version may be obtained by considering mixed Hodge modules on
G/U
.

In particular, the stalks and costalks of an indecomposable tilting object in
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
should carry gradings reflecting a mysterious weight structure, canonical up to an overall shift. We refer to
[
28
]
where the weight structure on stalks of tilting sheaves in the complex group case is determined.

11.10.
Numerical consequences of Koszul duality

For a
G_{\mathbb{R}}
-orbit
O^{\mathbb{R}}_{\lambda}\subset X
, recall from Lemma
4.6
that
{\mathbf{T}}
carries a canonical real form
\sigma_{\lambda}
depending only on
\lambda
. Let
\theta_{\lambda}:{\mathbf{T}}\to{\mathbf{T}}
be the corresponding Cartan involution.

For a
\check{K}
-orbit
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}\subset\check{X}
, choosing a Borel subgroup
\check{B}\in O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}
, and let
\check{\mathbf{T}}_{\check{\lambda}}\subset\check{\mathbf{T}}
be the image of the projection
\check{K}\cap\check{B}\to\check{\mathbf{T}}
, which is independent of the choice of
\check{B}
. As in Lemma
4.6
, by choosing a
\check{\theta}
-stable maximal torus
\check{T}\subset\check{B}
,
\check{\mathbf{T}}
is identified with
\check{T}
and
\check{\mathbf{T}}_{\check{\lambda}}
is identified with
\check{T}^{\check{\theta}}
. This induces an involution
\check{\theta}_{\check{\lambda}}
on
\check{\mathbf{T}}
that depends only on
\check{\lambda}
, such that
\check{\mathbf{T}}_{\check{\lambda}}=\check{\mathbf{T}}^{\check{\theta}_{\check{\lambda}}}
.

The following is a known property of Vogan duality
[
27
]
; we give a geometric argument using Koszul duality.

Corollary 11.11
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
be a simple perverse sheaf supported on the
\check{K}
-orbit
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}}
for some
\check{\lambda}\in\check{I}
. Let
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}=\kappa(\mathcal{F})
. Then under the canonical identification

(11.7)

\mathbb{X}_{*}({\mathbf{T}})\cong\mathbb{X}^{*}(\check{\mathbf{T}}),

the involution
\theta_{\lambda}
corresponds to the involution
-\check{\theta}_{\check{\lambda}}
.

Proof.

Combining the two isomorphisms in Proposition
11.7
(2) and Theorem
11.8
(2), both applied to
\mathcal{F}_{1}=\mathcal{F}_{2}=\Delta^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}}
, and using Theorem
11.8
(
4
), we get an isomorphism of
\mathcal{R}
-modules

(11.8)

\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}(\Delta_{\mathcal{F}},\Delta_{\mathcal{F}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi},\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi}).

Note that
\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}(\Delta_{\mathcal{F}},\Delta_{\mathcal{F}})=H^{*}_{\check{\mathbf{T}}_{\check{\lambda}}}(\textup{pt},{\mathbf{k}})\cong\mathop{\textrm{Sym}}\nolimits(\mathbb{X}^{*}(\check{\mathbf{T}}_{\check{\lambda}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbf{k}}[-2])
as an
\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}
-module. On the other hand, by Lemma
2.7
,
\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi},\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda,\chi})\cong\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda}
, which is the completion of
{\mathbf{k}}[\mathbb{X}_{*}({\mathbf{T}}/{\mathbf{T}}_{\lambda})]
at the augmentation ideal. The isomorphism (
11.8
) as
\mathcal{R}
-modules imply that
\mathbb{X}^{*}(\check{\mathbf{T}}_{\check{\lambda}})_{\mathbb{Q}}=\mathbb{X}_{*}({\mathbf{T}}/{\mathbf{T}}_{\lambda})_{\mathbb{Q}}
as quotients of
\mathbb{X}^{*}(\check{\mathbf{T}})=\mathbb{X}_{*}(\check{\mathbf{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
. This implies that under (
11.7
), the
-1
eigenspace of
\check{\theta}_{\check{\lambda}}
on
\mathbb{X}^{*}(\check{\mathbf{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
coincides with the
+1
eigenspace of
\theta_{\lambda}
on
\mathbb{X}_{*}({\mathbf{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
. Since both
\theta_{\lambda}
and
\check{\theta}_{\check{\lambda}}
preserve the Killing forms, which are intertwined under (
11.7
), we conclude that
-\check{\theta}_{\check{\lambda}}
is intertwined with
\theta_{\lambda}
under (
11.7
).
∎

Recall the Lusztig-Vogan polynomials for the stalks of
IC
sheaves on
\check{K}\backslash\check{X}
, see
[
18
, Theorems 1.11, 1.12]
. For simple perverse sheaves
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\check{K}}
, let

P_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}}(q)=\sum_{i\geq 0}\dim_{{\mathbf{k}}}q^{i}\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\mathcal{F},\nabla_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}[2i]).

Corollary 11.12
.

For an indecomposable free-monodromic tilting sheaf
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
in
{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
, denote by
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\chi}\in\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}}
the simple perverse sheaf that corresponds to
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
under the bijection
|\kappa|
in Theorem
11.3
(
4
). Then for any
\lambda^{\prime}\in I
, we have an isomorphism

\widetilde{i}_{\lambda^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}\cong\bigoplus_{(\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})\in\widetilde{I}^{a}}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}}^{\oplus P_{\mathcal{F}_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\chi}}(1)}[p_{\lambda^{\prime}}].

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}=\mathcal{F}_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}}
. Let
m_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}}
be the multiplicity of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}}[p_{\lambda^{\prime}}]
in
\widetilde{i}_{\lambda^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi}
. Combining the two isomorphisms in Proposition
11.7
(2) and Theorem
11.8
(2), both applied to
\mathcal{F}_{1}=\mathcal{F}\{0\}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}=\nabla^{gr}_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}
, and using Theorem
11.8
(
4
), we get an isomorphism of
\mathcal{R}
-modules

\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F},\nabla_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\chi},\widetilde{\nabla}_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}}).

We know the right side is a direct sum of
m_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}}
copies of
\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}},\widetilde{\nabla}_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}})
, which is a free
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
-module of rank
m_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}}
. The left side is a direct sum of
P_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}}(1)
copies of
\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}(\Delta_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}},\nabla_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}
(using that the stalks of
\mathcal{F}
along
O^{\check{K}}_{\check{\lambda}^{\prime}}
are concentrated in degrees of the same parity), which is a free
H^{*}_{\check{\mathbf{T}}_{\check{\lambda}^{\prime}}}(\textup{pt},{\mathbf{k}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}
-module of rank
P_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}}(1)
(where
\check{\lambda}^{\prime}
indexes the
\check{K}
-orbit whose closure is the support of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
). Comparing the dimension of the reductions to
{\mathbf{k}}
-modules on both sides, we conclude that
m_{\lambda^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}}=P_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}}(1)
.
∎

11.13.
Corepresentability of the real Soergel functor

Recall from Proposition
8.7
that the big tilting object
\mathcal{T}_{w_{0}}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{G}
corepresents the classical Soergel functor
\mathbb{V}
. The next statement is its analogue for quasi-split real groups.

Let
G_{\mathbb{R}}
be quasi-split and nice. For a
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
-orbit
a\subset\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
, let
\mathcal{B}^{a}=(\prod_{\chi\in a}{\mathcal{S}})^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}
be the direct factor of
\mathcal{B}
corresponding to the block
a
.

Theorem 11.14
.

Assume
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split and nice.

(1)

Let
\chi\in\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
and let
a
be the
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
-orbit of
\chi
. Then
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}\star\mathcal{T}_{w_{0}}
contains a direct summand
\mathcal{T}^{a}
satisfying
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{T}^{a})\cong\mathcal{B}^{a}
as
\mathcal{B}
-modules. The isomorphism class of
\mathcal{T}^{a}
only depends on the block
a
.

(2)

We have an
\mathcal{R}
-algebra isomorphism
\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits(\mathcal{T}^{a})\cong\mathcal{B}^{a}
.

(3)

For any choice of a regular covector
\xi\in i\mathcal{N}^{*}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{O}_{{\text{\rm reg}}}
and its lifting
\widetilde{\xi}
, there is an isomorphism of functors

\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\widetilde{\xi}}\cong\operatorname{RHom}(\oplus_{a}\mathcal{T}^{a},-):{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}\to D^{b}(\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R})

where the sum is over
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
-orbits
a\subset\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
.
In particular, the functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\widetilde{\xi}}
does not depend on the choice of
\widetilde{\xi}
, up to isomorphism.

(4)

Under the functor
\kappa
in Theorem
11.3
,
\mathcal{T}^{a}
is the image of the constant perverse sheaf
{\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}[\dim\check{X}]
.

Proof.

We fix
\widetilde{\xi}
and denote
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R},\widetilde{\xi}}
by
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

(1) Under
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}
,
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}\star\mathcal{T}_{w_{0}}
becomes the
\mathcal{B}^{a}
-module
{\mathcal{S}}_{\chi}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R})\cong{\mathcal{S}}_{\chi}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}
. Note that
\mathcal{B}^{a}={\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}
, where
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}
is the stabilizer of
\chi
under the cross action of
{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0}}
on
\textup{LS}_{\lambda_{0}}
. Since
{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}}
is a summand of
{\mathcal{S}}
as a
{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbf{W}}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}}
-module,
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{T}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}\star\mathcal{T}_{w_{0}})\cong{\mathcal{S}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathbf{W}}}}\mathcal{R}
contains a free rank one
\mathcal{B}^{a}
-module as a direct summand. Since
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}
is fully faithful by Corollary
9.22
, we conclude that
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda_{0},\chi}\star\mathcal{T}_{w_{0}}
contains a summand
\mathcal{T}^{a}
with
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{T}^{a})\cong\mathcal{B}^{a}
as
\mathcal{B}
-modules.

For another
\chi^{\prime}\in a
, the same argument would give another
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
with the same property
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{T}^{\prime})\cong\mathcal{B}^{a}
. Since
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}
is fully faithful, we conclude that
\mathcal{T}^{a}\cong\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
.

(2) By Corollary
9.22
,
\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits(\mathcal{T}^{a})\cong\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{T}^{a}))\cong\mathop{\sf End}\nolimits_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{B}^{a})=\mathcal{B}^{a}
.

(3) Choose a free generator
v_{a}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{T}^{a})
as a right
\mathcal{B}^{a}
-module. Then we get a map functorial in
\mathcal{F}\in{\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}

\alpha:\operatorname{RHom}(\oplus_{a}\mathcal{T}^{a},\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(-)}\operatorname{RHom}_{\mathcal{R}}(\oplus_{a}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{T}^{a}),\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F}))\xrightarrow{\oplus\textup{ev}_{v_{a}}}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F})

where the last map is evaluation at
v_{a}
. This defines a map between two triangulated functors
{\mathcal{M}}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}\to D^{b}(\textup{mod-}\mathcal{R})
. To show it is an isomorphism, it suffices to check on generators. We show that
\alpha
is an isomorphism for
\mathcal{F}\in\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}_{G_{{\mathbb{R}}}})
. Indeed, in this case, both sides are concentrated in degree
0
. Using Corollary
9.22
,
\alpha
becomes

\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits(\oplus\mathcal{T}^{a},\mathcal{F})\cong\mathop{\sf Hom}\nolimits_{\mathcal{B}}(\oplus\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{T}^{a}),\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F}))\xrightarrow{\textup{ev}_{\sum v_{a}}}\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F})=\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F})

where
\textup{ev}_{\sum v_{a}}
is an isomorphism because
\sum v_{a}
is a free generator of
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}(\oplus_{a}\mathcal{T}^{a})
as a
\mathcal{B}
-module. This proves (2).

(4) Let
C={\underline{{\mathbf{k}}}}[\dim\check{X}]
. By construction, the global sections functor
\mathbb{H}^{\check{a}}
on
\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
is intertwined with
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}
on
\textup{Tilt}({\mathcal{M}}^{a}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}})
in the sense that if
\mathcal{F}\in\textup{SSC}(\check{\mathcal{D}}^{\check{a}}_{\check{K}})
, then

\mathbb{H}^{\check{a}}(\mathcal{F})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}=\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}(C,\mathcal{F})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}\cong\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}(\kappa(\mathcal{F}))

as
\mathcal{B}^{a}
-modules, where we use the isomorphism
\check{\mathcal{B}}^{\check{a},\bullet}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}=H^{*}(\check{K}\backslash\check{X},\mathcal{F})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}\cong\mathcal{B}^{a}
proved in
[
9
, Corollary 2.6]
. From this we see that
\mathbb{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathbb{R}}(\kappa(C))\cong\mathbb{H}^{\check{a}}(C)\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}=\check{\mathcal{B}}^{\check{a},\bullet}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}\mathcal{R}\cong\mathcal{B}^{a}
. Repeating the argument in (3) we see that
\kappa(C)
also corepresents the functor
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
, hence
\kappa(C)\cong\mathcal{T}^{a}
.

∎
